The girth of the great toe is declared to be four aṅgulas; of the next one, three aṅgulas; and the rest, in order, are less by half a barley-corn each.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
Beginning from the ankle, four and a half aṅgulas. The ancients, however, explained that the fore-foot (prapada) should be four and a half aṅgulas. She whose initial portion of the fore-foot is six aṅgulas high; whose middle of the fore-foot is three and a half aṅgulas. (6) "At the end" — at the tip of the fore-foot, an elevation of two aṅgulas. "Tryaṅgula-vyaṅgulām": she whose two digits — the great toe and the toe immediately following it — measure three aṅgulas each. "With the middle toe less by a half": the measure less a half, that is, two and a half aṅgulas — her middle toe is of that measure. (7) "Less by three quarters of an aṅgula": an aṅgula diminished by three quarters — the sense being two digits and a quarter (2¼ aṅgulas); she whose "other" toe, the anāmikā (fourth), is of that measure. "Then, following it, one of like kind": after the anāmikā, one like it but less by a quarter — she whose next toe, the little toe, is such; that is, the little toe is two aṅgulas long. "With the great-toe's nail a quarter less (an aṅgula) high": her great toe's nail is raised one aṅgula less a quarter. "The next, half of that": the one following is one and a half quarters high. The purport is this: the great toe's nail has a height of three quarters; the nail of the toe next to it, a height of one and a half quarters; for the one after that, when that one-and-a-half-quarter measure is divided into four parts, its height is three of those parts; and for the two after that, likewise. (8) He makes that very point clear: "kramaśaḥ" — in order, each diminished by a quarter. She whose toe-nails are red, crimson. He then states the measure of the limbs not yet mentioned: "vistāraḥ." Of the prapada — the fore-part of the foot — the breadth measures six aṅgulas. (9) The girth of the great toe is four aṅgulas. Of the one other than it — other than the great toe — the girth, its measured breadth, is three aṅgulas. The remaining toes, in order, are "less by half a vrīhi" — each having a girth of three aṅgulas diminished (successively) by half a barley-grain. (10)
Chapter 3 · verse 10
3.10
दशाङ्गुलं जङ्घमूलं मध्यं च त्र्यङ्गुलाधिकम्।
सप्तादशाङ्गुलं तूर्ध्वं जानुरष्टादशाङ्गुलः॥ १०॥
daśāṅgulaṃ jaṅghamūlaṃ madhyaṃ ca tryaṅgulādhikam|
saptādaśāṅgulaṃ tūrdhvaṃ jānuraṣṭādaśāṅgulaḥ||
10||
Exact English meaningThe base of the shank ten aṅgulas (in girth); at its middle three aṅgulas more; and above, seventeen aṅgulas; the knee eighteen aṅgulas.
Word and compound explanation
"The base of the shank, ten aṅgulas": the base of the two shanks has a girth of ten aṅgulas. The middle of the shanks has a girth of ten aṅgulas plus three. Above — the top end of the shank — has a girth of seventeen aṅgulas. The knee-region is eighteen aṅgulas. (11)
Chapter 3 · verse 11
3.11
ऊर्वन्तोऽपि तथा ज्ञेयो मध्यं द्विर्द्वादशाङ्गुलम्।
चतुरङ्गुलाधिकं मूलं गुह्यं सार्धचतुष्टयम्॥ ११॥
ūrvanto’pi tathā jñeyo madhyaṃ dvirdvādaśāṅgulam|
caturaṅgulādhikaṃ mūlaṃ guhyaṃ sārdhacatuṣṭayam||
11||
Exact English meaningThe end of the thigh too is to be known as the same (eighteen); its middle twenty-four aṅgulas; its base four aṅgulas more (twenty-eight); the private part four and a half in length.
Word and compound explanation
"The end of the thigh" — the part above the knee too has (the same, i.e.) a girth of eighteen aṅgulas. The middle of the thigh is "twice twelve," a girth of twenty-four aṅgulas. The base of the thigh is four aṅgulas more, a girth of twenty-eight aṅgulas. The private part — the phallus — is four and a half (aṅgulas) long. (12)
Chapter 3 · verse 12
3.12
सार्धत्रयं परीणाहाद् वृषणं चतुरङ्गुलम्।
सप्ताङ्गुलं परीणाहाद् वृत्तं प्रोक्तं समस्तशः॥ १२॥
sārdhatrayaṃ parīṇāhād vṛṣaṇaṃ caturaṅgulam|
saptāṅgulaṃ parīṇāhād vṛttaṃ proktaṃ samastaśaḥ||
12||
Exact English meaningthree and a half in girth; the scrotum four aṅgulas (long), seven aṅgulas in girth, declared to be rounded on all sides.
Word and compound explanation
Its girth is three and a half aṅgulas. The scrotum is four and a half aṅgulas long; its girth is seven aṅgulas. (13)
Chapter 3 · verse 13
3.13
अष्टत्रिंशाङ्गुलः कट्याः परिणाह उदाहृतः।
सुवृत्तत्वं तथा श्रोण्योः पीनत्वं च समस्तशः॥ १३॥
aṣṭatriṃśāṅgulaḥ kaṭyāḥ pariṇāha udāhṛtaḥ|
suvṛttatvaṃ tathā śroṇyoḥ pīnatvaṃ ca samastaśaḥ||
13||
Exact English meaningThe girth of the waist is declared to be thirty-eight aṅgulas; likewise the well-roundedness of the two buttocks and their plumpness, evenly.
Word and compound explanation
The girth of the waist is thirty-eight aṅgulas. Of the buttocks, plumpness and well-roundedness; "samatvataḥ" — with evenness. (14)
Chapter 3 · verse 14
3.14
विस्तारश्च तथा कट्याः सुप्रतिष्ठितता पदोः।
कट्याश्चैवाङ्गुलाधिक्यं नाभेरध उदाहृतः॥ १४॥
vistāraśca tathā kaṭyāḥ supratiṣṭhitatā padoḥ|
kaṭyāścaivāṅgulādhikyaṃ nābheradha udāhṛtaḥ||
14||
Exact English meaningand likewise the breadth of the waist, and the firm well-set condition of the feet; and it is declared that the waist has one aṅgula more below the navel.
Word and compound explanation
The breadth of the waist, and the firm well-set condition of the feet, are to be provided — supply "are to be made." "Below the navel" — the waist has, over the waist's girth, an excess of one aṅgula — supply "of girth." (15)
Chapter 3 · verses 15–17
3.15
नाभिः सार्धाङ्गुलश्चैव गम्भीरोऽर्धाङ्गुलोन्नतः।
वृत्तः प्रदक्षिणश्चैव द्विचत्वारिंशदङ्गुलम्।
मध्यं स्तने परीणाहः षडङ्गुलमतोऽधिकः॥ १५॥
nābhiḥ sārdhāṅgulaścaiva gambhīro’rdhāṅgulonnataḥ|
vṛttaḥ pradakṣiṇaścaiva dvicatvāriṃśadaṅgulam|
madhyaṃ stane parīṇāhaḥ ṣaḍaṅgulamato’dhikaḥ||
15||
3.16
एकोनविंशाङ्गुलं तदुरोविस्तारलक्षणम्।
पादोनमङ्गुलं चैव भुजाभ्यां सह सार्धकम्॥ १६॥
ekonaviṃśāṅgulaṃ tadurovistāralakṣaṇam|
pādonamaṅgulaṃ caiva bhujābhyāṃ saha sārdhakam||
16||
3.17
अंसयोर्विस्तृतिश्चैव पृथगष्टाङ्गुला मता।
सप्ताङ्गुलोच्छ्रयः कक्षादुपरि स्कन्धयोर्मतः॥ १७॥
aṃsayorvistṛtiścaiva pṛthagaṣṭāṅgulā matā|
saptāṅgulocchrayaḥ kakṣādupari skandhayormataḥ||
17||
Exact combined meaningThe navel is one and a half aṅgulas, half an aṅgula deep, round, and turning to the right; the girth at the middle is forty-two aṅgulas. At the breasts the girth is six aṅgulas greater. The breadth of the chest is nineteen aṅgulas; the next measure is a quarter-aṅgula less, and together with the arms one and a half more. The breadth of the two shoulders is eight aṅgulas each, and their height above the armpit is held to be seven aṅgulas.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Chapter 3 · verse 18
3.18
अष्टत्रिंशाङ्गुलं चैव हस्तयोर्मानमुच्यते।
अष्टादशाङ्गुलो बाह्वोः परिणाह उदाहृतः।
क्रमादूनं तदन्ते तु सार्धाष्टाङ्गुलमीरितम्॥ १८॥
aṣṭatriṃśāṅgulaṃ caiva hastayormānamucyate|
aṣṭādaśāṅgulo bāhvoḥ pariṇāha udāhṛtaḥ|
kramādūnaṃ tadante tu sārdhāṣṭāṅgulamīritam||
18||
Exact English meaningThe measure of the two hands is declared to be thirty-eight aṅgulas. The girth of the upper arms is declared to be eighteen aṅgulas; diminishing progressively, at their ends it is stated to be eight and a half aṅgulas.
Chapter 3 · verse 19
3.19
साङ्गुलस्तु तलः सार्धनवाङ्गुल उदाहृतः।
समं तदुभयं ज्ञेयं तथैव तलविस्तृतिः॥ १९॥
sāṅgulastu talaḥ sārdhanavāṅgula udāhṛtaḥ|
samaṃ tadubhayaṃ jñeyaṃ tathaiva talavistṛtiḥ||
19||
Exact English meaningThe palm together with the fingers is declared to be nine and a half aṅgulas; both (length and breadth) are to be known as equal, and likewise the breadth of the palm.
Word and compound explanation
"Together with the fingers" — the palm with the fingers, the palm-surface, is nine and a half aṅgulas. Both of these — the fingers and the palm — are to be known as equal. The breadth of the palm too is to be known as the same. The purport is that the three — the breadth of the palm, the length of the palm, and the length of the fingers — are each equal, being four aṅgulas and three quarters. (21)
Chapter 3 · verse 20
3.20
अर्धाङ्गुलोन्नता मध्या द्वयोः सार्धाङ्गुलोन्नता।
कनिष्ठिकायास्तस्यास्तु व्रीहिमात्राधिकः परः॥ २०॥
ardhāṅgulonnatā madhyā dvayoḥ sārdhāṅgulonnatā|
kaniṣṭhikāyāstasyāstu vrīhimātrādhikaḥ paraḥ||
20||
Exact English meaningThe middle (finger) is half an aṅgula high; of the two (adjoining) the height is one and a half aṅgulas; of that little finger, however, the next is more by a barley-corn.
Word and compound explanation
The middle finger, compared (with the palm), is half an aṅgula in height. Compared with that little finger, the next — the thumb — is more by a barley-corn: more by the measure of a barley-corn. (22)
Chapter 3 · verses 21–25
3.21
सार्धत्रयपरीणाहौ मध्यज्येष्ठावुदाहृतौ।
अन्यौ व्रीहितदर्धोनावन्या सार्धद्वयाङ्गुला।
पादोनान्तपरीणाहाः सर्वा ज्येष्ठां विना स्मृताः॥ २१॥
sārdhatrayaparīṇāhau madhyajyeṣṭhāvudāhṛtau|
anyau vrīhitadardhonāvanyā sārdhadvayāṅgulā|
pādonāntaparīṇāhāḥ sarvā jyeṣṭhāṃ vinā smṛtāḥ||
21||
3.22
वक्षोविस्तारसदृशः परिणाहो गलस्य च।
ललाटकुक्षिकण्ठास्तु दीर्घरेखात्रयान्विताः॥ २२॥
vakṣovistārasadṛśaḥ pariṇāho galasya ca|
lalāṭakukṣikaṇṭhāstu dīrgharekhātrayānvitāḥ||
22||
3.23
वृत्तो गलो बाहवश्च स्तनावङ्गुलयस्तथा।
रक्तास्तलनखाश्चैव नेत्रान्तोऽधर एव च॥ २३॥
vṛtto galo bāhavaśca stanāvaṅgulayastathā|
raktāstalanakhāścaiva netrānto’dhara eva ca||
23||
3.24
उत्तरोष्ठश्च जिह्वा च नखौ पादोनकाङ्गुलौ।
अर्धाङ्गुलौ तथा मध्यावन्येऽष्टांशक्रमोनकाः॥ २४॥
uttaroṣṭhaśca jihvā ca nakhau pādonakāṅgulau|
ardhāṅgulau tathā madhyāvanye’ṣṭāṃśakramonakāḥ||
24||
3.25
नासिकाया अधस्ताच्च नासिका च ललाटिका।
समा मानेन नासाया उच्चत्वेनैव मापने।
अर्धाङ्गुलाधिकं सर्वं मुखस्य तु भविष्यति॥ २५॥
nāsikāyā adhastācca nāsikā ca lalāṭikā|
samā mānena nāsāyā uccatvenaiva māpane|
ardhāṅgulādhikaṃ sarvaṃ mukhasya tu bhaviṣyati||
25||
Exact combined meaningThe middle and the greatest (thumb) are declared to have a girth of three and a half; the other two are less by a barley-corn and half of that; and the other (the little finger) is two and a half aṅgulas. All (the fingers), save the greatest, are held to have a girth diminishing by a quarter (from the middle finger down); and the girth of the neck is equal to the breadth of the chest. The forehead, the belly and the throat are furnished with three long lines; the throat is round, and so are the arms, the breasts and the fingers. Red are the palm and the nails, and the corner of the eye and the lower lip, and the upper lip and the tongue; the (two great) nails are a quarter-aṅgula less than an aṅgula. the two middle ones (nails) half an aṅgula; the others diminishing in order by an eighth-part. And below the nose, the nose and the rarāṭikā (philtrum) are equal in measure; and in the measuring, by the height of the nose, everything of the face will come to half an aṅgula more.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
The middle and the greatest — the middle finger and the thumb — have a girth of three and a half aṅgulas. The other two — those before and after the middle finger — have a girth less by one and a half barley-corns. Compared with the middle finger, the other, the little finger, has a girth of two and a half aṅgulas. (23) All the fingers, except the greatest — the thumb — have girths diminishing by a quarter. In order (down) to the forefinger: the middle finger has a girth diminishing by a quarter, then the ring-finger, then the little finger — such is the meaning. The girth of the gala — the throat — is equal to the breadth of the chest; the sense is: nineteen aṅgulas less a quarter. (24) The forehead, belly and throat are furnished with three long lines. The throat, the arms, the two breasts and the fingers are to be known as round — circular. (25) The palm-surface, the nails, the corner of the eye, the lower lip, the upper lip and the tongue are "rakta" — reddish. The two nails — the great-toe/thumb nails — are one aṅgula less a quarter in length. (26) "The two middle ones" — the nails of the forefinger and the middle finger — are half an aṅgula long. The other finger-nails diminish in order by an eighth-part: less by an eighth successively — compared with the middle-finger's nail, the ring-finger's nail is less by an eighth, and then the little-finger's nail. Below the nose down to the chin — the nose and the rarāṭikā (the furrow of the upper lip) and the forehead — these are of equal measure. When the measuring is done from the chin to the tip of the nose and up to the top of the forehead, by the height at the nose, the whole face comes to half an aṅgula in height — such is the meaning. (27–28)
Chapter 3 · verses 26–32
3.26
चतुरङ्गुलं तथैवास्यं सार्धव्रीह्यधरं स्मृतम्।
उत्तरं व्रीहिमात्रं तु तदूर्ध्वं चाधरोपमम्।
सार्धाङ्गुलमथ प्रोक्तमधरादध एव च॥ २६॥
caturaṅgulaṃ tathaivāsyaṃ sārdhavrīhyadharaṃ smṛtam|
uttaraṃ vrīhimātraṃ tu tadūrdhvaṃ cādharopamam|
sārdhāṅgulamatha proktamadharādadha eva ca||
26||
3.27
आस्यपार्श्वद्वयं प्रोक्तमा कर्णात् तु षडङ्गुलम्।
सप्ताङ्गुलं नासिकायाः पार्श्वमा कर्णतः स्मृतम्॥ २७॥
āsyapārśvadvayaṃ proktamā karṇāt tu ṣaḍaṅgulam|
saptāṅgulaṃ nāsikāyāḥ pārśvamā karṇataḥ smṛtam||
27||
3.28
सपादाङ्गुलमुच्चा च नासिका परिकीर्तिता।
अर्धाङ्गुले पुटे तस्या मध्यं च समुदाहृतम्॥ २८॥
sapādāṅgulamuccā ca nāsikā parikīrtitā|
ardhāṅgule puṭe tasyā madhyaṃ ca samudāhṛtam||
28||
3.29
नासिका व्रीहिविस्तारा चक्षुषी चाङ्गुलत्रये।
अङ्गुलं चैव विस्तीर्णे फुल्लेऽर्धं च तदन्यथा॥ २९॥
nāsikā vrīhivistārā cakṣuṣī cāṅgulatraye|
aṅgulaṃ caiva vistīrṇe phulle’rdhaṃ ca tadanyathā||
29||
3.30
चतुरङ्गुलौ भ्रुवौ चैव तथाऽर्धाङ्गुलिविस्तृतौ।
असंहतौ च निबिडौ तथा पक्ष्म सुनीलकम्॥ ३०॥
caturaṅgulau bhruvau caiva tathā’rdhāṅgulivistṛtau|
asaṃhatau ca nibiḍau tathā pakṣma sunīlakam||
30||
3.31
कर्णौ च त्र्यङ्गुलौ सार्धद्वयविस्तारसंयुतौ।
सकुण्डलं तावदेव विवरं सम्प्रकीर्तितम्॥ ३१॥
karṇau ca tryaṅgulau sārdhadvayavistārasaṃyutau|
sakuṇḍalaṃ tāvadeva vivaraṃ samprakīrtitam||
31||
3.32
तथा द्व्यङ्गुलविस्तारं लताभ्यां सह कथ्यते।
नीलालकसहस्रेण युक्तं तन्मुखपङ्कजम्॥ ३२॥
tathā dvyaṅgulavistāraṃ latābhyāṃ saha kathyate|
nīlālakasahasreṇa yuktaṃ tanmukhapaṅkajam||
32||
Exact combined meaningThe mouth is four aṅgulas. The lower lip is one and a half barley-corns; the upper lip one barley-corn; the space above it is like the lower lip. Below the lower lip is one and a half aṅgulas. Each side of the mouth up to the ear is six aṅgulas; the side of the nose up to the ear is seven. The nose is one and a quarter aṅgulas high; each nostril-wing and its middle are half an aṅgula. The nose is a barley-corn in breadth, and the two eyes span three aṅgulas. An eye is one aṅgula wide when open and half that when otherwise. The brows are four aṅgulas long and half an aṅgula broad, separate yet dense; the eyelashes are deep blue-black. The ears are three aṅgulas long and two and a half broad; with the earrings, the aperture is declared to be the same, and the ear-tendrils make a breadth of two aṅgulas. The lotus-face is adorned with a thousand dark curls.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Chapter 3 · verse 33
3.33
ललाटं सुविशालं च तथा सार्धनवाङ्गुलः।
विस्तारो मूर्ध्नि वृत्तं च शिरश्छत्राकृति स्मृतम्॥ ३३॥
lalāṭaṃ suviśālaṃ ca tathā sārdhanavāṅgulaḥ|
vistāro mūrdhni vṛttaṃ ca śiraśchatrākṛti smṛtam||
33||
Exact English meaningThe forehead is very broad, and likewise nine and a half aṅgulas; the breadth at the crown is round, and the head is held to be umbrella-shaped.
Word and compound explanation
The forehead is very broad; and the head is nine and a half aṅgulas in breadth — beautiful, broad, round like an umbrella. Or else it may be construed: at the crown, the breadth is by (a measure of) nine and a half aṅgulas. (37)
Chapter 3 · verse 34
3.34
दीर्घाश्च कुञ्चिताग्राश्च नीलाः केशा हरेर्मताः।
मुखमानेन चैवोच्चं किरीटं केशवस्य हि॥ ३४॥
dīrghāśca kuñcitāgrāśca nīlāḥ keśā harermatāḥ|
mukhamānena caivoccaṃ kirīṭaṃ keśavasya hi||
34||
Exact English meaningLong, curling at the tips, and dark are Hari's locks held to be; and Keśava's diadem is high by the measure of the face.
Word and compound explanation
Śrī Hari's locks are dark — black — and curling at the tips. By the measure of the face — of nine aṅgulas — Keśava's diadem, the crown, is to be known. (38)
Chapter 3 · verses 35–36
3.35
कुण्डले मकराकारे वक्षश्चैव सकौस्तुभम्।
सश्रीवत्सं दक्षिणतः पीनं तुङ्गमुदाहृतम्।
हारग्रैवेयसहितमुपवीतयुतं तथा॥ ३५॥
kuṇḍale makarākāre vakṣaścaiva sakaustubham|
saśrīvatsaṃ dakṣiṇataḥ pīnaṃ tuṅgamudāhṛtam|
hāragraiveyasahitamupavītayutaṃ tathā||
35||
3.36
बाहवश्च सकेयूरकङ्कणाङ्गदमुद्रिकाः।
समध्यबन्धं मध्यं च नितम्बे पीतमम्बरम्।
काञ्चीगुणश्च पदयोर्नूपुरे चातिसुस्वने॥ ३६॥
bāhavaśca sakeyūrakaṅkaṇāṅgadamudrikāḥ|
samadhyabandhaṃ madhyaṃ ca nitambe pītamambaram|
kāñcīguṇaśca padayornūpure cātisusvane||
36||
Exact combined meaningThe two earrings are of makara shape; and the chest bears the Kaustubha, and, on the right, the Śrīvatsa; it is declared to be plump and high. furnished with necklace and neck-ornament, and likewise with the sacred thread; and the arms with armlets, bracelets, upper-armlets and rings. the waist with a girdle-band, and on the hips the yellow garment; and the girdle-cord, and on the feet the sweetly-sounding anklets.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
The two earrings are such as have the shape of a makara. And the chest is furnished with the Kaustubha; on the right, plumpness — plump and high — furnished with the mark called Śrīvatsa. (39) With necklaces of pearls; and with neck-ornaments (graiveya), that is, throat-ornaments; and to be known as furnished with the sacred thread. And the arms are to be known as furnished with armlets (keyūra), upper-armlets (aṅgada), bracelets (kaṅkaṇa) and rings (mudrikā). Keyūra and aṅgada are mentioned separately because of the subtle difference between them. (40) Furnished with a waist-band, a hip-band. "Madhya" — the waist-region. On the hips, the yellow garment, the cloth. And the girdle-cord; and on the feet, the sweet-sounding anklets. (41)
Chapter 3 · verse 37
3.37
अङ्गुलीयानि च पदोः कृतिभिः साधु कारयेत्।
सोत्तरीयं च चक्राद्यैरायुधैश्च समन्वितम्॥ ३७॥
aṅgulīyāni ca padoḥ kṛtibhiḥ sādhu kārayet|
sottarīyaṃ ca cakrādyairāyudhaiśca samanvitam||
37||
Exact English meaningand on the feet toe-rings; one should have (all this) well made by skilled craftsmen; (the image) with an upper garment, and furnished with the discus and the other weapons.
Word and compound explanation
And the toe-rings on the feet: one should have them well made by the skillful — by expert craftsmen. "Sottarīya" — having an upper garment; furnished with the discus and the other weapons. (42)
Chapter 3 · verse 38
3.38
अनुन्नतैरविरलैर्दन्तैर्युक्तं च सुस्मितम्।
समदृष्टियुतं कार्यं श्लक्ष्णं चैव मनोहरम्॥ ३८॥
anunnatairaviralairdantairyuktaṃ ca susmitam|
samadṛṣṭiyutaṃ kāryaṃ ślakṣṇaṃ caiva manoharam||
38||
Exact English meaningfurnished with teeth neither prominent nor gapped, and fair-smiling; even of gaze, great-bodied, smooth and enchanting.
Word and compound explanation
Furnished with teeth neither prominent nor set apart; fair-smiling; he whose two eyes, of gaze, are even; whose body is great and well-proportioned; smooth, glossy, capturing the mind by the very sight of its loveliness — one should have (it made) by skilled craftsmen: the connection is with the foregoing. But it is to be understood that the teachers stated this especially for the purpose of meditation. (43)

शिलापरीक्षा-प्रतिमानयनादि

Chapter 3 · verse 39
3.39
प्रतिमार्थं व्रजन् पञ्चध्वनिभिर्मङ्गलैर्युतः।
गत्वा शुचि स्थलं चैव शिलां तत्र परीक्षयेत्॥ ३९॥
pratimārthaṃ vrajan pañcadhvanibhirmaṅgalairyutaḥ|
gatvā śuci sthalaṃ caiva śilāṃ tatra parīkṣayet||
39||
Exact English meaningGoing forth for the sake of the image, accompanied by the five auspicious sounds, having reached a clean place, one should there examine the stone.
Word and compound explanation
Having thus stated the characteristics of the image, he now states the examination of the place and the rest: "pratimārtham." One wishing to make an image, with the playing of the five (kinds of) auspicious sound, on a holy day, having gone to a clean place, should there examine the stone. (44)
Chapter 3 · verses 40–53
3.40
ध्वनिभेदेन विज्ञाय शिलां गर्भवतीं त्यजेत्।
मूलमन्त्रं जपन् विष्णुं ध्यायंस्तिष्ठेदुपोषितः।
वार्क्षी चेद् यज्ञवृक्षस्य सारेणैव तु कारयेत्॥ ४०॥
dhvanibhedena vijñāya śilāṃ garbhavatīṃ tyajet|
mūlamantraṃ japan viṣṇuṃ dhyāyaṃstiṣṭhedupoṣitaḥ|
vārkṣī ced yajñavṛkṣasya sāreṇaiva tu kārayet||
40||
3.41
बलिदानं च सङ्क्षेपविधानेनैव कथ्यते।
अस्य सङ्क्षेपशास्त्रत्वान्न विस्तरविरोधिता॥ ४१॥
balidānaṃ ca saṅkṣepavidhānenaiva kathyate|
asya saṅkṣepaśāstratvānna vistaravirodhitā||
41||
3.42
दत्वोपहारं हरये तद्भूतेभ्यो बलिं हरेत्।
प्राच्येभ्यो विष्णुभूतेभ्य इत्याद्यखिलदिक्षु च॥ ४२॥
datvopahāraṃ haraye tadbhūtebhyo baliṃ haret|
prācyebhyo viṣṇubhūtebhya ityādyakhiladikṣu ca||
42||
3.43
आदाय विष्णुभूतेभ्यो लोकपेभ्यस्तथा हरेः।
अनुज्ञामुद्धरेद् वृक्षं शिलां च प्रतिमाकृते॥ ४३॥
ādāya viṣṇubhūtebhyo lokapebhyastathā hareḥ|
anujñāmuddhared vṛkṣaṃ śilāṃ ca pratimākṛte||
43||
3.44
प्रतिमां पिण्डिकां पीठं शिलाभिस्तु त्रिजातिभिः।
स्वरस्थूलत्वभेदेन पुंस्त्र्याद्या जातितः शिलाः॥ ४४॥
pratimāṃ piṇḍikāṃ pīṭhaṃ śilābhistu trijātibhiḥ|
svarasthūlatvabhedena puṃstryādyā jātitaḥ śilāḥ||
44||
3.45
पुंशिला प्रतिमार्थं तु स्त्रीशिला पीठकॢत्पये।
वेदमङ्गलघोषेण वाद्यैस्तत्प्रतिमां हरेत्॥ ४५॥
puṃśilā pratimārthaṃ tu strīśilā pīṭhakaॢtpaye|
vedamaṅgalaghoṣeṇa vādyaistatpratimāṃ haret||
45||
3.46
प्रागुदक्प्रवणे देशे कुर्याद् देवालयं सुधीः।
रामावृते दक्षिणतः पश्चाद् गिर्यग्र एव वा॥ ४६॥
prāgudakpravaṇe deśe kuryād devālayaṃ sudhīḥ|
rāmāvṛte dakṣiṇataḥ paścād giryagra eva vā||
46||
3.47
मध्ये वा ग्रामपुरयोर्विशाले सुशुचौ तथा।
ऐशानीं दिशमेकां तु विनोदकविवर्जिते॥ ४७॥
madhye vā grāmapurayorviśāle suśucau tathā|
aiśānīṃ diśamekāṃ tu vinodakavivarjite||
47||
3.48
जलाशयस्य मध्ये वा शिलावृक्षविवर्जिते।
स्मशानादिकदेशानां विदूरे सुमनोहरे॥ ४८॥
jalāśayasya madhye vā śilāvṛkṣavivarjite|
smaśānādikadeśānāṃ vidūre sumanohare||
48||
3.49
किष्कुद्वादशतोऽनूनं कुर्याद् देवालयस्थलम्।
द्विगुणं चतुर्गुणं वाऽपि शतकिष्कुप्रमाणकम्॥ ४९॥
kiṣkudvādaśato’nūnaṃ kuryād devālayasthalam|
dviguṇaṃ caturguṇaṃ vā’pi śatakiṣkupramāṇakam||
49||
3.50
कृत्वा भूशोधनं सम्यक् सार्धपुम्मानतस्त्वधः।
केशाङ्गारास्थिवल्मीकलोष्टाश्मादिविवर्जिते॥ ५०॥
kṛtvā bhūśodhanaṃ samyak sārdhapummānatastvadhaḥ|
keśāṅgārāsthivalmīkaloṣṭāśmādivivarjite||
50||
3.51
हरिं तत्रापि सम्पूज्य बलिं दत्वाऽपि पूर्ववत्।
मृद्दारुशैलैर्लोहैर्वा कुर्याद् देवालयं दृढम्॥ ५१॥
hariṃ tatrāpi sampūjya baliṃ datvā’pi pūrvavat|
mṛddāruśailairlohairvā kuryād devālayaṃ dṛḍham||
51||
3.52
दीर्घस्थकलमास्तिस्रस्तिर्यगष्टयवोदराः।
अङ्गुल्या मध्यरेखायाः समा लक्षणतः स्मृताः॥ ५२॥
dīrghasthakalamāstisrastiryagaṣṭayavodarāḥ|
aṅgulyā madhyarekhāyāḥ samā lakṣaṇataḥ smṛtāḥ||
52||
3.53
स्वाङ्गुल्या मध्यरेखा तु प्रतिमादिषु लक्षणम्।
ह्रस्वमध्योच्चभेदेन तत्तन्मानं समस्तशः।
प्रादेशहस्तपुरुषमानं सामान्यलक्षणम्॥ ५३॥
svāṅgulyā madhyarekhā tu pratimādiṣu lakṣaṇam|
hrasvamadhyoccabhedena tattanmānaṃ samastaśaḥ|
prādeśahastapuruṣamānaṃ sāmānyalakṣaṇam||
53||
Exact combined meaningHaving discerned, by the difference of sound, a stone that contains a hollow-core (garbha), one should reject it; repeating the mūla-mantra and meditating on Viṣṇu, one should remain fasting. If (the image be) of wood, one should have it made only with the heartwood of a sacrificial tree; and the offering of bali is stated only by way of the abridged procedure. and because this (text) is a treatise of abridgment, there is opposition to elaboration. Having given the offering to Hari, one should offer bali to His messengers, 'to the Viṣṇu-beings of the east,' and so on, in all the quarters; and having taken permission from the Viṣṇu-beings and from the world-guardians and from Hari, one should extract the tree or the stone for the making of the image; (and) the image's pedestal-plinth and the seat with stones of the three kinds. By the difference in the coarseness of their sound, stones are of the classes male, female and (neuter); the male stone for the image, the female stone for the making of the seat. With the auspicious sound of the Vedas and with instruments one should convey that image; and the wise man should build the temple on ground sloping toward the east or the north. enclosed on the south and the west, and just in front of a mountain; or in the middle of a village or a town, on broad and very clean (ground); (avoiding) the north-east quarter alone; on ground free of standing water, or in the middle of a reservoir, free of stones, trees and the like; far from cremation-grounds and such places, on a very pleasing (site) — one should make the temple-ground not less than twelve kiṣkus (in extent). double, or even fourfold, or of the measure of a hundred kiṣkus; having thoroughly purified the ground down to one and a half times a man's height, on (ground) free of hair, charcoal, bone, ant-hills, clods, stones and the like — there too having worshipped Hari and given bali as before, With clay, wood, stone or metal one should make the temple firm. And the three long kalamas, of eight barley-bellies laid crosswise, are held to be, in measure, equal to the middle line of a (thumb-)finger; and the middle line of one's own (thumb-)finger is the standard-measure for images and the rest. By the distinction of short, middling and tall, the respective measure is (fixed) in every case; the measures of a span (prādeśa), a hasta, and a man's height are the general standard.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
By the sculptors, through the difference of sound — by the particular (dull) sound — a stone containing a hollow is to be rejected. There, for the sake of taking (a fit) stone, one should remain fasting, repeating the mūla-mantra. (45) "Of wood" — made of a tree; if the image one wishes to install is of wood, then one should have it made with the heartwood of a sacrificial tree — khadira, udumbara, śamī, palāśa, and the like. And here the offering of bali is stated only in brief. (46) "But in other treatises there is elaboration too" — if so, let it be; what harm? — for this (text) is a treatise of abridgment. First, having given to Hari the upahāra, the naivedya, one should offer bali to His messengers: "to the Viṣṇu-beings of the east," ( ) "this (bali) is not Mine" — (offering) in the east. By the sequence "to the Viṣṇu-beings of the south-east, bali," and so on, having given the balis in the eight quarters, having taken their leave, and having taken the leave of the world-guardians and of Hari, one should extract the tree or the stone for the sake of the image. Supply: one should have the image's plinth (piṇḍikā) — the pedestal that serves as the image's support — made with (stones) of the three kinds, distinguished as female, male and neuter. (47–49) Since the treatise on sculpture states: the coarse-sounding is the male stone, the fine-sounding the female stone, the even-sounding the neuter — for the image the male stone is to be taken; for the seat, the female stone. (50) There, on an auspicious day, with the auspicious sound of the Vedas and with the five instruments, one should convey the image — the construction being: one should carry the finished image for the purpose of installation. He states the building of the temple: "prāgudakpravaṇe." "Sloping to the east" — inclining eastward. (51) Of what sort? "Enclosed on the south and the west" — therefore one should make the doorway to the east or the north — such is the meaning. "In front of a mountain" — upon (or before) a hill. "In the middle of a village or town, on clean, holy ground." "The north-east quarter alone excepted" — the sense is that in a town or village one should not build in the north-east. "Free of water" — where, in the four-months (rains) and so forth, water does not gather and stand; or in the middle of a reservoir; free of stones, trees and the like; a place far from cremation-grounds and other impure places. One should make the temple-ground not less than twelve kiṣkus — not less than twelve hastas; since the lexicon says "kiṣku means both a hasta and a vitasti." (52–54) "Double" — twenty-four hastas; "fourfold" — measuring forty-eight hastas; or measuring a hundred hastas — so one should make it. Having purified the ground down to one and a half times a man's height, and, at the time of commencing the temple, having worshipped Hari as before, one should give bali to His messengers. (55–56) He states the materials that make up the temple: "mṛdāruśailaiḥ" — with clay, with wood-timber, with stone. One should make the temple firm and impenetrable with clay, wood-timber, stone, or metals such as copper. He states the (definition of the) aṅgula: "dīrgha." Three kalamas — grains of rice — placed lengthwise, or eight barley-bellies (placed crosswise), are held to be, in measure, equal to the middle line of the (thumb-)finger. This does not apply in the (earlier) definition of the image (v. 3) "the shining face of nine aṅgulas": there the aṅgula-measure is not the three lengthwise kalamas nor the eight crosswise barley-bellies. Then what? He says: having fixed the aṅgula of images distinguished as short, middling and tall, one should make their measure accordingly: "svāṅgulyā." By the word "sva" ("one's own") the image is here meant, since it stands in construction with what is mentioned along with it. The middle line of the aṅgula of a short (etc.) image is the standard-measure — the indicator — for images and the rest, that is, for the feet and so on of the images. (57–58) Of the measure of a span is the short (image); of the measure of a hasta, the middling; larger than that, up to the measure of a man, the tall. (59)

अङ्गुलिपरिमाणादि

Chapter 3 · verse 54
3.54
प्रतिमाध्यर्धकं द्वारमूर्ध्वमर्धं च विस्तृतिः।
प्रतिमार्धप्रमाणेन पीठस्योच्चत्वमिष्यते॥ ५४॥
pratimādhyardhakaṃ dvāramūrdhvamardhaṃ ca vistṛtiḥ|
pratimārdhapramāṇena pīṭhasyoccatvamiṣyate||
54||
Exact English meaningThe doorway is one and a half times the image (in height), and half (of that) above in breadth; the height of the seat is desired to be by the measure of half the image.
Word and compound explanation
"One and a half times the image" — this concerns a tall image. Above, one and a half times the image; and half of that is to be made the breadth. By the measure of half the image is the height of the pedestal-seat. (60)
Chapter 3 · verses 55–57
3.55
उन्नतिः पिण्डिकायास्तु चतुरङ्गुलमानतः।
त्र्यङ्गुलं द्व्यङ्गुलं वाऽपि गृहान्तः प्रतिमासमम्।
उच्चं पर्यक् च क्रमश उच्चत्वं मध्यतः स्मृतम्॥ ५५॥
unnatiḥ piṇḍikāyāstu caturaṅgulamānataḥ|
tryaṅgulaṃ dvyaṅgulaṃ vā’pi gṛhāntaḥ pratimāsamam|
uccaṃ paryak ca kramaśa uccatvaṃ madhyataḥ smṛtam||
55||
3.56
तावदुच्चं बहिश्चैव यद्येकं शिखरं भवेत्।
अर्धोच्चमथवाऽपि स्यादधस्तात् प्रतिमोन्नतम्।
ततो द्विगुणमानं वा सार्धप्रतिममेव वा॥ ५६॥
tāvaduccaṃ bahiścaiva yadyekaṃ śikharaṃ bhavet|
ardhoccamathavā’pi syādadhastāt pratimonnatam|
tato dviguṇamānaṃ vā sārdhapratimameva vā||
56||
3.57
शिखरान्तरे तु प्रतिमामानं तद्गल उच्यते।
तत्त्रिपादं तदर्धं वा तदध्यर्धमथापि वा॥ ५७॥
śikharāntare tu pratimāmānaṃ tadgala ucyate|
tattripādaṃ tadardhaṃ vā tadadhyardhamathāpi vā||
57||
Exact combined meaningThe height of the plinth is by the measure of four aṅgulas — or three aṅgulas, or two — equal to the image within the shrine (in relation to the ground); high, and all around, and progressively (its) height (is reckoned) from the middle; and so high on the outside too, if it be a single-spired (temple); or else it may be half-high; below, the image's height; or twice that measure, or just one and a half times the image; but within the spire the image's measure is called its 'petal' (i.e. core-dimension); three quarters of that, or half, or one and a half of that.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
The height of the piṇḍikā — that which serves as the image's support — may be four aṅgulas, three aṅgulas, or two aṅgulas. From the edge of the shrine — the chief (inner) temple — above the wall, the height equals the image's measure; and likewise all around, progressively. On the outside too the height is just so much, if the temple is single-spired; but where there are many spires, this is no fixed rule. He states an alternative height, reckoned from the shrine, for a single-spired temple: "ardhoccam." On the outside, half the image's height. Below, in the inner part of the temple, the image's height; or twice that measure; or one and a half times the image's height. (61–64)
Chapter 3 · verses 58–59
3.58
वर्तुलं पद्मसदृशं हस्तिपृष्ठसमं तथा।
चतुरस्रं चाष्टकोणं विमानं परिकीर्तितम्॥ ५८॥
vartulaṃ padmasadṛśaṃ hastipṛṣṭhasamaṃ tathā|
caturasraṃ cāṣṭakoṇaṃ vimānaṃ parikīrtitam||
58||
3.59
प्रतिमाया दशगुणं विंशद्गुणमथापि वा।
वर्तुलं शिखरं त्वेकं किरीटाकृतिमद् भवेत्॥ ५९॥
pratimāyā daśaguṇaṃ viṃśadguṇamathāpi vā|
vartulaṃ śikharaṃ tvekaṃ kirīṭākṛtimad bhavet||
59||
Exact combined meaningRound, lotus-like, or shaped like an elephant's back, and four-cornered, and eight-cornered — such is the vimāna (spire-tower) declared. Ten times the image, or even twenty times; and there should be one round spire (śikhara) shaped like a diadem.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
One should make the temple round, lotus-shaped, or shaped like an elephant's back — supply "one should make." He states the measure of the vimāna: "caturasram." The vimāna is four-cornered or eight-cornered. From the image's measure, ten times or twenty times (its size). Even in the case of many spires, one chief spire should be diadem-shaped and round. (65–66)
Chapter 3 · verses 60–61
3.60
सभयाऽपि समेतं वा पृथक्सभमथाऽपि वा।
द्विसभं त्रिसभं वाऽपि सगोपुरमथाऽपि वा॥ ६०॥
sabhayā’pi sametaṃ vā pṛthaksabhamathā’pi vā|
dvisabhaṃ trisabhaṃ vā’pi sagopuramathā’pi vā||
60||
3.61
प्राकारवृत्तयुक्तं वा सप्तप्राकरमेव वा।
समवृत्तं किष्कुमात्रं बहिर्वा चतुरश्रकम्॥ ६१॥
prākāravṛttayuktaṃ vā saptaprākarameva vā|
samavṛttaṃ kiṣkumātraṃ bahirvā caturaśrakam||
61||
Exact combined meaningFurnished with an assembly-hall (sabhā), or with a separate hall; or with two halls, or three halls; or with a gateway-tower (gopura); or provided with an enclosing wall-circuit, or with seven enclosures; evenly rounded, a kiṣku in measure, or on the outside four-cornered.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"Sabhayā": that where people, standing, behold the deity is the sabhā (assembly-hall). With it ( Furnished with a hall attached to the temple; or a separate hall — a hall not attached to the temple; that which has two halls is "two-halled"; or "three-halled." "With a gopura" — provided with an outer gateway. "Prākāra-vṛtta-yukta" — furnished with an enclosing wall that is round (vṛtta, circular). The placing of the word "vṛtta" first (in the compound) is a Vedic-style usage. Or else: furnished with the "turning" (vṛtta) of the enclosing wall — its arrangement, disposition, being four-cornered and the like. Or provided with seven enclosures. He qualifies the enclosure: "samavṛttam" — even and round. A kiṣku is two hastas; (the enclosure is) of that measure. "Caturasra" — four-cornered. (67–68)
Chapter 3 · verse 62
3.62
ततः परं किष्कुमात्रं सभाया अन्तरं यदि।
ततस्तावत् तृतीयायाः सार्धमेव ह्युदाहृतम्॥ ६२॥
tataḥ paraṃ kiṣkumātraṃ sabhāyā antaraṃ yadi|
tatastāvat tṛtīyāyāḥ sārdhameva hyudāhṛtam||
62||
Exact English meaningBeyond that, if the interval of the (second) hall is a kiṣku, then that of the third is declared to be just one and a half.
Word and compound explanation
He states a particular point regarding the case of separate halls: "tataḥ." Beyond the temple, the interval of the (first) hall is a kiṣku; but of the second and the following halls the interval is declared to be one and a half kiṣkus. (69)
Chapter 3 · verse 63
3.63
किष्कुत्रयं च शक्रादेः स्थानं तद् वृत्ततः स्मृतम्।
ततस्तु मण्टपं कुर्यात् प्रतिष्ठायाः सुलक्षणम्॥ ६३॥
kiṣkutrayaṃ ca śakrādeḥ sthānaṃ tad vṛttataḥ smṛtam|
tatastu maṇṭapaṃ kuryāt pratiṣṭhāyāḥ sulakṣaṇam||
63||
Exact English meaningThree kiṣkus is remembered as the circular station of Śakra and the other deities. Then one should construct a well-proportioned pavilion for the installation.

वास्तुपूजादि

Chapter 3 · verses 64–75
3.64
पूजयेद् वास्तुपुरुषस्योपरिष्ठांस्तु सर्वदा।
देवान् ब्रह्मादिकान् वास्तुर्वराहस्य हरेः सुतः।
पातितो दैवतैर्भूमौ चतुरश्राकृतिः स्थितः॥ ६४॥
pūjayed vāstupuruṣasyopariṣṭhāṃstu sarvadā|
devān brahmādikān vāsturvarāhasya hareḥ sutaḥ|
pātito daivatairbhūmau caturaśrākṛtiḥ sthitaḥ||
64||
3.65
कृत्वाऽष्टकाष्टकोष्ठानि मध्ये ब्रह्मा चतुष्टये।
पूज्यो द्वयेद्वये शक्रो यमो वरुण एव च।
सोमश्च पूज्यः कोणेषु वह्न्याद्याः कोणदेवताः॥ ६५॥
kṛtvā’ṣṭakāṣṭakoṣṭhāni madhye brahmā catuṣṭaye|
pūjyo dvayedvaye śakro yamo varuṇa eva ca|
somaśca pūjyaḥ koṇeṣu vahnyādyāḥ koṇadevatāḥ||
65||
3.66
पुनस्त्रयेत्रये कामं धातारं सविधातृकम्।
स्कन्दं च पूजयेत् कोणे सूर्यं चैव विनायकम्।
कालं कुबेरं च बहिः प्रत्येकं दिक्षु पूजयेत्॥ ६६॥
punastrayetraye kāmaṃ dhātāraṃ savidhātṛkam|
skandaṃ ca pūjayet koṇe sūryaṃ caiva vināyakam|
kālaṃ kuberaṃ ca bahiḥ pratyekaṃ dikṣu pūjayet||
66||
3.67
हारं हरिं गभीरं च गाहनं गेहनं गुहम्।
भातमङ्गारकं चैव पूर्वस्यां दिशि पूजयेत्॥ ६७॥
hāraṃ hariṃ gabhīraṃ ca gāhanaṃ gehanaṃ guham|
bhātamaṅgārakaṃ caiva pūrvasyāṃ diśi pūjayet||
67||
3.68
दारुणं विदरिं दूतं प्रद्योतं द्योतनं दरिम् ।
मृत्युं शनैश्चरं चैव दक्षिणस्यां प्रपूजयेत्॥ ६८॥
dāruṇaṃ vidariṃ dūtaṃ pradyotaṃ dyotanaṃ darim |
mṛtyuṃ śanaiścaraṃ caiva dakṣiṇasyāṃ prapūjayet||
68||
3.69
पुष्करं क्षरणं क्षारं विक्षणं क्षोभणं क्षरम्।
चार्वङ्गं भार्गवं चैव पश्चिमायां प्रपूजयेत्॥ ६९॥
puṣkaraṃ kṣaraṇaṃ kṣāraṃ vikṣaṇaṃ kṣobhaṇaṃ kṣaram|
cārvaṅgaṃ bhārgavaṃ caiva paścimāyāṃ prapūjayet||
69||
3.70
चारं विचारं प्रचरं सञ्चरं चारणं चरम्।
बृहस्पतिं बुधं चैवाप्युत्तरस्यां प्रपूजयेत्॥ ७०॥
cāraṃ vicāraṃ pracaraṃ sañcaraṃ cāraṇaṃ caram|
bṛhaspatiṃ budhaṃ caivāpyuttarasyāṃ prapūjayet||
70||
3.71
एवमेव विमानस्य कृतेः प्रागपि पूजयेत्।
सप्तपर्णमयं साधु कारयेन्मण्डपं ततः॥ ७१॥
evameva vimānasya kṛteḥ prāgapi pūjayet|
saptaparṇamayaṃ sādhu kārayenmaṇḍapaṃ tataḥ||
71||
3.72
सप्तहस्तं द्विषड्ढस्तमथवा द्विगुणं ततः।
चतुर्गुणं वा सम्यक् तु चतुरस्रं सहाजिरम्॥ ७२॥
saptahastaṃ dviṣaḍḍhastamathavā dviguṇaṃ tataḥ|
caturguṇaṃ vā samyak tu caturasraṃ sahājiram||
72||
3.73
अन्यच्च मण्टपं दीर्घं पञ्चहस्तं च कारयेत्।
त्रिचतुष्कोष्ठकं तत्र मध्ये प्रागायतं शुभम्॥ ७३॥
anyacca maṇṭapaṃ dīrghaṃ pañcahastaṃ ca kārayet|
tricatuṣkoṣṭhakaṃ tatra madhye prāgāyataṃ śubham||
73||
3.74
कारयेन्मण्डलं साधु हस्तमात्रं सुविस्तृतम्।
तत्र द्वादशपात्राणि स्थापयेत् पालिकादिकम्॥ ७४॥
kārayenmaṇḍalaṃ sādhu hastamātraṃ suvistṛtam|
tatra dvādaśapātrāṇi sthāpayet pālikādikam||
74||
3.75
पालिकास्तु चतुर्विंशत्यङ्गुलोच्छ्रयसंयुताः।
अष्टादशाङ्गुलाश्चैव तथा पञ्चमुखा अपि।
द्वादशाङ्गुलकाश्चैव शरावाः पश्चिमानुगाः॥ ७५॥
pālikāstu caturviṃśatyaṅgulocchrayasaṃyutāḥ|
aṣṭādaśāṅgulāścaiva tathā pañcamukhā api|
dvādaśāṅgulakāścaiva śarāvāḥ paścimānugāḥ||
75||
Exact combined meaningOne should always worship the gods, Brahmā and the rest, who stand above the Vāstu-puruṣa. The Vāstu(-puruṣa) is the son of Varāha-Hari, cast down upon the earth by the gods, lying four-cornered in shape. Having made eight compartments of wood, in the middle, in a set of four, Brahmā is to be worshipped; and in pairs, Śakra, Yama, Varuṇa and Soma; and in the corners, Vahni and the rest, the corner-deities are to be worshipped. Again, in threes, one should worship Kāma, Dhātṛ together with Vidhātṛ, and Skanda in the corner, and Sūrya and Vināyaka, Kāla and Kubera, outside, each in the (proper) quarters. (The forms of Hari:) Hari, Hara, Gabhīra, Gāhana, Gahana, Guha, Bhānu and Aṅgāraka — these one should worship in the eastern quarter; (then) Dāruṇa, Vidiri, Dyota, Pradyota, Dyotana, Hari, Mṛtyu and Śanaiścara — these one should worship in the south; (then) Puṣkara, Kṣaraṇa, Kṣāra, Vikṣaṇa, Kṣobhaṇa, Kṣara, Cārvaṅga and Bhārgava — these one should worship in the west; (then) Cāra, Vicāra, Pracara, Sañcāra, Cāraṇa, Cara. (then) Bṛhaspati and Budha — these one should worship in the north; even before the making of the vimāna one should worship the one (Vāstu-puruṣa) alone. Likewise one should have the pavilion (maṇṭapa) well made of the seven colors (i.e. of the saptaparṇa wood); seven hastas, twelve hastas, or double that. or fourfold, well-made, four-cornered — the hall-court; and one should make another pavilion, five hastas long. There in the middle a well-shaped compartment-grid of three-by-four, elongated to the east; one should draw a fine maṇḍala, a full hasta broad. There one should set the twelve vessels, the pālikā and the rest; the pālikās are furnished with a height of twenty-four aṅgulas, or eighteen aṅgulas, and likewise five-mouthed; and the śarāvas of twelve aṅgulas, following the west.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
In order to state the Vāstu-deities, he describes the Vāstu-maṇḍala of sixty-four squares: "kṛtvā." Eight eights make sixty-four compartments. Vahni and the rest — Vahni (Agni), Nirṛti, Vāyu and Īśāna — are to be worshipped in the corners. The rest is self-explanatory. (71–79) "Saptavarṇamayam": saptavarṇa (saptaparṇa) is a particular tree; one should have the pavilion made of it. The variations of seven hastas and the rest depend on one's capacity. (80) "Sahājiram" — ajira is a courtyard; together with it. And one should make another pavilion too, measuring five hastas, for the maṇḍala. There, in the second pavilion, one should draw a maṇḍala of twelve compartments, a full hasta broad. In its compartments one should set the twelve vessels, the pālikā and the rest. He states the characteristics of the pālikā: "pālikās tu." The pālikās are particular vessels twenty-four aṅgulas high, with wide mouths, (for) the sprouting of the seven grains. Likewise the pots (kumbha), eighteen aṅgulas high, five-mouthed. And the śarāvas (shallow dishes), twelve aṅgulas high, are to be made. Of these, four pālikās, four five-mouthed (pots), and four śarāvas are to be set in the twelve compartments. (81–84)
Chapter 3 · verse 76
3.76
तत्र विष्णुं चतुर्मूर्तिं पूजयेद् भक्तितस्त्रिशः।
सप्तधान्यानि तेष्वेव पूरयेदङ्कुरार्थतः॥ ७६॥
tatra viṣṇuṃ caturmūrtiṃ pūjayed bhaktitastriśaḥ|
saptadhānyāni teṣveva pūrayedaṅkurārthataḥ||
76||
Exact English meaningThere one should worship Viṣṇu in His four forms, intent on devotion; and in those very (vessels) one should fill the seven grains for the sake of sprouting.
Word and compound explanation
The meaning is: there in the maṇḍala one should worship the four-formed one — in the forms of Vāsudeva, Pradyumna, Saṅkarṣaṇa and Aniruddha — thrice, three times over, in all the twelve compartments. (85)
Chapter 3 · verses 77–78
3.77
तत्र नित्यं बलिं दद्याद् विष्णवे दशरात्रकम्।
ब्रह्मशङ्करगीर्वाणपितृगन्धर्वयक्षकैः।
भूतैश्च पायसेनैव तदा पद्माक्षतेन च॥ ७७॥
tatra nityaṃ baliṃ dadyād viṣṇave daśarātrakam|
brahmaśaṅkaragīrvāṇapitṛgandharvayakṣakaiḥ|
bhūtaiśca pāyasenaiva tadā padmākṣatena ca||
77||
3.78
अन्नापूपेन पुष्पान्नैरन्नलाजाक्षतैरपि।
सक्तुभिश्च क्रमेणैव देवान्तं पायसेन वा॥ ७८॥
annāpūpena puṣpānnairannalājākṣatairapi|
saktubhiśca krameṇaiva devāntaṃ pāyasena vā||
78||
Exact combined meaningThere one should offer bali daily to Viṣṇu for ten nights, (and) to Brahmā, Śaṅkara, the gods, the manes, the gandharvas and the yakṣas and the (elemental) beings — with pāyasa, and with lotus and unbroken rice; with cooked food and cakes, with flower-food, with rice-parched-grain and unbroken rice, and with barley-meal, in that very order; or to those gods with pāyasa.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
One should offer bali each day. Together with Brahmā, Rudra, the gods, the manes, the gandharvas and the yakṣas, one should offer balis to them also. "Bhūtān" — the Viṣṇu-beings; the accusative case is used in the sense of the dative: the meaning is "to the beings." He states the bali-substances: "pāyasenaiva." To Viṣṇu with pāyasa; to Brahmā, Śaṅkara and the gods with lotus and unbroken rice; to the manes with cooked food and cakes; to the gandharvas with flower-food; to the yakṣas with rice, parched grain and unbroken rice; to the (elemental) beings with barley-meal — in this order one should offer the balis. Or else to Viṣṇu, Brahmā, Rudra and the gods with pāyasa; to the manes and the rest with the substances mentioned. (86–87)
Chapter 3 · verse 79
3.79
तत्रैव प्रतिमायाश्च कारयेदधिवासनम्।
पञ्चगव्ये सप्तरात्रं क्षिप्त्वा पुरुषसूक्ततः॥ ७९॥
tatraiva pratimāyāśca kārayedadhivāsanam|
pañcagavye saptarātraṃ kṣiptvā puruṣasūktataḥ||
79||
Exact English meaningAt that same time one should perform the consecration (adhivāsana) of the image also, having immersed it seven nights in the five products of the cow, with the Puruṣa-sūkta.
Word and compound explanation
"At that same time" — on the very day of the setting-up of the pālikās and the rest, one should perform the consecration of the image too. One should place the image for seven nights in the five products of the cow, with the Puruṣa-sūkta. (88)
Chapter 3 · verse 80
3.80
वेदांश्च चतुरः सम्यक् पारयेयुर्द्विजोत्तमाः।
प्राच्यादिद्वारगौ द्वौद्वावेकैकं तु दशावराः।
यथालब्धं यथावित्तमथवा विष्णुतत्पराः॥ ८०॥
vedāṃśca caturaḥ samyak pārayeyurdvijottamāḥ|
prācyādidvāragau dvaudvāvekaikaṃ tu daśāvarāḥ|
yathālabdhaṃ yathāvittamathavā viṣṇutatparāḥ||
80||
Exact English meaningAnd the best of the twice-born should duly recite through the four Vedas; two by two, at the eastern and the other doorways, each (reciting) one (Veda), at the least ten (in all); (recruited) as they may be got, according to one's means, or (rather) such as are devoted to Viṣṇu.
Word and compound explanation
And on those days the best of the twice-born should recite through the Vedas, the Ṛg and the rest. Two reciters at each doorway of the pavilion should recite one Veda each. And thus there are eight reciters, sixteen officiants (ṛtvij), and the ācārya — twenty-five in all. "The best of the twice-born, as they may be got" — as many as fit ones are obtained; "according to one's means" or one's capacity — brāhmaṇas devoted to Viṣṇu. "Daśāvarāḥ" — those whose lower limit is ten; if (the means be) very small, they should be ten. (89)
Chapter 3 · verse 81
3.81
त्रयोदशात्र कुण्डानि परितः कारयेद् बुधः।
उक्तलक्षणयुक्तानि प्रधानं त्वग्निकोणके।
साक्षात् तु कोणकाद्धस्तमात्रमन्तरतः सुधीः॥ ८१॥
trayodaśātra kuṇḍāni paritaḥ kārayed budhaḥ|
uktalakṣaṇayuktāni pradhānaṃ tvagnikoṇake|
sākṣāt tu koṇakāddhastamātramantarataḥ sudhīḥ||
81||
Exact English meaningHere the wise man should have thirteen kuṇḍas made round about, furnished with the characteristics stated; the principal one in the fire-corner (south-east); and the wise one (should set them) at an interval of one hasta from the corner-(kuṇḍa).
Word and compound explanation
Here one should have thirteen kuṇḍas made, furnished with the aforesaid characteristics of the mekhalā, yoni and the rest — four in each direction. One should make the principal kuṇḍa in the fire-corner (south-east). From the corner-(kuṇḍa), the principal one, the eastern kuṇḍas and the southern kuṇḍas should be made at an interval of one hasta each. In each of those kuṇḍas one should make a hundred thousand or ten thousand ājya-oblations — for the sake of the deities presiding over the twenty-five tattvas. (90–92)
Chapter 3 · verses 82–85
3.82
आज्याहुतिं तेषु कुर्यात् प्रत्येकं लक्षसङ्ख्यकम्।
प्रत्येकमयुतं वाऽपि प्रधाने लक्षसङ्ख्यकम्।
अर्धं तदर्धमथवा मूलमन्त्रेण मन्त्रवित्॥ ८२॥
ājyāhutiṃ teṣu kuryāt pratyekaṃ lakṣasaṅkhyakam|
pratyekamayutaṃ vā’pi pradhāne lakṣasaṅkhyakam|
ardhaṃ tadardhamathavā mūlamantreṇa mantravit||
82||
3.83
पञ्चविंशतितत्त्वार्थे प्रधाने जुहुयात् पुरः।
प्रत्येकं तु सहस्रं वा प्रत्येकं शतमेव वा।
शतं पुरुषसूक्तेन गायत्र्या च सहस्रकम्॥ ८३॥
pañcaviṃśatitattvārthe pradhāne juhuyāt puraḥ|
pratyekaṃ tu sahasraṃ vā pratyekaṃ śatameva vā|
śataṃ puruṣasūktena gāyatryā ca sahasrakam||
83||
3.84
कृत्वा जलाधिवासं च पूर्वं वा पञ्चगव्यतः।
चक्राब्जं भद्रकं वाऽपि मण्टपे मण्डलं शुभम्॥ ८४॥
kṛtvā jalādhivāsaṃ ca pūrvaṃ vā pañcagavyataḥ|
cakrābjaṃ bhadrakaṃ vā’pi maṇṭape maṇḍalaṃ śubham||
84||
3.85
पद्मं वृत्तत्रयं चक्रं राशयो वीथिका तथा।
शोभोपशोभिकाश्चैव चक्राब्जस्य तु लक्षणम्।
चतुरस्रं विना चक्रं विशेषो भद्रके स्मृतः॥ ८५॥
padmaṃ vṛttatrayaṃ cakraṃ rāśayo vīthikā tathā|
śobhopaśobhikāścaiva cakrābjasya tu lakṣaṇam|
caturasraṃ vinā cakraṃ viśeṣo bhadrake smṛtaḥ||
85||
Exact combined meaningIn them ājya-oblations are to be made, a hundred thousand each; or ten thousand each; in the principal one, a hundred thousand, or a half, or half of that; the knower of mantras should offer in front, in the principal (kuṇḍa), with the mūla-mantra, for the sake of the twenty-five tattvas. or a thousand each, or a hundred each; a hundred with the Puruṣa-sūkta, and a thousand with the Gāyatrī; and having first made the water-consecration, or (the consecration) with the five products of the cow. In the pavilion (one should draw) an auspicious maṇḍala, the cakrābja or the bhadraka: a lotus, three circles, the discus, the 'heaps' (rāśis), and likewise the lanes (vīthikās), and the śobhās and upaśobhās — such is the characteristic of the cakrābja; the special feature in the bhadraka is held to be the discus without a square.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
In the principal kuṇḍa one should offer a thousand or a hundred each. With the Gāyatrī one should offer a thousand. Supply: having, before the consecration with the five products of the cow, made the water-consecration, one should perform the homa and the rest. (93) In the pavilion one should draw the cakrābja, the bhadraka, (or) the Sarvatobhadra maṇḍala. He states the characteristics of the cakrābja: "vṛtta." In the middle of the lotus, all around, three circles — the discus; the "heaps," the lanes, and the śobhās and upaśobhās; outside, a square — that is the mark of the bhadra. That (bhadraka) is without the discus; but the śobhā, upaśobhā and the rest are present there too. (94–95)

कलशपूजादि

Chapter 3 · verse 86
3.86
सहस्रमर्धं पादं वा कलशांस्तत्र पूजयेत्।
शतं तदर्धं पादं वा वित्ताभावे निगद्यते॥ ८६॥
sahasramardhaṃ pādaṃ vā kalaśāṃstatra pūjayet|
śataṃ tadardhaṃ pādaṃ vā vittābhāve nigadyate||
86||
Exact English meaningA thousand, a half, or a quarter — so many kalaśas one should worship there; a hundred, a half of that, or a quarter, is prescribed in the absence of means.
Word and compound explanation
He states the rule of number for the kalaśas: "sahasram." "Half" — five hundred; "a quarter" — two hundred fifty. In the absence of means, half of that, or the (whole) — such kalaśas are to be made. (96)
Chapter 3 · verses 87–89
3.87
क्वाथेन क्षीरवृक्षाणां तथा पञ्चामृतैरपि।
पञ्चगव्येन शुद्धेन जलेन च पृथक्पृथक्।
पूरयेत् कलशान् मध्यं शुद्धोदेनैव पूरयेत्॥ ८७॥
kvāthena kṣīravṛkṣāṇāṃ tathā pañcāmṛtairapi|
pañcagavyena śuddhena jalena ca pṛthakpṛthak|
pūrayet kalaśān madhyaṃ śuddhodenaiva pūrayet||
87||
3.88
ब्राह्मस्यैव चतुर्दिक्षु गन्धोदानां चतुष्टयम्।
चतुर्दलश्वेतपद्मचतुष्के मण्डलस्थिते।
पूर्वादिक्रमयोगेन रसक्वाथशुभोदकान्॥ ८८॥
brāhmasyaiva caturdikṣu gandhodānāṃ catuṣṭayam|
caturdalaśvetapadmacatuṣke maṇḍalasthite|
pūrvādikramayogena rasakvāthaśubhodakān||
88||
3.89
गन्धांश्च स्थापयेत् पञ्चगव्यं शुद्धान्तरे न्यसेत्।
पृथक् चतुर्दलं पद्मं रक्तं कृत्वाऽल्पकं सुधीः॥ ८९॥
gandhāṃśca sthāpayet pañcagavyaṃ śuddhāntare nyaset|
pṛthak caturdalaṃ padmaṃ raktaṃ kṛtvā’lpakaṃ sudhīḥ||
89||
Exact combined meaningWith a decoction of the milky trees, and likewise with the five nectars, with pure pañcagavya and with water — one should fill the kalaśas, each separately; the central one one should fill with pure water alone. In the four quarters of the Brahmā(-kalaśa), a tetrad of scented-water (kalaśas). On a set of four four-petalled white lotuses placed in the maṇḍala, one should set, in the order beginning from the east, (kalaśas of) juice, decoction, auspicious water, and scents; and the pañcagavya one should place in the pure (central) one; the wise man, having made separately a small red four-petalled lotus.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
One should fill the kalaśas, each separately, with a decoction of the milky trees — the udumbara and the rest — with the five nectars, with pañcagavya — the five cow-products mixed together — and with pure water. "The middle" — the central kalaśa. In the four quarters of the Brahmā-kalaśa one should set a tetrad of kalaśas filled with scented water — such is the meaning. On the set of four four-petalled white lotuses fashioned in the four quarters of the whole (set of) kalaśas, one should set the kalaśas of juice, decoction, auspicious water and scent. "And scents": as it is said, "uśīra, kuṅkuma, māṁsa (a fragrant substance), kula, sandal, aguru, hrībera and mura — these eight they declare to be the scents." One should set the great kalaśa. Having done what "separately"? Having made a small red four-petalled lotus, for the placing of the kalaśa of the five substances one should set the pañcagavya-kalaśa in the pure (space), between the pure-water kalaśa and the whole (set of) kalaśas. (97–100)
Chapter 3 · verses 90–91
3.90
पञ्चाशत्त्वे तु गन्धोदं सर्वमध्ये निधापयेत्।
ब्राह्मस्य पुरतस्त्वेकं शार्वं संस्थापयेद् बृहत्।
अष्टदिक्ष्वष्टपद्मेषु पञ्चकम्पञ्चकं न्यसेत्॥ ९०॥
pañcāśattve tu gandhodaṃ sarvamadhye nidhāpayet|
brāhmasya puratastvekaṃ śārvaṃ saṃsthāpayed bṛhat|
aṣṭadikṣvaṣṭapadmeṣu pañcakampañcakaṃ nyaset||
90||
3.91
अनेन क्रमयोगेन कलशा अखिला अपि।
तत्तद्द्रव्यमयास्तत्रतत्र स्थाप्यास्तु बाह्यतः।
तदैवाङ्कुरपात्राणि परितस्तत्र विन्यसेत्॥ ९१॥
anena kramayogena kalaśā akhilā api|
tattaddravyamayāstatratatra sthāpyāstu bāhyataḥ|
tadaivāṅkurapātrāṇi paritastatra vinyaset||
91||
Exact combined meaningIn the case of fifty (kalaśas), one should set the scented-water (kalaśa) in the very middle of all; and in front of the Brahmā(-kalaśa) one should set up a single great Śārva (kalaśa). In the eight quarters, on eight lotuses, one should place five and five; by this same sequence all the kalaśas, made of their respective substances, are to be set there and there on the outside; and at the same time one should set out all around the sprouting-vessels.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"In the fifty-ness of the kalaśas" — in the case where there are fifty. One should set the scented-water kalaśa in the midst of all and of the Brahmā-kalaśa. In front of the Brahmā-kalaśa — in the eastern part — one should set a great kalaśa. (101) Around it, having drawn a lotus in the eight quarters, one should set five kalaśas on each of them. By this very sequence ( ) all the kalaśas, filled with their respective substances, are to be set all around on the outer region; the sprouting-vessels one should set out all around, outside the kalaśas. (102–103)
Chapter 3 · verse 92
3.92
कुशोदकं च कार्पूरं कौङ्कुमं चान्दनं तथा।
तुहिनोदं हीरबेरमौशीरं कोष्ठसार्षपम्॥ ९२॥
kuśodakaṃ ca kārpūraṃ kauṅkumaṃ cāndanaṃ tathā|
tuhinodaṃ hīraberamauśīraṃ koṣṭhasārṣapam||
92||
Exact English meaning(kalaśas of) kuśa-water, and camphor-water, saffron-water, sandal-water, likewise cool water, hrībera-water, uśīra-water, kuṣṭha- and mustard-water.
Word and compound explanation
He states the special kalaśas: "kauśodam." Filled with kuśa-water. "Kārpūra" — filled with camphor-water. "Kauṅkuma" — furnished with saffron-water. "Cāndana" — filled with sandal-water. "Tuhinoda" — furnished with cool water. "Hīrabera" — filled with the medicinal-water of hrībera. "Auśīra" — filled with uśīra-water. "Kauṣṭha" — filled with the medicinal-water of kuṣṭha. "Sārṣapa" — filled with mustard-water. (104)
Chapter 3 · verse 93
3.93
नदीसङ्गमजं चैव ताटाकं कौप्यमेव च।
निर्झरोदमिति प्रोक्ता विशेषकलशा इमे।
शुद्धोदद्वयमप्येतन्महातीर्थसमुद्भवम्॥ ९३॥
nadīsaṅgamajaṃ caiva tāṭākaṃ kaupyameva ca|
nirjharodamiti proktā viśeṣakalaśā ime|
śuddhodadvayamapyetanmahātīrthasamudbhavam||
93||
Exact English meaningand (water) born of a river-confluence, and pond-water, or well-water, and cascade-water — these are declared the special kalaśas; and this pair of pure-water (kalaśas) too, arisen from a great holy ford.
Word and compound explanation
"Nadīsaṅgamaja" — filled with the water of a river-confluence. "Tāṭāka" — filled with pond-water. "Kaupya" — filled with well-water. "Nirjharoda" — filled with cascade-water. (105)
Chapter 3 · verses 94–95
3.94
चतुर्विंशतिमूर्तीनां वर्णमूर्तीस्तथाऽपराः।
आवाहयेच्छतत्वे तु क्रमव्युत्क्रमतस्तु ताः॥ ९४॥
caturviṃśatimūrtīnāṃ varṇamūrtīstathā’parāḥ|
āvāhayecchatatve tu kramavyutkramatastu tāḥ||
94||
3.95
वर्णमूर्तीः पुनश्चैव द्विचतुर्वारमेव च।
सहस्रकलशादित्वे जपेन्मन्त्रांश्च शक्तितः॥ ९५॥
varṇamūrtīḥ punaścaiva dvicaturvārameva ca|
sahasrakalaśāditve japenmantrāṃśca śaktitaḥ||
95||
Exact combined meaningOne should invoke the twenty-four mūrtis, and likewise the other letter-forms; and in the case of a hundred (kalaśas), (one should invoke) them by the direct and reverse orders, and the letter-forms again, twice and four times over; and in the case of a thousand kalaśas and so forth, one should recite the mantras with devotion.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
The twenty-four beginning with Keśava; the letter-forms, Aja and the rest — one should invoke them in the kalaśas. In the case of a hundred pots, or more, one should invoke those same mūrtis by the direct and reverse orders. (106–107)
Chapter 3 · verses 96–98
3.96
ततस्तु पञ्चघोषैश्च वेदघोषैस्तथाऽऽदरात्।
आनीय प्रतिमां स्नानं कारयेत् प्रणवेन तु॥ ९६॥
tatastu pañcaghoṣaiśca vedaghoṣaistathā’’darāt|
ānīya pratimāṃ snānaṃ kārayet praṇavena tu||
96||
3.97
मन्त्रैः पुरुषसूक्तान्तैर्विश्वतश्चक्षुषा तथा।
पुनश्चाष्टाक्षरेणैव प्रणवेन च भक्तितः॥ ९७॥
mantraiḥ puruṣasūktāntairviśvataścakṣuṣā tathā|
punaścāṣṭākṣareṇaiva praṇavena ca bhaktitaḥ||
97||
3.98
स्नापयित्वा गन्धजलैर्गन्धपुष्पादिभिः पुनः।
अलङ्कृत्य स्थापयेत् तु प्रतिमां प्रणवेन तु॥ ९८॥

Exact combined meaningThen, with the five sounds and with the Vedas, reverently having brought the image, one should have it bathed with the praṇava, with the mantras ending with the Puruṣa-sūkta, and with the 'Viśvataścakṣus'; and again with the eight-syllable mantra and the praṇava, with devotion, having installed it, (bathing) again with scented waters, with scents, flowers and the rest, and having adorned it, one should install the image with the praṇava.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
With the five sounds and with the sounds of the Vedas, having brought the image there and set it upon the seat placed in the maṇḍala, one should bathe it with the praṇava and the other mantras. Then, having bathed it with scented waters and adorned it, one should install it with the praṇava. (108–110)
Chapter 3 · verses 99–100
3.99
जपेदष्टाक्षरं मन्त्रं तत्तन्मूर्तिमनुं तथा।
अष्टोत्तरशतं मन्त्री ध्यायेत् तेजोमयीं पुनः॥ ९९॥
japedaṣṭākṣaraṃ mantraṃ tattanmūrtimanuṃ tathā|
aṣṭottaraśataṃ mantrī dhyāyet tejomayīṃ punaḥ||
99||
3.100
प्रतिमां प्रतिमान्तस्स्थां तन्मध्ये परमं वपुः।
चिदानन्दरसं सर्वगुणसम्पूर्णमुत्तमम्॥ १००॥
pratimāṃ pratimāntassthāṃ tanmadhye paramaṃ vapuḥ|
cidānandarasaṃ sarvaguṇasampūrṇamuttamam||
100||
Exact combined meaningThe mantrin should recite the eight-syllable mantra, and likewise the mantra of the respective mūrti, a hundred and eight times; then he should meditate on the image as luminous, the image standing within the image, and in its midst the supreme body — the essence of consciousness-and-bliss, complete in all attributes, the highest.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
One should recite the eight-syllable mantra, and the mantra of the respective mūrti — of the Kṛṣṇa or other form one wishes to install — a hundred and eight times. Then one should meditate on the luminous image standing within the image; and in the midst of that luminous one, meditate on Hari, the essence of consciousness-and-bliss, complete in all attributes. (111–112)
Chapter 3 · verses 101–103
3.101
पञ्चविंशतितत्त्वानां देवतास्तदनन्तरम्।
स्थापयेत् प्रतिमामध्ये परितः केशवस्य तु॥ १०१॥
pañcaviṃśatitattvānāṃ devatāstadanantaram|
sthāpayet pratimāmadhye paritaḥ keśavasya tu||
101||
3.102
तत्राऽत्वाहार्षसूक्तं च घर्मसूक्तं च वैष्णवम्।
सूक्तं च विश्वकर्मीयं पौरुषं सूक्तमेव च॥ १०२॥
tatrā’tvāhārṣasūktaṃ ca gharmasūktaṃ ca vaiṣṇavam|
sūktaṃ ca viśvakarmīyaṃ pauruṣaṃ sūktameva ca||
102||
3.103
जप्त्वा चैव निजं मन्त्रं पुनरष्टाक्षरं सुधीः।
प्रणवं च जपेद् विष्णुं ध्यायन्नुत्तमरूपिणम्॥ १०३॥
japtvā caiva nijaṃ mantraṃ punaraṣṭākṣaraṃ sudhīḥ|
praṇavaṃ ca japed viṣṇuṃ dhyāyannuttamarūpiṇam||
103||
Exact combined meaningThereafter one should install the deities of the twenty-five tattvas in the middle of the image, all around Keśava. There, having recited the 'ā tvā ahārṣam' sūkta, the Vaiṣṇava 'dharma' sūkta, the Viśvakarman sūkta, and the Puruṣa-sūkta, and having recited his own mantra, and again the eight-syllable mantra and the praṇava, the wise man should recite (them), meditating on Viṣṇu of supreme form.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
Around Keśava, meditated upon in the middle of the image, one should meditate on the deities of the tattvas. He states the sūktas to be recited: the sūkta beginning "ā tvā ahārṣam"; the "dharma" sūkta beginning "dharmā samantāt"; the Vaiṣṇava — the Viṣṇu-sūkta beginning "ato devā(ḥ)"; the Viśvakarman sūkta beginning "ya imā viśvā bhuvanāni." Having installed his own mantra — the Kṛṣṇa or other mantra — one should recite the eight-syllable mantra and the praṇava, meditating on Viṣṇu. (113–115)
Chapter 3 · verses 104–108
3.104
ततस्तु कलशैर्बाह्यक्रमेणैव जनार्दनम्।
स्नापयेदुक्तमन्त्रैस्तु मध्यमं प्रणवेन तु॥ १०४॥

pūrvādyuttaraparyantaḥ kalaśakrama ucyate|

द्वारलोकपतिभ्यश्च बलिं दत्वा यथाक्रमम्।
वस्त्ररत्नहिरण्याद्यैरलङ्कृत्य विभूषितः।
गुरवे दक्षिणां दद्यात् कोटिं लक्षं सहस्रकम्॥ १०७॥

शतमर्धं तदर्धं वा निस्स्वो भक्त्या क्षमापयेत्।
तदर्धमृत्विजां चैव तदर्धं पारणाकृताम्॥ १०८॥
śatamardhaṃ tadardhaṃ vā nissvo bhaktyā kṣamāpayet|
tadardhamṛtvijāṃ caiva tadardhaṃ pāraṇākṛtām||
108||
Exact combined meaningThen one should bathe Janārdana with the kalaśas in the outer sequence, with the mantras stated; the central one with the praṇava, and with the eight-syllable and one's own (mantra), and likewise with the Puruṣa-sūkta; the invocation and the bathing with pañcagavya as in the middle (rite); the sequence of kalaśas from the east up to the north is stated. Having recited those mantras again, one should worship according to rule, and give bali to the (deities of) the doorways and to the world-guardians, in due order. Having adorned (the image) with garments, gems, gold and the rest, according to his wealth, one should give the guru a fee — a crore, a hundred thousand, a thousand, a hundred, a half, or a half of that; a pauper should ask pardon with devotion. And half of that (fee) for the officiants, and half of that for those who perform the pāraṇā (concluding meal).

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
Then one should set (them) with the kalaśas, in the Brahmā-order, from the east up to the north. Afterward, having sprinkled with the central kalaśa, the pañcagavya-kalaśa, having adorned (the image) with garments, ornaments and the rest, having worshipped according to rule, one should give bali to the (deities of) the doorways and to the world-guardians. (116–118) "Half of that" — half the guru's fee, for the officiants, to the ṛtvijs. "Half of that" — half the officiants' fee, for those who perform the pāraṇā — for the reciters (of the pārāyaṇa), the japakas. (119–120)
Chapter 3 · verses 109–110
3.109
आ श्वभ्य आ श्वपाकेभ्यो दद्यादन्नं समस्तशः।

Exact combined meaningTo all, down to the dogs and the dog-cookers (outcastes), one should give food; before and after (the installation) for seven nights without interruption. With gold, garments, gems and the rest one should honor those who have come and those who have arrived, according to one's means, with devotion, thinking 'may the Lord be pleased.'

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"Before" — before the installation. "After" — the meaning is: one should do (this) after the installation as well. (121–122)
Chapter 3 · verses 111–113
3.111
गीतनृत्यैश्च वाद्यैश्च पुराणैरितिहासकैः।
स्तोत्रैर्मङ्गलघोषैश्च वैदिकैर्दिनसप्तकम्॥ १११॥

महानदीसङ्गमेषु तीर्थे चातिप्रशस्तके।

mahānadīsaṅgameṣu tīrthe cātipraśastake|

113||
Exact combined meaningWith songs, dances and instruments, with the Purāṇas and the Itihāsas, with sūktas and auspicious sounds, Vedic ones, one should pass the seven days; then the wise man, with royal splendor, should have the avabhṛtha (concluding ablution) of the God of gods performed, with recitations of scripture, with songs and dances; at the confluence of a great river, at a most excellent holy ford, one should bathe (the image) as before, with the mantras ending with the Puruṣa-sūkta, remembering Hari.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
Then one should pass seven days with a festival of song, dance and the rest. Then, at an excellent holy ford such as a river-confluence, (one should perform) the God's ( ) avabhṛtha — the great ablution at the end of the sacrifice — with the aforesaid mantras. (123–125)
Chapter 3 · verses 114–115
3.114
ततः पूर्ववदागत्य प्रवेश्य पुरुषोत्तमम्।
पूजयित्वा विधानेन दत्वा दानानि शक्तितः।
गुरुं च भक्त्या सम्पूज्य स्वीकुर्यादाशिषस्ततः॥ ११४॥
tataḥ pūrvavadāgatya praveśya puruṣottamam|

3.115
शतप्रस्थादनूनं तु नैवेद्यं पायसोत्तरम्।
दिनेष्वेतेषु दातव्यं सघृतं ससितादिकम्।
पश्चादपि यथाशक्ति पूजा कार्या हरेः सदा॥ ११५॥
