115||
Exact combined meaningThen, having come back as before, having brought Puruṣottama in, having worshipped according to rule, having given gifts according to one's means, and having reverently worshipped the guru, one should thereupon accept his blessings; naivedya of not less than a hundred prasthas, chiefly pāyasa, is to be given on these days, together with ghee, sugar and the rest; and afterward too the worship of Hari is ever to be performed according to one's means.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"Not less than a hundred prasthas" — the meaning is that one should not make the naivedya less than that. (126–128)

जीर्णदेवालयोद्धारप्रकारः

Chapter 3 · verse 116
3.116
जीर्णालयोद्धृतौ चैव तत्त्वमन्त्रान् स्वकं तथा।
प्रातिलोम्येन जप्त्वैव मूर्तौ सङ्कोचयेद्धरिं॥ ११६॥
jīrṇālayoddhṛtau caiva tattvamantrān svakaṃ tathā|
prātilomyena japtvaiva mūrtau saṅkocayeddhariṃ||
116||
Exact English meaningAnd in the removal (of an image) from a decayed temple, in the same way, having recited the tattva-mantras and one's own in reverse order, one should withdraw Hari into the image.
Word and compound explanation
"In the removal from a decayed temple" — in the restoration of what is decayed. (129)
Chapter 3 · verse 117
3.117
उक्तेनैव विधानेन कृत्वा देवालयं पुनः।
स्नापयेत् पुण्डरीकाक्षं द्विगुणेन प्रवाहणात्।
विभवेनानुलोमेन जपेन्मन्त्रान् पुनस्तथा॥ ११७॥
uktenaiva vidhānena kṛtvā devālayaṃ punaḥ|
snāpayet puṇḍarīkākṣaṃ dviguṇena pravāhaṇāt|
vibhavenānulomena japenmantrān punastathā||
117||
Exact English meaningHaving again made the temple by the very procedure already stated, one should bathe the Lotus-eyed Lord with twice the magnificence, following the prescribed course, and then again recite the mantras in their direct order.
Chapter 3 · verses 118–120
3.118
उक्तेनैव विधानेन तदर्धैर्विभवैः सुधीः।
पादमात्रैरपि हरिं स्नापयित्वा यथोदितम्॥ ११८॥
uktenaiva vidhānena tadardhairvibhavaiḥ sudhīḥ|
pādamātrairapi hariṃ snāpayitvā yathoditam||
118||
3.119
एतेनैव विधानेन कृत्वा देवालयं पुनः।
आराधयेज्जगन्नाथं ध्यायन् भक्त्या यथोदितम्।
यात्राऽपि पूर्ववत् तत्राप्युत्सवेषु च सर्वशः॥ ११९॥
etenaiva vidhānena kṛtvā devālayaṃ punaḥ|
ārādhayejjagannāthaṃ dhyāyan bhaktyā yathoditam|
yātrā’pi pūrvavat tatrāpyutsaveṣu ca sarvaśaḥ||
119||
3.120
कलशोक्तविधानेन पूजा बलिहृतिस्तथा।
उत्सवेषु सदा कार्या कलशश्चोत्सवादनु।
यात्रास्थानं च कर्तव्यं सम्यगुक्तविधानतः॥ १२०॥
kalaśoktavidhānena pūjā balihṛtistathā|
utsaveṣu sadā kāryā kalaśaścotsavādanu|
yātrāsthānaṃ ca kartavyaṃ samyaguktavidhānataḥ||
120||
Exact combined meaningBy the very procedure stated, with half that magnificence—or even with only a quarter—the wise person should bathe Hari as prescribed. By this same procedure, having again made the temple, one should worship the Lord of the universe while meditating with devotion as taught. The procession is likewise as before, and so in every festival. Worship and the offering of bali are always to be performed at festivals according to the kalaśa procedure; a kalaśa is to follow the festival, and the place and course of the procession are to be arranged exactly according to the stated procedure.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

सम्प्रोक्षणविधानम्

Chapter 3 · verses 121–123
3.121
चोरचण्डालपतितश्वोदक्यादिप्रवेशने।
शवाद्युपहतौ चैव पूजाविच्छेदने तथा।
स्नापनोक्तेन मार्गेण प्रायश्चित्तविधिः स्मृतः॥ १२१॥
coracaṇḍālapatitaśvodakyādipraveśane|
śavādyupahatau caiva pūjāvicchedane tathā|
snāpanoktena mārgeṇa prāyaścittavidhiḥ smṛtaḥ||
121||
3.122
सर्वत्र विष्णुगायत्र्या होमः स्यादयुतावरः।
नावाव्याद्यभिधानानि विद्महे धीमहे तथा।
प्रचोदयात् तृतीयाधस्तन्नो गायत्रिनामिका॥ १२२॥
sarvatra viṣṇugāyatryā homaḥ syādayutāvaraḥ|
nāvāvyādyabhidhānāni vidmahe dhīmahe tathā|
pracodayāt tṛtīyādhastanno gāyatrināmikā||
122||
3.123
दुर्गाशिवस्कन्दसूर्यविनायकमुखानपि।
स्थापयेदुक्तमार्गेण तत्तन्मन्त्रैः स्मरन् हरिं॥ १२३॥
durgāśivaskandasūryavināyakamukhānapi|
sthāpayeduktamārgeṇa tattanmantraiḥ smaran hariṃ||
123||
Exact combined meaningOn the entering (into the temple) of a thief, an outcaste, a fallen person, a dog, a menstruating woman and the like, and on defilement by a corpse and so forth, and likewise on an interruption of the worship, the performance of the expiation (prāyaścitta) is enjoined by the procedure stated for the bathing; and everywhere the homa with the Viṣṇu-gāyatrī should be at least ten thousand. After the words 'nā' (Nārāyaṇa) and 'vā' (Vāsudeva), 'vidmahe,' likewise 'dhīmahi,' and below the third, 'pracodayāt,' 'tan naḥ' — such is the (Viṣṇu-)Gāyatrī by name. Even the (images) of Durgā, Śiva, Skanda, Sūrya, Vināyaka and the rest one should install by the stated procedure, with their respective mantras, (while) remembering Hari.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
On the entering of a thief and the rest into the chief shrine; "udakī" — a menstruating woman; on defilement by corpses and other most impure things, even in the case of a touch (transmitted) through an intermediary; on an interruption — the ceasing — of the worship. (135–136) He states the extraction of the Viṣṇu-gāyatrī: "nā." By "nā" is to be understood "nārāyaṇāya." Below it, "vidmahe" is to be joined. By "vā," "vāsudevāya." Below that, "dhīmahi" is to be joined. Then "tan no viṣṇuḥ pracodayāt." And thus the form of the mantra is: "nārāyaṇāya vidmahe vāsudevāya dhīmahi, tan no viṣṇuḥ pracodayāt." (137–138)

अन्यदेवताप्रतिमाप्रतिष्ठापनविधिः

Chapter 3 · verse 124
3.124
गोलकद्वितयं विष्णोस्त्रितयं वा प्रकीर्तितम्।
अन्यदेवप्रतिष्ठासु तदन्तश्चिन्तयेद्धरिं॥ १२४॥
golakadvitayaṃ viṣṇostritayaṃ vā prakīrtitam|
anyadevapratiṣṭhāsu tadantaścintayeddhariṃ||
124||
Exact English meaningA pair of 'globes' (golakas) of Viṣṇu, or even a triad, is declared; in the installations of other gods one should contemplate Hari within them.
Word and compound explanation
"A pair of golakas": in (the case of) Viṣṇu — in the installation of a Viṣṇu-image — there are a pair of golakas, a pair of images: one, the image to be installed; the second, the luminous one within it — such is the pair of golakas. The third is Viṣṇu Himself. In the installations of other gods there are three: one, the image of the other god to be installed; the second, the luminous image of that god; the third is that god himself — such is the triad. The fourth is Viṣṇu abiding within them. (139)
Chapter 3 · verse 125
3.125
विष्णुस्मृतिविहीना तु पूजा स्यादासुरी स्मृता।
गृह्णन्ति देवता नैतां ततः स्याद् देशविप्लवः।
व्याधिचोरादिभिस्तस्मादन्तर्ध्येयो हरिः सदा॥ १२५॥
viṣṇusmṛtivihīnā tu pūjā syādāsurī smṛtā|
gṛhṇanti devatā naitāṃ tataḥ syād deśaviplavaḥ|
vyādhicorādibhistasmādantardhyeyo hariḥ sadā||
125||
Exact English meaningBut worship devoid of the remembrance of Viṣṇu becomes demonic (āsurī); the deities do not accept it; thence arises a disorder of the realm through disease, thieves and calamities; therefore Hari is ever to be meditated upon within.
Word and compound explanation
The construction is: "a disorder of the realm through disease and the rest." (140)

भगवद्भक्तेरावश्यकता

Chapter 3 · verses 126–127
3.126
विष्णुभक्तिविहीनस्य गतिः स्यान्न शुभा क्वचित्।
भक्तस्याप्यन्यदेवेषु तस्माद् ध्येयो हरिः सदा॥ १२६॥
viṣṇubhaktivihīnasya gatiḥ syānna śubhā kvacit|
bhaktasyāpyanyadeveṣu tasmād dhyeyo hariḥ sadā||
126||
3.127
संस्मृतो भगवान् विष्णुः सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गलः।
समस्ताभीष्टदायी स्यात् तेन ध्येयोऽखिलैर्जनैः॥ १२७॥
saṃsmṛto bhagavān viṣṇuḥ sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalaḥ|
samastābhīṣṭadāyī syāt tena dhyeyo’khilairjanaiḥ||
127||
Exact combined meaningFor one devoid of devotion to Viṣṇu there can never be an auspicious course, even (for one who is) devoted to other gods; therefore Hari is ever to be meditated upon. The Lord Viṣṇu, when remembered, being the auspiciousness of all auspicious things, becomes the giver of all that is desired; therefore He is to be meditated upon by all people.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

पञ्चविंशत्तत्वानां (षट्त्रिंशत्तत्त्वानां वा) निरूपणम्

Chapter 3 · verse 128
3.128
पुरुषोऽव्यक्तं च महानहङ्कारो मनस्तथा।
दशेन्द्रियाणि शब्दाद्या भूतेता पञ्चविंशतिः॥ १२८॥
puruṣo’vyaktaṃ ca mahānahaṅkāro manastathā|
daśendriyāṇi śabdādyā bhūtetā pañcaviṃśatiḥ||
128||
Exact English meaningPuruṣa, and the Unmanifest (avyakta), Mahat, ahaṅkāra, and mind; the ten senses, (the subtle elements) beginning with sound, together with the (gross) elements — twenty-five.
Word and compound explanation
It was said "the twenty-five tattvas." He states their division: "puruṣo 'vyaktam." "Beginning with sound" — sound, touch, form, taste and smell (the five tanmātras). "Bhūtetāḥ" — together with the five gross elements. (141–143)
Chapter 3 · verses 129–130
3.129
तत्वाख्याः कालमाये च नियतिर्मतिरेव च।
विद्या कला प्रवृत्तिश्च द्वात्रिंशत्तत्वसङ्ग्रहः॥ १२९॥
tatvākhyāḥ kālamāye ca niyatirmatireva ca|
vidyā kalā pravṛttiśca dvātriṃśattatvasaṅgrahaḥ||
129||
3.130
स्फूर्तिः संवित् प्रतिष्ठा च शक्तिरित्यपराणि च।
महालक्ष्मीस्वरूपाणि ह्येकादश विदो विदुः।
विष्णोरपि हि रूपाणि तन्नाम्नैकादशैव तु॥ १३०॥
sphūrtiḥ saṃvit pratiṣṭhā ca śaktirityaparāṇi ca|
mahālakṣmīsvarūpāṇi hyekādaśa vido viduḥ|
viṣṇorapi hi rūpāṇi tannāmnaikādaśaiva tu||
130||
Exact combined meaningNamed tattvas (also are): Kāla and Māyā, and Niyati, and Mati, and Vidyā, Kalā and Pravṛtti — the assemblage of twenty-two tattvas; and Sphūrti, Saṁvit, Pratiṣṭhā and Śakti — these other eleven, the knowers know, are forms of Mahālakṣmī; and, under those very names, they are indeed also eleven forms of Viṣṇu.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
The eleven tattvas, from Kāla up to Śakti, exist separately; and they are forms both of Viṣṇu and of Mahālakṣmī. (144–145)
Chapter 3 · verses 131–136
3.131
प्रधानोपमवर्णानि द्विभुजान्यप्यशेषतः।
कृताञ्जलिपुटान्येव प्रधानस्थं हरिं प्रति।
सर्वाण्येतानि तत्त्वानि ब्रह्मा तु पुरुषः स्मृतः॥ १३१॥
pradhānopamavarṇāni dvibhujānyapyaśeṣataḥ|
kṛtāñjalipuṭānyeva pradhānasthaṃ hariṃ prati|
sarvāṇyetāni tattvāni brahmā tu puruṣaḥ smṛtaḥ||
131||
3.132
महांश्चाव्यक्तनाम्नी तु ब्रह्माणी सम्प्रकीर्तिता।
एवं वायुरपि ज्ञेयो भारती चापि तत् त्रयम्॥ १३२॥
mahāṃścāvyaktanāmnī tu brahmāṇī samprakīrtitā|
evaṃ vāyurapi jñeyo bhāratī cāpi tat trayam||
132||
3.133
रुद्रोऽहङ्कार उद्दिष्टः स्कन्देन्द्रौ मन उच्यते।
अहङ्कारः शेषवीन्द्रावपि विद्वद्भिरीरितौ॥ १३३॥
rudro’haṅkāra uddiṣṭaḥ skandendrau mana ucyate|
ahaṅkāraḥ śeṣavīndrāvapi vidvadbhirīritau||
133||
3.134
दिग्वायुसूनुसूर्याश्च वरुणश्चाश्विनावपि।
वह्निदक्षाविन्द्रसूनुर्मित्रश्चैव मनुस्तथा॥ १३४॥
digvāyusūnusūryāśca varuṇaścāśvināvapi|
vahnidakṣāvindrasūnurmitraścaiva manustathā||
134||
3.135
इन्द्रियाख्याः शब्दनामा बृहस्पतिरुदाहृतः।
अन्ये तु सूनवो वायो रुद्रस्यापि प्रकीर्तिताः॥ १३५॥
indriyākhyāḥ śabdanāmā bṛhaspatirudāhṛtaḥ|
anye tu sūnavo vāyo rudrasyāpi prakīrtitāḥ||
135||
3.136
एतेषु भगवान् विष्णुः प्रधानतनुरेव तु।
ध्येयः परात्मनोर्मध्ये तत्वनाम तदर्थयोः।
जपे न्यासे नमोन्तस्तु स्वाहान्तो होमकर्मणि॥ १३६॥
eteṣu bhagavān viṣṇuḥ pradhānatanureva tu|
dhyeyaḥ parātmanormadhye tatvanāma tadarthayoḥ|
jape nyāse namontastu svāhānto homakarmaṇi||
136||
Exact combined meaning(These forms are) of a color like the principal one, two-armed, all without exception, their hands folded in añjali toward that principal Hari. All these are the tattvas. Brahmā is held to be the Puruṣa; and Brahmāṇī is proclaimed as Mahat and by the name Avyakta. Thus Vāyu too is to be known, and Bhāratī also — that triad. Rudra is designated as ahaṅkāra; Skanda and Indra are called the mind (manas). Ahaṅkāra: Śeṣa and the Lord of Birds (Garuḍa) too are declared (to be its forms); and the (deities of the) quarters, the son of Vāyu (Hanumat), the Sun, and Varuṇa, and the two Aśvins, Vahni, Dakṣa, the son of Indra (Jayanta), and Mitra likewise — (these are) the manes (or: the mind); the (deity) named after the senses, the (deity) named after sound, is declared to be Bṛhaspati. And others — the sons of Vāyu and of Rudra — are also proclaimed (as tattva-deities); and among these the Lord Viṣṇu is the principal body indeed. (The tattva-deity) named after the tattva is to be meditated upon between the two (words) para and ātman, which stand for it; in japa and nyāsa it ends with namaḥ, but in the homa-rite it ends with svāhā.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation on the tattva-gods: "pradhāna." The pradhāna is the supreme, Śrī Nārāyaṇa; "of a color like Him" — of the color of the rising sun. Or else the pradhāna is the Unmanifest; "of its color," since it, being of the nature of the three guṇas, is white, black and red. Their hands are folded in añjali toward the principal Hari abiding in the pradhāna. Thus, from Puruṣa up to Śakti, the thirty-six tattvas. (146) He states the deities presiding over the tattvas: "brahmā tu." (147–151) He states the manner of the tattva-nyāsa: "parātmanoḥ." The meaning is: between the two words para and ātman with dative endings, which convey the (respective) tattva-meaning, the name of the tattva is to be uttered. For example: "parāya puruṣātmane brahmaṇe namaḥ." (152)
Chapter 3 · verse 137
3.137
त्रिचतुःषड्दशावृत्तिरेतेषां तु हुतादिके।
न्यासोऽङ्गुलीषूरुबाहुमध्येषु व्युत्क्रमात् स्मृतः।
क्रमे विपर्ययेणात्र शक्त्यादित्वं प्रकीर्तितम्॥ १३७॥
tricatuḥṣaḍdaśāvṛttireteṣāṃ tu hutādike|
nyāso’ṅgulīṣūrubāhumadhyeṣu vyutkramāt smṛtaḥ|
krame viparyayeṇātra śaktyāditvaṃ prakīrtitam||
137||
Exact English meaningOf these (tattva-mantras), the repetition in the homa and the rest is three, four, six, or ten times; the nyāsa is enjoined on the fingers, thighs and arms and the middle (parts) in the reverse order (of dissolution); in the (direct) order (of creation), here, by inversion, the beginning-with-Śakti (order) is declared.
Word and compound explanation
Of these tattva-mantras a threefold repetition is to be made in the homa and the rest. He states the stations of the nyāsa: "aṅgulīṣu." This is the order of the dissolution-nyāsa. Thus: beginning from the little toe of the left foot up to the (great) toe, one should place the pentad beginning with earth — "Oṁ parāya pṛthivyātmane dharāyai namaḥ," and so on. Beginning from the little toe of the right foot up to the great toe, one should place the pentad beginning with smell. On the left and right thighs, in order, manas and ahaṅkāra. On the left and right arms, one should place Avyakta and Mahat. From Puruṣa up to Śakti one should place on the heart. The reverse of this is the creation-order: namely, from Śakti up to Puruṣa on the heart; Avyakta, Mahat, ahaṅkāra and manas in order on the right and left arms and thighs; from the thumb of the right hand up to the little finger one should place the pentad beginning with hearing; from the (thumb of the) left hand up to the little finger one should place the pentad beginning with speech and ending with the generative organ; from the great toe of the right foot to the little toe, the pentad beginning with sound; from the great toe of the left foot to the little toe one should place the pentad beginning with ether — such is the creation-nyāsa. From earth up to Puruṣa is the maintenance-nyāsa. The creation–maintenance and dissolution–maintenance nyāsa belongs to householders; the dissolution–maintenance and creation–dissolution nyāsa belongs to ascetics — this is to be gathered from another āgama. As they say: "This nyāsa, ending with dissolution, is enjoined for the mendicant Vaikhānasas; ending with maintenance, for the householders; ending with creation, for the (celibate) students" — so they declare. Here the purport is only this: that by touching the several limbs with the mantra of the several deities, one comes to have a body purified by the mantras, since it is said "pierced through by the mantra-forms" — not that "those gods reside in him, the ignorant one," for that would conflict with other authorities. — such is the purport. (153)
Chapter 3 · verse 138
3.138
ऋषिच्छन्दोदेवताङ्गध्यानानुक्तौ प्रधानवत्।
ज्ञेया मन्त्राः समस्तास्ते पृथङ् मोक्षादिदायकाः॥ १३८॥
ṛṣicchandodevatāṅgadhyānānuktau pradhānavat|
jñeyā mantrāḥ samastāste pṛthaṅ mokṣādidāyakāḥ||
138||
Exact English meaningWhere the ṛṣi, metre, deity, limbs and meditation are not (separately) stated, they are to be known as with the principal (mantra); all those mantras, severally, are givers of liberation and the rest.
Word and compound explanation
"Ṛṣiś chandaḥ": "as with the principal one" — as with the mūla-mantra. (154)

एतद्ग्रन्थमहत्त्ववर्णम्

Chapter 3 · verses 139–141
3.139
तन्त्रमार्गास्तु हरिणा ह्यसङ्ख्याः कीर्तिता अपि।
तेष्वयं सुगमो मार्गः सुफलश्चानुतिष्ठताम्॥ १३९॥
tantramārgāstu hariṇā hyasaṅkhyāḥ kīrtitā api|
teṣvayaṃ sugamo mārgaḥ suphalaścānutiṣṭhatām||
139||
3.140
यावतो ह्यननुष्ठाने कर्मपूर्तिर्न विद्यते।
तावत् समस्तं कथितमस्मिंस्तन्त्रे यथाविधि॥ १४०॥
yāvato hyananuṣṭhāne karmapūrtirna vidyate|
tāvat samastaṃ kathitamasmiṃstantre yathāvidhi||
140||
3.141
प्रीयतेऽनेन मार्गेण पूजितो मुक्तिदो भवेत्।
कामदश्च स्वभक्तानां भगवान् पुरुषोत्तमः॥ १४१॥
prīyate’nena mārgeṇa pūjito muktido bhavet|
kāmadaśca svabhaktānāṃ bhagavān puruṣottamaḥ||
141||
Exact combined meaningThe paths of Tantra, though innumerable, have been proclaimed by Hari; among them this is an easy path, and fruitful for those who follow it. For however much, in the non-performance (of which), the completion of the rite would not obtain — all that has been stated in this Tantra according to rule. He is pleased by that path; worshipped (thereby), He becomes the giver of liberation and the fulfiller of the desires of His own devotees — the Lord, Puruṣottama.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"Yāvataḥ": as much as, in the non-doing of which, the completeness (with all limbs) of the rite would not come about — that much has been stated here in this Tantra. Hence there is no fault even in performing (rites) stated in other Tantras not opposed to this one; but in not doing what is here stated there is no completion of the rite — such is the purport. (154–157)

Chapter 4चतुर्थोऽध्यायः

द्वासप्ततिमन्त्राः

Chapter 4 · verse 1
4.1
अथ विष्णूदिते तन्त्रसारे मन्त्रगणो हि यः।
उदितः सङ्ग्रहेणासावुच्यतेऽखिलसाधकः॥ १॥
atha viṣṇūdite tantrasāre mantragaṇo hi yaḥ|
uditaḥ saṅgraheṇāsāvucyate’khilasādhakaḥ||
1||
Exact English meaningNow the host of mantras that was declared in the Tantrasāra spoken by Viṣṇu — that host, accomplishing all (ends), is here stated in summary.
Word and compound explanation
He begins to state the extraction of the mantras and the rest: "atha." "Saṅgraheṇa" — in brief. (1)
Chapter 4 · verses 2–3
4.2
याष्टमस्तूयुतोऽन्तेन समेतश्चैव भूभृतः।
बीजं नमश्च भगवानुद्देश्यो रूपसंयुतः॥ २॥
yāṣṭamastūyuto’ntena sametaścaiva bhūbhṛtaḥ|
bījaṃ namaśca bhagavānuddeśyo rūpasaṃyutaḥ||
2||
4.3
स एव तादृशो लोकत्रयपालो भुवःपतेः।
भावं मे देहि दान्ते तु दापयस्वाहया युतः॥ ३॥
sa eva tādṛśo lokatrayapālo bhuvaḥpateḥ|
bhāvaṃ me dehi dānte tu dāpayasvāhayā yutaḥ||
3||
Exact combined meaningThe eighth (of the ya-series, i.e. ha), joined with u, and united with the ending (bindu), is the bīja of the Boar (Varāha); then 'namaḥ,' and 'bhagavān' with the object (in the dative) and joined with (the phrase about) His form; that same one, of such kind, 'protector of the three worlds,' 'of the Lord of the earth,' 'give me the state of' — and at the end 'dāpaya,' joined with 'svāhā.'

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He extracts the Varāha-mantra: "yāṣṭamaḥ." The eighth letter from ya is ha. "Uyuta" — joined with u. At the end united with the bindu (ṁ). (This, "huṁ," is) the bīja of the Bhūbhṛt, the Boar. After it, the word "namaḥ"; "bhagavān" — the word bhagavat, as the object, with a dative ending, joined with (the mention of) His form; that same "varāha" word likewise with a dative ending — "varāharūpāya"; "protector of the three worlds" also with a dative ending. And thus the mantra is: "Oṁ huṁ namo bhagavate varāharūpāya bhūrbhuvaḥsvaḥpataye bhūpatitvaṁ me dehi dadāpaya svāhā." This has thirty-three syllables. (2–3)
Chapter 4 · verse 4
4.4
मनुस्तस्यापरस्तारबीजोद्देशनमोयुतः ।
नमः सोद्देशभगवान् महाशब्दयुतश्च सः।
तादृशः स्वाहया युक्तो वराहस्यापरो मनुः॥ ४॥
manustasyāparastārabījoddeśanamoyutaḥ |
namaḥ soddeśabhagavān mahāśabdayutaśca saḥ|
tādṛśaḥ svāhayā yukto varāhasyāparo manuḥ||
4||
Exact English meaningAnother mantra of his (Varāha) is joined with the name-bīja, the object (in the dative), and 'namaḥ'; 'namaḥ,' 'bhagavān' with the object, and joined with the word 'mahā' — that one too, of such kind, joined with 'svāhā,' is another mantra of the Boar.
Word and compound explanation
He states another Varāha-mantra of seven syllables: "manus tasyāparaḥ." The name-bīja is the aforesaid (huṁ). The "uddeśa" is the dative. Joined with the word "namaḥ." At the beginning the bīja, then the word "varāha" with a dative ending. And thus the seven-syllable mantra is "huṁ varāhāya namaḥ." He states another mantra: at the beginning the word "namaḥ"; then the word "bhagavat" with a dative ending; that Varāha joined with the word "mahā"; of such kind, with the object; at the end joined with the word "svāhā." And thus "namo bhagavate mahāvarāhāya svāhā" is the third Varāha-mantra, of fourteen syllables. (4)
Chapter 4 · verse 5
4.5
श्यामः सुदर्शनदराभयसद्वरेतो भूम्या युतोऽखिलनिजोक्तपरिग्रहैश्च।
ध्येयो निजैश्च तनुभिः सकलैरुपेतः कोलो हरिः सकलवाञ्छितसिद्धयेऽजः॥ ५॥
śyāmaḥ sudarśanadarābhayasadvareto bhūmyā yuto’khilanijoktaparigrahaiśca|
dhyeyo nijaiśca tanubhiḥ sakalairupetaḥ kolo hariḥ sakalavāñchitasiddhaye’jaḥ||
5||
Exact English meaningDark-hued, holding the Sudarśana (discus), the conch, the gesture of fearlessness, and the boon-giving (hand), together with Bhūmī (Earth) and with all His aforesaid attendants, is to be meditated upon — attended by all His own embodied forms — the Boar, Hari, the Unborn, for the accomplishment of all that is desired.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation of all three aforesaid mantras: "śyāmaḥ." "Holding the Sudarśana, the conch, fearlessness and the boon" — four-armed, with arms bearing the discus, the conch, the abhaya-mudrā and the vara-mudrā. (5)
Chapter 4 · verse 6
4.6
वैष्णवानां मुनिर्ब्रह्मा मन्त्राणां वर्णभेदतः।
ज्ञेयं छन्दो दैवतैकस्तत्तद्रूपो हरिः परः॥ ६॥
vaiṣṇavānāṃ munirbrahmā mantrāṇāṃ varṇabhedataḥ|
jñeyaṃ chando daivataikastattadrūpo hariḥ paraḥ||
6||
Exact English meaningOf the Vaiṣṇava mantras the sage (ṛṣi) is Brahmā; the metre is to be known according to the number of their letters; and the sole deity is the supreme Hari in His respective form.
Word and compound explanation
He states the ṛṣi and the rest common to all the mantras: "vaiṣṇavānām." (6)
Chapter 4 · verse 7
4.7
प्रणवेन स्वनाम्ना च भूधरेण परात्मना।
सर्वज्ञसर्वशक्तिभ्यां षडङ्गानि विदो विदुः॥ ७॥
praṇavena svanāmnā ca bhūdhareṇa parātmanā|
sarvajñasarvaśaktibhyāṃ ṣaḍaṅgāni vido viduḥ||
7||
Exact English meaningWith the praṇava, with His own name, with 'bhūdhara' (the Earth-bearer), with 'parātman,' and with 'sarvajña' and 'sarvaśakti' — the knowers know the six limbs.
Word and compound explanation
He states the six limbs: "praṇavena." (7)
Chapter 4 · verse 8
4.8
ज्ञानैश्वर्यप्रभानन्दतेजःशक्तिभिरेव वा।
पूर्णात्ममध्यगैः सर्ववैष्णवाङ्गान्यथो विदुः॥ ८॥
jñānaiśvaryaprabhānandatejaḥśaktibhireva vā|
pūrṇātmamadhyagaiḥ sarvavaiṣṇavāṅgānyatho viduḥ||
8||
Exact English meaningAnd with (the terms) knowledge, lordship, radiance, bliss, splendor and power — placed between (the words) 'pūrṇa' and 'ātman' — they know the limbs of all the Vaiṣṇava (mantras).
Word and compound explanation
He states the limbs common to all the mantras: "jñānaiśvarya." Placed between the word "pūrṇa" and the word "ātman": "pūrṇajñānātmane namaḥ," and so on. In the mantras whose limbs are not (separately) stated, these very are to be understood as the limbs. (8)
Chapter 4 · verse 9
4.9
तिष्ठोदादिः स्वजायायाः कारणं स्वाहया युतः।
श्रीकरोऽष्टार्णको मन्त्रो गरुडारूढसंस्मृतिः॥ ९॥
tiṣṭhodādiḥ svajāyāyāḥ kāraṇaṃ svāhayā yutaḥ|
śrīkaro’ṣṭārṇako mantro garuḍārūḍhasaṃsmṛtiḥ||
9||
Exact English meaning'tiṣṭha,' preceded by 'ud,' the 'kāraṇa' (kara) of His own consort Śrī, joined with 'svāhā' — the eight-syllable mantra 'Śrīkara,' (whose deity is) remembered as mounted on Garuḍa.
Word and compound explanation
He states the Śrīkara-mantra: the word "tiṣṭha," (preceded) by "ud" — "uttiṣṭha." Of His own consort, the word "śrī"; the "kāraṇa," the word "kara"; joined with the word "svāhā." And thus "uttiṣṭha śrīkara svāhā" is this eight-syllable mantra; (its deity) is to be meditated as mounted on Garuḍa. (9)
Chapter 4 · verse 10
4.10
द्विर्भीषय त्रासय च प्रमर्दय तथैव च।
प्रध्वंसयाथो रक्षेति पञ्चाङ्गश्चेदुदाहृतः॥ १०॥
dvirbhīṣaya trāsaya ca pramardaya tathaiva ca|
pradhvaṃsayātho rakṣeti pañcāṅgaścedudāhṛtaḥ||
10||
Exact English meaningThe five limbs are declared as: ‘bhīṣaya’ twice, ‘trāsaya,’ ‘pramardaya,’ then ‘pradhvaṃsaya,’ and ‘rakṣa.’
Chapter 4 · verse 11
4.11
आदित्यचन्द्रावपि चक्रशङ्खौ शङ्खप्रसूनाख्यनिधी दधानः।
चिन्त्यः कराभ्यां वसु तर्पयन् नः स्वाङ्कस्थलक्ष्मीर्गरुडांससंस्थः॥ क्रूरं वीरं बृहद्विष्णुं दीप्यन्तं विश्वतोमुखम्।
पुंमृगेन्द्रं भयकरं शुभं मृत्योश्च मारकम्।
नमामि स्वयमित्येष द्वात्रिंशार्णो मनुर्हरेः॥ ११॥
ādityacandrāvapi cakraśaṅkhau śaṅkhaprasūnākhyanidhī dadhānaḥ|
cintyaḥ karābhyāṃ vasu tarpayan naḥ svāṅkasthalakṣmīrgaruḍāṃsasaṃsthaḥ||
krūraṃ vīraṃ bṛhadviṣṇuṃ dīpyantaṃ viśvatomukham|
puṃmṛgendraṃ bhayakaraṃ śubhaṃ mṛtyośca mārakam|
namāmi svayamityeṣa dvātriṃśārṇo manurhareḥ||
11||
Exact English meaningBearing the Sun and Moon, the discus and conch, and the treasures named Śaṅkha and Padma, He is to be contemplated as satisfying us with wealth through His hands, with Lakṣmī seated on His lap and Himself stationed on Garuḍa’s shoulder. ‘The fierce, the heroic, the mighty Viṣṇu, blazing and facing every direction, the man-lion, causing fear, auspicious, the slayer even of Death—I myself bow to Him’: this is Hari’s mantra of thirty-two syllables.
Chapter 4 · verse 12
4.12
द्विर्जयस्योपरि निजं सोद्देशं सर्ववित् तथा।
महातेजोबलं वीर्यं तादृशं स्वाहया युतम्।
चतुर्विंशाक्षरो मन्त्रो निखिलेष्टप्रदायकः॥ १२॥
dvirjayasyopari nijaṃ soddeśaṃ sarvavit tathā|
mahātejobalaṃ vīryaṃ tādṛśaṃ svāhayā yutam|
caturviṃśākṣaro mantro nikhileṣṭapradāyakaḥ||
12||
Exact English meaning'jaya' twice, after it His own (name) with the object (in the dative), and 'sarvavid,' likewise 'mahātejobala,' 'vīrya' — such (a mantra), joined with 'svāhā,' is a mantra of twenty-four syllables, the bestower of all that is desired.
Word and compound explanation
He states another mantra of that same (Nṛsiṁha): "dvir jayasya." Of the two "jaya"s too; with the object, ending in the dative; at the end joined with the word "svāhā." This being so, "jaya jaya narasiṁhāya sarvajñāya mahātejobalavīryāya svāhā" is this mantra of twenty-four syllables. (12)
Chapter 4 · verse 13
4.13
विद्महेधीमहेपूर्वं स्वयं चैव महाबलः।
प्रेरयेत्तन्नइत्येतन्मध्येऽनन्तः प्रकीर्तितः॥ १३॥
vidmahedhīmahepūrvaṃ svayaṃ caiva mahābalaḥ|
prerayettannaityetanmadhye’nantaḥ prakīrtitaḥ||
13||
Exact English meaning'vidmahe,' 'dhīmahi'; before (each) His own (name), and 'mahābala'; 'prerayet,' 'tan naḥ' — in the middle of this, Ananta (the endless one) is proclaimed.
Word and compound explanation
He states the Nṛsiṁha-gāyatrī: "vidmahe." Before "vidmahe," with His own (name): "nṛsiṁha" with a dative ending; before "dhīmahi," the word "mahābala" with a dative ending; "prerayet naḥ." Between those two words (tat and naḥ), the word "ananta." And thus "narasiṁhāya vidmahe mahābalāya dhīmahi, tan no'nantaḥ pracodayāt" is the Nṛsiṁha-gāyatrī mantra of twenty-four syllables. (13)
Chapter 4 · verse 14
4.14
पादैर्व्यस्तैः समस्तैश्च पदैश्चाङ्गं प्रकीर्तितम्।
पूर्वयोस्तत्र पूर्वं तु सर्वज्ञेन षडङ्गकम्।
तृतीयं तु द्विरावृत्तैः पादैरङ्गसमन्वितम्॥ १४॥
pādairvyastaiḥ samastaiśca padaiścāṅgaṃ prakīrtitam|
pūrvayostatra pūrvaṃ tu sarvajñena ṣaḍaṅgakam|
tṛtīyaṃ tu dvirāvṛttaiḥ pādairaṅgasamanvitam||
14||
Exact English meaningWith the pādas (quarters) taken severally and jointly, and with the pādas, the limbs are proclaimed; of the first two (mantras), for the first, the six limbs are with 'sarvajña'; but the third is furnished with limbs by the pādas repeated twice.
Word and compound explanation
He states the limb-nyāsa: "pādaiḥ." Of the three mantras, for the first two, the five limbs are with the pādas taken severally and jointly; the sixth limb is with the word "sarvajña" in the dative. In the thirty-two-syllable mantra, the third — in the form of the Gāyatrī — has its six limbs by the three pādas repeated twice. (14)
Chapter 4 · verse 15
4.15
ध्यायेन्नृसिंहमुरुवृत्तरवित्रिनेत्रं जानुप्रसक्तकरयुग्ममथापराभ्याम्।
चक्रं दरं च दधतं प्रियया समेतं तिग्मांशुकोट्यधिकतेजसमग्र्यशक्तिम्॥ १५॥
dhyāyennṛsiṃhamuruvṛttaravitrinetraṃ jānuprasaktakarayugmamathāparābhyām|
cakraṃ daraṃ ca dadhataṃ priyayā sametaṃ tigmāṃśukoṭyadhikatejasamagryaśaktim||
15||
Exact English meaningOne should meditate on Nṛsiṁha, three-eyed with large round sun-(like) eyes, one pair of hands resting on His knees, and with the other two bearing the discus and the conch, together with His beloved, of splendor exceeding ten million fierce-rayed (suns), of foremost power.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation of all three mantras: "nṛsiṁham." Him whose three eyes — large, round, luminous like the sun — shine forth. (15)
Chapter 4 · verses 16–17
4.16
सर्वेष्वपि हि मन्त्रेषु द्व्याद्यनन्तान्तबाहुकः।
चेतनान्तस्स्थमारभ्य सर्वगान्तस्वरूपवान्॥ १६॥
sarveṣvapi hi mantreṣu dvyādyanantāntabāhukaḥ|
cetanāntassthamārabhya sarvagāntasvarūpavān||
16||
4.17
अनन्तरूपो ध्येयोऽत्र स्वगुरूक्तानुसारतः।
यथायोग्यं यथाशक्ति तत्तदायुधभूषणः॥ १७॥
anantarūpo dhyeyo’tra svagurūktānusārataḥ|
yathāyogyaṃ yathāśakti tattadāyudhabhūṣaṇaḥ||
17||
Exact combined meaningIn all the mantras (Viṣṇu) has arms (numbering) from two up to endless; beginning as the one dwelling within the sentient (soul), He is of the form abiding within all the pervading (elements); He is to be meditated upon here as of endless forms, according to the teaching of one's own guru, as is fitting and according to one's capacity, bearing the several weapons and ornaments.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the special meditation in all the Vaiṣṇava mantras: "sarveṣu." He whose arms are from two up to numberless in count; beginning from the one dwelling within the sentient — the Inner Ruler of the jīva — He whose form dwells within all the pervading (elements); He whose forms are endless; He who bears those several weapons and ornaments. (16–17)
Chapter 4 · verses 18–19
4.18
चक्रशङ्खगदापद्मखड्गखेटाः सशार्ङ्गकाः।
शरो मुसलवज्रौ च हलः पाशाङ्कुशौ तथा॥ १८॥
cakraśaṅkhagadāpadmakhaḍgakheṭāḥ saśārṅgakāḥ|
śaro musalavajrau ca halaḥ pāśāṅkuśau tathā||
18||
4.19
शूलं च भिण्डिपालश्च पट्टिशोऽग्निर्वराभये।
तर्कमुद्राऽक्षमाला च पुस्तकं च विदारणम्।
इत्यादीन्यखिलान्येव विष्णोश्चिन्त्यानि बाहुषु॥ १९॥
śūlaṃ ca bhiṇḍipālaśca paṭṭiśo’gnirvarābhaye|
tarkamudrā’kṣamālā ca pustakaṃ ca vidāraṇam|
ityādīnyakhilānyeva viṣṇościntyāni bāhuṣu||
19||
Exact combined meaningconch, discus, mace, lotus, sword and shield, together with the Śārṅga (bow); arrow, pestle and thunderbolt, and plough, noose and goad; trident, and bhiṇḍipāla (a sling-weapon), and paṭṭiśa (spear), fire, boon and fearlessness; the tarka-mudrā, the rosary, the book, and the (weapon of) tearing — all such, without exception, are to be contemplated in Viṣṇu's hands.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the weapons: "śaṅkhacakra." The bhiṇḍipāla and the paṭṭiśa are particular weapons. The tarka-mudrā is the jñāna-mudrā. "Vidāraṇa" is the axe, or another weapon. (18–19)
Chapter 4 · verses 20–23
4.20
वियत् स इति युग्मार्णो विपरीतश्च स स्मृतः।
स सर्गहीनो वेतश्च बिन्दुहीनोत्तरस्तथा॥ २०॥
viyat sa iti yugmārṇo viparītaśca sa smṛtaḥ|
sa sargahīno vetaśca binduhīnottarastathā||
20||
4.21
त्रय एते महामन्त्राः पुरुषार्थचतुष्टये।
कल्पवृक्षाः प्रिया विष्णोर्विशेषाज्ज्ञानदायकाः।
तैरेव तु द्विरावृत्तैरङ्गमेषां प्रकीर्तितम्॥ २१॥
traya ete mahāmantrāḥ puruṣārthacatuṣṭaye|
kalpavṛkṣāḥ priyā viṣṇorviśeṣājjñānadāyakāḥ|
taireva tu dvirāvṛttairaṅgameṣāṃ prakīrtitam||
21||
4.22
श्वासरूपो जपो नित्यमुभयोर्विद्वदज्ञयोः।
एकविंशत्सहस्रात्मा सषट्शतमहर्निशम्।
अर्पणीयो हरौ नित्यं प्रातर्योगो महानयम्॥ २२॥
śvāsarūpo japo nityamubhayorvidvadajñayoḥ|
ekaviṃśatsahasrātmā saṣaṭśatamaharniśam|
arpaṇīyo harau nityaṃ prātaryogo mahānayam||
22||
4.23
अशेषदोषदहनस्तत्त्वज्ञानप्रदायकः।
अष्टैश्वर्यप्रदश्चैव कृतादौ समुपासताम्।
तारयोगोऽप्येवमेव ब्रह्मादावेव वर्तते॥ २३॥
aśeṣadoṣadahanastattvajñānapradāyakaḥ|
aṣṭaiśvaryapradaścaiva kṛtādau samupāsatām|
tārayogo’pyevameva brahmādāveva vartate||
23||
Exact combined meaning'viyat' and 'sa' — the two-lettered (mantra 'haṁsa'); and its reverse is (also) held (as a mantra, 'so'ham'); that same, deprived of the visarga, and 'va,' and the latter without the bindu (i.e. 'sa vā ha' ... 'svāhā'-form) likewise. These three are great mantras, wish-fulfilling trees for the fourfold aim of human life, dear to Viṣṇu, givers of special knowledge; by those very (mantras) repeated twice, the limbs of these are proclaimed. The japa (of the haṁsa) is in the form of breath, constant, for both the wise and the ignorant. (The haṁsa-japa,) of the nature of twenty-one thousand (and) six hundred (breaths) day and night, is ever to be offered to Hari; this great morning yoga is the burner of all faults, the giver of knowledge of the tattvas, and the bestower of the eight lordly powers, for those who devoutly practice it from the Kṛta age onward; and the conjoining with the tāra (praṇava) likewise obtains only with Brahmā and the rest.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the Haṁsa-mantra: "viyat sa." "Viyat" is ha with a bindu; immediately after it, sa. And thus "haṁsa" is one mantra. That same, "reversed" — stated in inverse order — is "so'ham," the second mantra. "Deprived of the visarga" (so'ham → so'ha), joined with sa and with va, and the latter ha lengthened without the bindu — and thus "svāhā" is the third mantra. By those very mantras repeated twice, the six limbs of these (are formed). This japa, in the form of the breath, is common to the learned and the ignorant. (20–22) They amount to twenty-one thousand six hundred day and night. And that (japa) is to be offered to Viṣṇu; the "yoga" is the conjunction with the praṇava. That too is one mantra; therein only the deities Brahmā and the rest are entitled. (23–24)
Chapter 4 · verse 24
4.24
ध्यायेद् रवीन्दुकरमिन्दुसहस्रलक्षकान्तिं प्रियासहितमास्थितमिन्दुबिम्बे।
शङ्खारिदोर्द्वयमुदर्कमहेन्दुबिम्बात् संसिच्यमानममृतेन रमाधिनाथम्॥ २४॥
dhyāyed ravīndukaramindusahasralakṣakāntiṃ priyāsahitamāsthitamindubimbe|
śaṅkhāridordvayamudarkamahendubimbāt saṃsicyamānamamṛtena ramādhinātham||
24||
Exact English meaningOne should meditate on the Lord of Ramā, sun-and-moon-handed, of a radiance like a hundred thousand moons, together with His beloved, seated in the orb of the moon, His two arms (bearing) conch and discus, being sprinkled with nectar from the glorious great moon-orb above.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "dhyāyet." (25)
Chapter 4 · verses 25–26
4.25
अन्त्यस्य रक्तवर्णो वा ध्येयो विष्णुः सनातनः।
विद्युद्वर्णोऽथ वा ज्ञेयः शक्त्येतः पञ्चमन्त्रयुक्॥ २५॥
antyasya raktavarṇo vā dhyeyo viṣṇuḥ sanātanaḥ|
vidyudvarṇo’tha vā jñeyaḥ śaktyetaḥ pañcamantrayuk||
25||
4.26
अष्टाक्षरो महामन्त्रस्तैरेवाङ्गैः समन्वितः।
अन्त्यध्यानयुतश्चैव निश्शेषपुरुषार्थदः॥ २६॥
aṣṭākṣaro mahāmantrastairevāṅgaiḥ samanvitaḥ|
antyadhyānayutaścaiva niśśeṣapuruṣārthadaḥ||
26||
Exact combined meaningFor the last (svāhā-mantra), the eternal Viṣṇu is to be meditated as red-hued, or else as lightning-hued; joined with the fifth mantra (i.e. with 'hrīṁ'), it is the eight-syllable great mantra, furnished with those same limbs, and joined with the last meditation — the giver of every human aim.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
For the last, the svāhā-mantra, (Viṣṇu) is to be meditated as red-hued or lightning-hued. "Śaktyetaḥ" — joined with the Śakti-(bīja), the syllable hrīṁ. With the praṇava, it is the aforesaid fifth mantra. That too is to be known as worshipped by the gods. At the beginning the praṇava, then "hrīṁ," then the aforesaid six syllables. And thus "Oṁ hrīṁ haṁsaḥ so'haṁ svāhā" is this eight-syllable great mantra, furnished with the aforesaid limbs, and joined with the last meditation — the meditation of the lightning-hued (form). (26–27)
Chapter 4 · verses 27–28
4.27
मूलं नः प्रतिषेधश्च सोद्देशो भगवानपि।
विष्णुर्भोज्याधिपः स्वाहायुक्तोऽष्टादशवर्णकः॥ २७॥
mūlaṃ naḥ pratiṣedhaśca soddeśo bhagavānapi|
viṣṇurbhojyādhipaḥ svāhāyukto’ṣṭādaśavarṇakaḥ||
27||
4.28
अङ्गानि तत्पदैरेव मन्त्रचिन्तामणिः स्वयम्।
यथेष्टभक्ष्यभोज्यादिदाता मुक्तिप्रदायकः॥ २८॥
aṅgāni tatpadaireva mantracintāmaṇiḥ svayam|
yatheṣṭabhakṣyabhojyādidātā muktipradāyakaḥ||
28||
Exact combined meaningThe mūla (na-kāra), 'naḥ,' and the negation (ma-kāra), with the object (in the dative); 'bhagavān' also, 'viṣṇu,' 'bhojyādhipa,' joined with 'svāhā' — of eighteen letters; its limbs are by its very words; it is itself the wish-granting gem among mantras, the giver of desired food and dishes and the rest, and the bestower of liberation.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states another mantra, of the Lord of food: "mūlam." "The mūla, naḥ" is the syllable na; the "negation" is the syllable ma. ( ) then the word "bhagavat" with a dative ending; "viṣṇu" with a dative ending; "bhojyādhipa" — the word "annādhipati" with a dative ending, joined with "svāhā." And thus "Oṁ namo bhagavate viṣṇave annādhipataye svāhā" is the eighteen-letter Mantra-cintāmaṇi. Its six limbs are by its six mantra-words. (28–29)
Chapter 4 · verse 29
4.29
ध्यायेत् सुशुक्लमरविन्ददलायताक्षं सौवर्णपात्रदधिभोज्यमथामृतं च।
दोर्भ्यां दधानमखिलैश्च सुरैः परीतं शीतांशुमण्डलगतं रमया समेतम्॥ २९॥
dhyāyet suśuklamaravindadalāyatākṣaṃ sauvarṇapātradadhibhojyamathāmṛtaṃ ca|
dorbhyāṃ dadhānamakhilaiśca suraiḥ parītaṃ śītāṃśumaṇḍalagataṃ ramayā sametam||
29||
Exact English meaningOne should meditate (on Him) as very white, with eyes long as lotus-petals, bearing in His two hands a golden vessel of curds and other food, and nectar, surrounded by all the gods, seated in the orb of the cool-rayed (moon), together with Ramā.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "dhyāyet." (30)
Chapter 4 · verse 30
4.30
अमृतं स्वयमुद्देशयुक्तः सहृदयो मनुः।
सतारोऽष्टाक्षरोऽङ्गानि पदैर्व्यस्तैः समस्तकैः॥ ३०॥
amṛtaṃ svayamuddeśayuktaḥ sahṛdayo manuḥ|
satāro’ṣṭākṣaro’ṅgāni padairvyastaiḥ samastakaiḥ||
30||
Exact English meaning'amṛtaṁ' (i.e. vaṁ), His own (name Vāmana) with the object (in the dative), joined with the hṛdaya (namaḥ) — this mantra, with the tāra (praṇava), is of eight syllables; its limbs are by its words taken severally and jointly.
Word and compound explanation
He states the Vāmana-mantra: "amṛtam." "Amṛta" is the letter va; joined with the bindu (vaṁ). His own (name) "Vāmana," with the object, ending in the dative. "Sahṛdaya" — with "namaḥ." "Sa (praṇava)" — joined with the praṇava at the beginning. And thus "Oṁ vaṁ vāmanāya namaḥ" is this eight-syllable mantra; its limbs are by its words taken severally and jointly. (31)
Chapter 4 · verse 31
4.31
उद्यद्रविप्रभमरीन्द्रदरौ गदां च ज्ञानं च बिभ्रतमजं प्रियया समेतम्।
विश्वावकाशमभितः प्रतिपूरयन्तं भासा स्वया स्मरत विष्णुमजादिवन्द्यम्॥ ३१॥
udyadraviprabhamarīndradarau gadāṃ ca jñānaṃ ca bibhratamajaṃ priyayā sametam|
viśvāvakāśamabhitaḥ pratipūrayantaṃ bhāsā svayā smarata viṣṇumajādivandyam||
31||
Exact English meaningRemember Viṣṇu, radiant as the rising sun, bearing the discus and the conch, the mace and (the gesture of) knowledge, the Unborn, together with His beloved, filling all the space of the universe round about with His own splendor, worthy of the homage of the Unborn (Brahmā) and the rest.
Word and compound explanation
The meditation is "udyadraviprabham." In "amṛtaṁ svayam," the word "amṛta" with the object, "sva" joined with the word "ātman" — thus another mantra, "Oṁ amṛtātmane namaḥ," is also expounded. Of this one the limbs are by its words repeated twice; the meditation is the same as stated. (32)
Chapter 4 · verse 32
4.32
त्रिविक्रमश्च सोद्देशो विश्वरूपश्च तत्परः।
धीविदौ च महे विष्णुस्तन्नःप्रेरणमध्यगः॥ ३२॥
trivikramaśca soddeśo viśvarūpaśca tatparaḥ|
dhīvidau ca mahe viṣṇustannaḥpreraṇamadhyagaḥ||
32||
Exact English meaning'trivikrama' with the object (in the dative), and 'viśvarūpa' after it, and 'dhī' and 'vid' (i.e. dhīmahi, vidmahe), and 'mahe'; 'viṣṇu,' 'tan naḥ,' with 'prerayet' (pracodayāt) in between.
Word and compound explanation
He states the Trivikrama-gāyatrī: "trivikramas tu." With the object, ending in the dative: after "trivikrama," "vidmahe"; after "viśvarūpa," "dhīmahi"; then "tan no viṣṇuḥ pracodayāt" is to be joined. And thus "trivikramāya vidmahe viśvarūpāya dhīmahi, tan no viṣṇuḥ pracodayāt" is the Gāyatrī-mantra of twenty-four syllables. (33)
Chapter 4 · verse 33
4.33
स्वयमुद्देशसंयुक्तस्तदाद्यर्णपुरस्सरः।
सनतिः षडक्षरो मन्त्रो वर्णैरङ्गक्रिया मता॥ ३३॥
svayamuddeśasaṃyuktastadādyarṇapurassaraḥ|
sanatiḥ ṣaḍakṣaro mantro varṇairaṅgakriyā matā||
33||
Exact English meaningHis own (name) joined with the object (in the dative), preceded by its first letter, with 'namaḥ' (nati) — this is a six-syllable mantra; the performance of its limbs is held to be by its letters.
Word and compound explanation
He states the mantra of Paraśurāma and of Rāma the son of Daśaratha: "svayam." His own (name) "Rāma," ending in the dative; its first letter is "rā(m)." That before which stands the "nati," the word "namaḥ," joined with it. And thus "rāṁ rāmāya namaḥ" is the six-syllable mantra; its six limbs are by its letters. (34)
Chapter 4 · verse 34
4.34
अङ्गारवर्णमभितोऽण्डबहिःप्रभाभिर्व्याप्तं परश्वधधनुर्धरमेकवीरम्।
ध्यायेदजेशपुरुहूतमुखैः स्तुवद्भिरावीतमात्मपदवीं प्रतिपादयन्तम्॥ ३४॥
aṅgāravarṇamabhito’ṇḍabahiḥprabhābhirvyāptaṃ paraśvadhadhanurdharamekavīram|
dhyāyedajeśapuruhūtamukhaiḥ stuvadbhirāvītamātmapadavīṃ pratipādayantam||
34||
Exact English meaningOne should meditate on (Paraśurāma), fire-hued, spreading light on every side beyond the (cosmic) egg, bearing the axe and the bow, the sole hero, surrounded by the Unborn (Brahmā), Īśa, Puruhūta (Indra) and the others who praise Him, teaching (them) the path to the Self.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "aṅgāravarṇam." (35)
Chapter 4 · verse 35
4.35
श्यामं रवीन्द्वमितदीधितिकान्तियुक्तं ज्ञानं शरं च दधतं प्रियया समेतम्।
स्वात्मस्वरूपममितं हनुमन्मुखेषु सन्दर्शयन्तमजितं स्मरतोरुगीर्भिः॥ ३५॥
śyāmaṃ ravīndvamitadīdhitikāntiyuktaṃ jñānaṃ śaraṃ ca dadhataṃ priyayā sametam|

35||
Exact English meaning(One should meditate on Rāma,) dark-hued, endowed with a radiance measureless as (that of) the sun and moon, bearing (the gesture of) knowledge and the arrow, together with His beloved, revealing His own boundless essential nature to Hanumat and the others (who) remember (Him) with mighty hymns, the unconquered one.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation of Rāghava: "śyāmam." (36)
Chapter 4 · verse 36
4.36
निजगोत्रं तु सोद्देशं पूर्वाक्षरपुरस्सरम्।
नत्यन्तं तारपूर्वं च मनुरष्टाक्षरो मतः।
ते च बीजे बीजभूते धर्मादीनामशेषतः॥ ३६॥
nijagotraṃ tu soddeśaṃ pūrvākṣarapurassaram|
natyantaṃ tārapūrvaṃ ca manuraṣṭākṣaro mataḥ|
te ca bīje bījabhūte dharmādīnāmaśeṣataḥ||
36||
Exact English meaningHis own gotra-(name Bhārgava), with the object (in the dative), preceded by its first letter, ending in 'namaḥ' (nati), and preceded by the tāra (praṇava) — this is held to be an eight-syllable mantra; and those two bījas are the seed-forms of Dharma and the rest, without exception.
Word and compound explanation
He states another mantra of Paraśurāma: "nijagotram." His own gotra — the word "bhārgava," with the object, ending in the dative; that which has its first letter "bhā(m)" preceding it at the beginning, of Bhārgava; and the word "rāghava," of Rāghava, ending in "nati," ending in "namaḥ," preceded by the praṇava. And thus "Oṁ bhāṁ bhārgavāya namaḥ" is this eight-syllable mantra. "Those two bījas": "rāṁ" and "bhāṁ" — these two. "The seed-forms" — the seed-forms of Dharma and the rest. And the mantra "rāṁ rāghavāya namaḥ." (37)
Chapter 4 · verses 37–38
4.37
एते विजयदा मन्त्रा ज्ञानमोक्षप्रदायकाः।
हिरण्यरत्नराज्यादिसमभीष्टसुरद्रुमाः॥ ३७॥
ete vijayadā mantrā jñānamokṣapradāyakāḥ|
hiraṇyaratnarājyādisamabhīṣṭasuradrumāḥ||
37||
4.38
कृष्णो गोविच्च कामेतो सोद्देशो वल्लवीजनः।
प्रियश्च तादृशः स्वाहायुगष्टादशवर्णकः।
पदैरङ्गानि सम्प्रीतिकाममोक्षप्रदो मनुः॥ ३८॥
kṛṣṇo govicca kāmeto soddeśo vallavījanaḥ|
priyaśca tādṛśaḥ svāhāyugaṣṭādaśavarṇakaḥ|
padairaṅgāni samprītikāmamokṣaprado manuḥ||
38||
Exact combined meaningThese mantras are givers of victory, bestowers of knowledge and liberation, wish-granting trees for gold, gems, kingdom and other desired things. 'Kṛṣṇa,' 'govid,' 'kāma,' with the object (in the dative), 'vallavījana,' and 'priya' of such kind, joined with 'svāhā' — of eighteen letters. ( its limbs are by its words; this mantra is the giver of loving affection, of desire (fulfilled), and of liberation.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the eighteen-syllable mantra of Śrī Kṛṣṇa: "kṛṣṇa." "Kṛṣṇa" ending in the dative; "govinda" too likewise; and "gopījanavallabha" of like kind; at the beginning furnished with the Kāma-bīja, "klīṁ." This eighteen-syllable one is the Śrī Kṛṣṇa great mantra. And thus "klīṁ kṛṣṇāya govindāya gopījanavallabhāya svāhā" is the form of the mantra. Its five limbs are thus by its words. (39)
Chapter 4 · verse 39
4.39
ध्यायेद्धरिन्मणिनिभं जगदेकवन्द्यं सौन्दर्यसारमरिशङ्खवराभयानि।
दोर्भिर्दधानमजितं सरसं च भैष्मीसत्यासमेतमखिलप्रदमिन्दिरेशम्॥ ३९॥
dhyāyeddharinmaṇinibhaṃ jagadekavandyaṃ saundaryasāramariśaṅkhavarābhayāni|

Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "dhyāyet." (40)
Chapter 4 · verse 40
4.40
सकामः स्वयमुद्देशी नत्यन्तोऽयं षडक्षरः।

Exact English meaningWith the Kāma-(bīja), His own (name) with the object, ending in 'namaḥ' (nati) — this is a six-syllable mantra; and that (mantra) beginning with it (the Kāma-bīja) is also declared the wish-gem for all desires.
Word and compound explanation
He states another six-syllable mantra: "sakāmaḥ." "Sakāma" — at the beginning joined with the Kāma-bīja; His own (name) "Kṛṣṇa" ending in the dative; "natyanta" — ending in "namaḥ." Chapter 4 · verse 41
4.41
दृष्टार्थ एव मन्त्राणां कलौ वीर्यं तिरस्कृतम्।
तत्राप्युद्दीप्तवीर्या हि मन्त्रा अत्र प्रकीर्तिताः।
वासिष्ठवृष्णिप्रवरा मन्त्रास्तत्रापि वीर्यदाः॥ ४१॥
dṛṣṭārtha eva mantrāṇāṃ kalau vīryaṃ tiraskṛtam|
tatrāpyuddīptavīryā hi mantrā atra prakīrtitāḥ|
vāsiṣṭhavṛṣṇipravarā mantrāstatrāpi vīryadāḥ||
41||
Exact English meaningIn the Kali age the potency of the mantras (with respect to) the perceptible aim (only) is set aside; yet even there, the mantras here proclaimed are of undiminished potency; and among them the mantras of the Vāsiṣṭha (Vyāsa) and of the foremost of the Vṛṣṇis (Kṛṣṇa) are (especially) bestowers of potency.
Word and compound explanation
"Dṛṣṭārtha" — concerning the perceptible aim. "Vāsiṣṭha" is Vyāsa; "vṛṣṇipravara" is Śrī Kṛṣṇa. (42)
Chapter 4 · verses 42–43
4.42
स्वयं वेदपदारूढः पूर्ववर्णपुरस्सरः।
नत्यन्तोऽष्टाक्षरो मन्त्रः प्रियो विज्ञानगोपतेः॥ ४२॥
svayaṃ vedapadārūḍhaḥ pūrvavarṇapurassaraḥ|
natyanto’ṣṭākṣaro mantraḥ priyo vijñānagopateḥ||
42||
4.43
व्याख्याश्रीसर्वविज्ञानकवितादिगुणप्रदः।
वादे विजयदो नित्यं यथा युद्धे नृकेसरी॥ ४३॥
vyākhyāśrīsarvavijñānakavitādiguṇapradaḥ|
vāde vijayado nityaṃ yathā yuddhe nṛkesarī||
43||
Exact combined meaningHis own (name) mounted on the word 'veda,' preceded by its first letter, ending in 'namaḥ' (nati) — an eight-syllable mantra, dear to the Lord of Vijñāna (Vyāsa); the giver of the qualities of exposition, glory, all knowledge, poetic skill and the rest; ever a bestower of victory in debate, as Nṛkesarī (Nṛsiṁha) is in battle.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the Śrī Vedavyāsa-mantra: "mounted on the word veda" — His own (name) "vyāsa" ending in the dative; the first letter in the word "vyāsa," this letter "vyā" ending in the bindu, the syllable "vyā(ṁ)," is what precedes it, that mantra of which it stands at the head; "natyanta" — ending in "namaḥ." And thus "vyāṁ vedavyāsāya namaḥ" is this eight-syllable mantra. "Of the Lord of Vijñāna" — of Vyāsa, the sun of vijñāna (discerning knowledge). (43–44)
Chapter 4 · verse 44
4.44
विज्ञानरोचिःपरिपूरितान्तर्बाह्याण्डकोशं हरितोपलाभम्।
तर्काभयेतं विधिशर्वपूर्वगीर्वाणविज्ञानदमानतोऽस्मि॥ ४४॥
vijñānarociḥparipūritāntarbāhyāṇḍakośaṃ haritopalābham|
tarkābhayetaṃ vidhiśarvapūrvagīrvāṇavijñānadamānato’smi||
44||
Exact English meaningI bow to Him whose interior fills the shell of the cosmic egg with the light of knowledge, hued like an emerald, bearing (the gestures of) reasoning and fearlessness, the giver of discerning knowledge to the gods, Vidhi (Brahmā), Śarva (Śiva) and the rest.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "vijñānarociḥ." "Tarkābhayeta" — with the pair of hands bearing the jñāna-mudrā and the abhaya-mudrā. (45)
Chapter 4 · verse 45
4.45
ज्ञानानन्दपुरःपूर्णो विद्महे धीमहे तथा।
तन्नःप्रेरणमध्ये तु व्यासो मन्त्राधिपाधिपः॥ ४५॥
jñānānandapuraḥpūrṇo vidmahe dhīmahe tathā|
tannaḥpreraṇamadhye tu vyāso mantrādhipādhipaḥ||
45||
Exact English meaning'jñāna' and 'ānanda' preceded by 'pūrṇa,' (with) 'vidmahe' and likewise 'dhīmahi'; 'tan naḥ' with 'prerayet' (pracodayāt) in the middle — (this is the Gāyatrī of) Vyāsa, the sovereign of the sovereigns of mantras.
Word and compound explanation
He states the Vyāsa-gāyatrī: "jñānānanda." (The word) "pūrṇa," at the head of which stand "jñāna" and "ānanda," ending in the dative. After the word "pūrṇajñāna," "vidmahe"; after "pūrṇānanda," "dhīmahi" is to be joined. Between the words "tan naḥ" and "pracodayāt," the word "vyāsaḥ" in the nominative singular is to be joined. And thus "pūrṇajñānāya vidmahe pūrṇānandāya dhīmahi, tan no vyāsaḥ pracodayāt" is the twenty-four-syllable Gāyatrī-mantra, the very best of the best of mantras. (46)
Chapter 4 · verse 46
4.46
आदिबीजं स्थिराशेषज्ञानबीजं विमुक्तिदम्।
सर्वपापक्षयकरं सर्वव्याधिविनाशनम्॥ ४६॥
ādibījaṃ sthirāśeṣajñānabījaṃ vimuktidam|
sarvapāpakṣayakaraṃ sarvavyādhivināśanam||
46||
Exact English meaningThe first bīja (vyāṁ) is the seed of firm and complete knowledge, the giver of liberation, the destroyer of all sins, the annihilator of all diseases.
Word and compound explanation
"The first bīja" — "vyāṁ." The means to firm and faultless knowledge; the remover of all sins; the remover of all diseases. And thus "vyāṁ" is this one-syllable Vyāsa mantra. (47)
Chapter 4 · verse 47
4.47
ध्यायेच्छशाङ्कशतकोट्यतिसौख्यकान्तिं संसिच्यमानममृतोरुघटैः सुरेशैः।
वर्णाभिमानिभिरजेशमुखैः सहैव पञ्चाशता प्रतिगिरन्तमशेषविद्याः॥ ४७॥
dhyāyecchaśāṅkaśatakoṭyatisaukhyakāntiṃ saṃsicyamānamamṛtorughaṭaiḥ sureśaiḥ|
varṇābhimānibhirajeśamukhaiḥ sahaiva pañcāśatā pratigirantamaśeṣavidyāḥ||
47||
Exact English meaningOne should meditate (on Vyāsa), of a radiance surpassingly blissful (as of) a hundred crore moons, being sprinkled with great pots of nectar by the lords of the gods, together with the fifty (deities) presiding over the letters, headed by the Unborn (Brahmā) and Īśa, uttering in response all the branches of knowledge.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "dhyāyet." Him whose radiance, surpassingly blissful, exceeds that of a hundred crore moons. "With those presiding over the letters" — Aja and the rest. (48)
Chapter 4 · verse 48
4.48
सोद्देशस्तु स्वयं दीर्घपूर्वार्णाद्यो नमोयुतः।
सर्वविद्याप्रदोऽष्टार्णः प्रतिवादिजयप्रदः।
विमुक्तिसाधनः कीर्तिबुद्धिस्थैर्यप्रदः सदा॥ ४८॥
soddeśastu svayaṃ dīrghapūrvārṇādyo namoyutaḥ|
sarvavidyāprado’ṣṭārṇaḥ prativādijayapradaḥ|
vimuktisādhanaḥ kīrtibuddhisthairyapradaḥ sadā||
48||
Exact English meaningHis own (name Hayagrīva) with the object (in the dative), beginning with its first letter lengthened, joined with 'namaḥ' — an eight-syllable (mantra), the giver of all knowledge, the bestower of victory over opponents, the means to liberation, ever the giver of fame, intelligence and steadfastness.
Word and compound explanation
He states the eight-syllable Hayagrīva-mantra: "soddeśa." His own (name) "Hayagrīva," with the object, ending in the dative; that mantra whose first (element) is ha with the first letter lengthened and ending in the bindu (hāṁ); joined with "namaḥ" — ending in the word "namaḥ." And thus "hāṁ hayagrīvāya namaḥ" is this eight-syllable mantra. (49)
Chapter 4 · verses 49–50
4.49
वन्दे तुरङ्गवदनं शशिबिम्बसंस्थं चन्द्रावदातममृतात्मकरैः समन्तात्।
अण्डान्तरं बहिरपि प्रतिभासयन्तं शङ्खाक्षपुस्तकसुबोधयुताब्जबाहुम्॥ ४९॥
vande turaṅgavadanaṃ śaśibimbasaṃsthaṃ candrāvadātamamṛtātmakaraiḥ samantāt|
aṇḍāntaraṃ bahirapi pratibhāsayantaṃ śaṅkhākṣapustakasubodhayutābjabāhum||
49||
4.50
नस्तो मुखादपि निरन्तरमुद्गिरन्तं विद्या अशेषत उताब्जभवेशमुख्यैः।
संसेव्यमानमतिभक्तिभराभिनुन्नैर्लक्ष्म्याऽमृतेन सततं परिषिच्यमानम्॥ ५०॥
nasto mukhādapi nirantaramudgirantaṃ vidyā aśeṣata utābjabhaveśamukhyaiḥ|
saṃsevyamānamatibhaktibharābhinunnairlakṣmyā’mṛtena satataṃ pariṣicyamānam||
50||
Exact combined meaningI bow to Him of the horse-face, seated in the orb of the moon, white as the moon, all around with nectar-natured rays, illumining the interior and the exterior of the (cosmic) egg, His lotus-hands (bearing) conch, rosary, book and (the gesture of) good understanding, uttering ceaselessly from His nose and mouth too all the branches of knowledge without exception, being served by (the deities) headed by the Unborn (Brahmā) and Īśa, bowed low with the fullness of great devotion, ever sprinkled by Lakṣmī with nectar.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "vande turaṅgavadanam." By the two (verses); ending with the nose (of the horse-faced one). (50–51)
Chapter 4 · verse 51
4.51
स्वयमुद्देशवान् पूर्ववर्णपूर्वो नमोयुतः।
सतारोऽष्टाक्षरश्चैव नवार्णश्च मनू स्मृतौ॥ ५१॥
svayamuddeśavān pūrvavarṇapūrvo namoyutaḥ|
satāro’ṣṭākṣaraścaiva navārṇaśca manū smṛtau||
51||
Exact English meaningHis own (name) with the object (in the dative), preceded by its first letter, joined with 'namaḥ,' with the tāra (praṇava) — an eight-syllable and a nine-syllable mantra are recorded.
Word and compound explanation
He states the mantras of Kapila and Dattātreya in a single verse: "svayam." His own (names), the words "kapila" and "dattātreya," with the object, ending in the dative; those preceded by their first letters with the bindu — "ka(ṁ)" and "da(ṁ)"; ending in the word "namaḥ"; with the praṇava. And thus "Oṁ kaṁ kapilāya namaḥ" and "Oṁ daṁ dattātreyāya namaḥ" are the two mantras. (52)
Chapter 4 · verse 52
4.52
प्रोद्यद्दिवाकरसमानतनुं सहस्रसूर्योरुदीधितिभिराप्तसमस्तलोकम्।
ज्ञानाभयाङ्कितकरं कपिलं च दत्तं ध्यायेदजादिसमितिं प्रतिबोधयन्तम्॥ ५२॥
prodyaddivākarasamānatanuṃ sahasrasūryorudīdhitibhirāptasamastalokam|
jñānābhayāṅkitakaraṃ kapilaṃ ca dattaṃ dhyāyedajādisamitiṃ pratibodhayantam||
52||
Exact English meaningOne should meditate on Kapila and on Datta (Dattātreya), of a body like the rising sun, filling all the worlds with the great rays of a thousand suns, His hands bearing (the gestures of) knowledge and fearlessness, awakening the assembly (of gods) headed by the Unborn (Brahmā).
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation of both at once: "prodyad-divākara." "Ajādi-samiti" — the assemblage of the gods, Brahmā and the rest. (53)
Chapter 4 · verses 53–54
4.53
अधृष्यताज्ञानमोक्षप्रदौ भक्तेष्विमौ सदा।
सूक्तं दीर्घतमोदृष्टं विष्णोर्नुकमिति प्रभोः।
सर्वार्थदं गार्त्समदं यो जात इति चापरम्॥ ५३॥
adhṛṣyatājñānamokṣapradau bhakteṣvimau sadā|
sūktaṃ dīrghatamodṛṣṭaṃ viṣṇornukamiti prabhoḥ|
sarvārthadaṃ gārtsamadaṃ yo jāta iti cāparam||
53||
4.54
वासिष्ठं च परो मात्रयेति ज्ञानविमुक्तिदम्।
भौवनीयं सर्वकाममोक्षदं य इमेत्यपि।
एवमेवाखिला वेदा ज्ञातव्या विष्णुतत्पराः॥ ५४॥
vāsiṣṭhaṃ ca paro mātrayeti jñānavimuktidam|
bhauvanīyaṃ sarvakāmamokṣadaṃ ya imetyapi|
evamevākhilā vedā jñātavyā viṣṇutatparāḥ||
54||
Exact combined meaningThese two are ever the givers, to their devotees, of invincibility, knowledge and liberation. The sūkta 'viṣṇor nu kam,' seen by (the ṛṣi) Dīrghatamas, of the Lord (Viṣṇu), the giver of all aims; and another, the 'gārtsamada' beginning 'yo jāta(ḥ)'; and the Vāsiṣṭha one beginning 'paro mātrayā,' the giver of knowledge and liberation. and the 'bhauvanīya' beginning 'ya ime,' the giver of all desires and of liberation. In just this way all the Vedas are to be known as directed toward Viṣṇu.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"Seen by Dīrghatamas" — seen by the ṛṣi Dīrghatamas. Of the Lord Viṣṇu. The "gārtsamada" sūkta — taught by the sage Gṛtsamada; the sūkta beginning "yo jāta(ḥ)." The "bhauvanīya," seen by Vasiṣṭha — the Viśvakarman sūkta. (54–56)
Chapter 4 · verse 55
4.55
स्वदृष्टं सूक्तमखिलकामदं हरितुष्टिदम्।
वाचोऽम्भ्रण्याः श्रियोऽङ्गानि श्रीलक्ष्मीर्मेन्दिरा रमा॥ ५५॥
svadṛṣṭaṃ sūktamakhilakāmadaṃ harituṣṭidam|
vāco’mbhraṇyāḥ śriyo’ṅgāni śrīlakṣmīrmendirā ramā||
55||
Exact English meaningThe sūkta seen by herself (Śrī), the giver of all desires and of the pleasure of Hari — the sūkta of Vāc, daughter of Ambhṛṇa, of Śrī; her limbs are (formed with) Śrī, Lakṣmī, (Ma-)Indirā, Ramā.
Word and compound explanation
"Seen by herself" — seen by Lakṣmī. The sūkta of Vāc — the speech-goddess named Ambhṛṇī — of Śrī. "Śrī, Lakṣmī, (Ma-)Indirā, Ramā" — its five limbs. "śriye — to the heart, namaḥ," and so on. (57)
Chapter 4 · verse 56
4.56
कौशेयपीतवसनामरविन्दनेत्रां पद्मद्वयाभयवरोद्यतपद्महस्ताम्।
उद्यच्छतार्कसदृशीं परमाङ्कसंस्थां ध्यायेद् विधीशनुतपादयुगां जनित्रीम्॥ ५६॥
kauśeyapītavasanāmaravindanetrāṃ padmadvayābhayavarodyatapadmahastām|
udyacchatārkasadṛśīṃ paramāṅkasaṃsthāṃ dhyāyed vidhīśanutapādayugāṃ janitrīm||
56||
Exact English meaningOne should meditate on the Mother (of the world), clad in yellow silk, lotus-eyed, her lotus-hands raised in (holding) two lotuses, boon and fearlessness, resembling a hundred rising suns, seated on the lap of the Supreme, her feet praised by Vidhi (Brahmā) and Īśa.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "kauśeyapīta." (58)
Chapter 4 · verse 57
4.57
लज्जाबीजं च तद्बीजं तस्या एवाभिधायकम्।
पाशाङ्कुशौ रक्तवस्त्रं लज्जाबीजे विशिष्यते।
अमध्ययुग्मस्वरगतेनैवाङ्गमुदाहृतम्॥ ५७॥
lajjābījaṃ ca tadbījaṃ tasyā evābhidhāyakam|
pāśāṅkuśau raktavastraṃ lajjābīje viśiṣyate|
amadhyayugmasvaragatenaivāṅgamudāhṛtam||
57||
Exact English meaningThe Lajjā-bīja (hrīṁ) is her bīja, the very designator of her; the noose, the goad and the red garment are the special features in (the meditation of) the Lajjā-bīja; (that syllable) formed with the pair of vowels excluding the middle one (i.e. ṛ-ḷ combined as the vowel of hrīṁ) — by that very (bīja) the limb is declared.
Word and compound explanation
He states the one-syllable mantra of Lakṣmī: "lajjābījam." Lajjā is "hrīḥ" (modesty); her bīja is "hrīṁ." Of that Śrī, the bīja "śrīṁ" — thus two mantras. The very designator — the expressor — of that Lakṣmī. In the Ambhṛṇī mantra she was said to be "clad in yellow silk"; but in the mantra called Lajjā-bīja she is to be meditated as red-garmented, and in place of boon and fearlessness the noose and goad are to be meditated — such is the distinction. "Amadhya" — excluding the two middle vowels ṛ and ḷ (which one should avoid); with the vowel ending in a long (ī) and bindu — by the syllable "hrīṁ" itself the six limbs (are formed): "hāṁ — to the heart, namaḥ; hrīṁ — to the head, svāhā; hūṁ — to the tuft, vauṣaṭ; haiṁ — to the armor, hum; hrauṁ — to the two eyes, vauṣaṭ; haḥ — to the weapon, phaṭ" — such is the manner of the limb-nyāsa. (59)
Chapter 4 · verse 58
4.58
सत्ताबीजं च दुर्बीजं तद्वदेव प्रकीर्तितम्।
वर्णः श्यामो विशेषोऽत्र ताम्बूलं नीलमुत्पलम्।
चक्रशङ्खौ तर्जनं च शूलमित्यपरत्र च॥ ५८॥
sattābījaṃ ca durbījaṃ tadvadeva prakīrtitam|
varṇaḥ śyāmo viśeṣo’tra tāmbūlaṃ nīlamutpalam|
cakraśaṅkhau tarjanaṃ ca śūlamityaparatra ca||
58||
Exact English meaningAnd the Sattā-bīja and the Dur-bīja — that very (syllable, i.e. the mantra of Durgā/Bhū) is proclaimed. The color is dark — that is the special feature here; the betel, and the blue lotus; and in the other case (Durgā): the discus and conch, the threatening (gesture), and the trident.
Word and compound explanation
He states another mantra: "sattā." The Sattā-bīja is "bhūḥ." The Dur-bīja is "duḥ" — the bīja of Durgā is "duḥ." With those, two mantras (bhūḥ and duḥ). In the earlier (case) it was said "resembling a hundred rising suns"; but here (for Bhū) a dark color is to be meditated; and in place of boon and fearlessness, the betel and the blue lotus are to be meditated — such is the distinction in the bīja. In the other, the Durgā-bīja, a dark color; and in the four arms, in order, the discus and conch, the threatening gesture, and the trident are to be meditated — such is the distinction. (60–61)
Chapter 4 · verse 59
4.59
नमोन्तः स्वेन सहितस्तदेवान्यो मनुः स्मृतः।
दुर्गात्रिष्टुप् कश्यपोक्ता तत्र वर्णोऽग्निवत् स्मृतः॥ ५९॥
namontaḥ svena sahitastadevānyo manuḥ smṛtaḥ|
durgātriṣṭup kaśyapoktā tatra varṇo’gnivat smṛtaḥ||
59||
Exact English meaningEnding in 'namaḥ,' joined with its own (bīja) — that same is recorded as another mantra; Durgā (as deity), Triṣṭubh (as metre), Kaśyapa (as ṛṣi) declared; therein the color is recorded as fire-like.
Word and compound explanation
He states another mantra: "namo'ntas svena." That which ends in "namaḥ," whose own (bīja is prefixed) — of the word "durgā" with a dative ending: joined with it, the Durgā-bīja. And thus "duḥ durgāyai namaḥ" is another six-syllable mantra. Here the deity Śrī named Durgā is to be known. Durgā (deity); Triṣṭubh (metre) — "jātavedase sunavāma soma," etc.; "kaśyapoktā" — the ṛṣi here is Kaśyapa. There Durgā is to be meditated as fire-hued. (62)
Chapter 4 · verse 60
4.60
त्रिनेत्रत्वं च दुर्गायाः प्रायः सर्वत्र कथ्यते।
पादैः समस्तेन तथा षडर्णेनाङ्गमुच्यते॥ ६०॥
trinetratvaṃ ca durgāyāḥ prāyaḥ sarvatra kathyate|
pādaiḥ samastena tathā ṣaḍarṇenāṅgamucyate||
60||
Exact English meaningAnd the three-eyedness of Durgā is stated almost everywhere; by the pādas taken together, and by the six-lettered (mantra), the limb is spoken.
Word and compound explanation
Everywhere, in all the Durgā-mantras, the limbs are to be placed by the pādas taken severally and jointly. By the six-lettered Durgā-mantra the six limbs are to be placed. (63)
Chapter 4 · verse 61
4.61
रमाया एव रूपाणि त्वेतानि हि विदो विदुः।
मुख्यतो जामदग्न्यस्तु देवताऽस्य मनोः स्मृतः॥ ६१॥
ramāyā eva rūpāṇi tvetāni hi vido viduḥ|
mukhyato jāmadagnyastu devatā’sya manoḥ smṛtaḥ||
61||
Exact English meaningThe knowers know that these (Bhū and Durgā) are indeed forms of Ramā herself; but principally Jāmadagnya (Paraśurāma?) — (rather Jātavedas, i.e. Agni-Viṣṇu) — is recorded as the deity of this mantra.
Word and compound explanation
The knowers know that these — named Śrī, Bhū and Durgā — are forms of Ramā herself. But principally, of the mantra "jātavedase," the deity is Jāmadagnya himself. (64)
Chapter 4 · verse 62
4.62
हिरण्यगर्भसूक्तं च भृगुदृष्टं प्रकीर्तितम्।
ब्रह्मधातृविरिञ्चाजपाद्मैरङ्गमुदाहृतम्॥ ६२॥
hiraṇyagarbhasūktaṃ ca bhṛgudṛṣṭaṃ prakīrtitam|
brahmadhātṛviriñcājapādmairaṅgamudāhṛtam||
62||
Exact English meaningAnd the Hiraṇyagarbha-sūkta, seen by Bhṛgu, is proclaimed; its limb is declared by (the names) Brahmā, Dhātṛ, Viriñca, Aja and the rest.
Word and compound explanation
"And the Hiraṇyagarbha-sūkta": "hiraṇyagarbhaḥ samavartatāgre," etc. — seen by Bhṛgu, it is a mantra of Brahmā. Its limbs are (formed) with (the names) Brahmā, Dhātṛ, Viriñca, Aja and the rest. (65)
Chapter 4 · verse 63
4.63
ध्यायेन्निषण्णमजमच्युतनाभिपद्मे प्रोद्यद्दिवाकरसमूहनिकाशमग्र्यम्।
मातृप्रकारकरमुत्तमकान्तिमद्भिर्वक्त्रैः सृजन्तमखिलैः परमार्थविद्याम्॥ ६३॥
dhyāyenniṣaṇṇamajamacyutanābhipadme prodyaddivākarasamūhanikāśamagryam|
mātṛprakārakaramuttamakāntimadbhirvaktraiḥ sṛjantamakhilaiḥ paramārthavidyām||
63||
Exact English meaningOne should meditate on the Unborn (Brahmā), seated on the navel-lotus of Acyuta, foremost, resembling a mass of rising suns, his hands (raised) in the manner of the Mother (Lakṣmī), of supreme radiance, an ocean, bringing forth with all his faces the knowledge of the highest truth.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "dhyāyet." "Mātṛprakāra" — he whose hands resemble the hands of Ramā, in the manner stated, "(bearing) two lotuses, fearlessness," etc. Or the sense is: whose radiance is in the manner of the Mother, resembling a hundred rising suns. Or: he whose hands are in the manner of fashioning, in the manner of creating. (66)
Chapter 4 · verse 64
4.64
स्वयमुद्देशनतिमांस्तस्य पञ्चाक्षरो मनुः।
स्वयमेवापरोऽष्टार्णस्तादृशः सम्प्रकीर्तितः॥ ६४॥
svayamuddeśanatimāṃstasya pañcākṣaro manuḥ|
svayamevāparo’ṣṭārṇastādṛśaḥ samprakīrtitaḥ||
64||
Exact English meaningHis own (name) with the object (in the dative) and 'namaḥ' (nati) — this is his five-syllable mantra; and another, likewise His own, of eight syllables, of the same kind, is proclaimed.
Word and compound explanation
His own (name), the word "brahma," with the object and the nati; ending in the dative, joined with the word "namaḥ" — five syllables. Likewise "Hiraṇyagarbha" of the same kind — with the dative, of eight syllables. And thus "brahmaṇe namaḥ" and "hiraṇyagarbhāya namaḥ" are the five-syllable and eight-syllable (mantras). (67)
Chapter 4 · verse 65
4.65
बळाद्यं भृगुणा दृष्टं प्राणाग्नेः सूक्तमुच्यते।
प्राणाद्यैः पञ्चभिस्तस्याप्यङ्गान्युक्तानि सूरिभिः॥ ६५॥
baḷādyaṃ bhṛguṇā dṛṣṭaṃ prāṇāgneḥ sūktamucyate|
prāṇādyaiḥ pañcabhistasyāpyaṅgānyuktāni sūribhiḥ||
65||
Exact English meaningThe sūkta of Prāṇa (Mukhyaprāṇa, i.e. Vāyu), beginning with 'bala,' seen by Bhṛgu, is stated; its limbs too are declared by the wise with the five (words) beginning with 'prāṇa.'
Word and compound explanation
He states the Mukhyaprāṇa mantra: "balādyam." "bali(t)," etc. By the pentad beginning with "prāṇa," the five limbs. (68)
Chapter 4 · verse 66
4.66
उद्यद्रविप्रकरसन्निभमच्युताङ्के स्वासीनमस्य नुतिनित्यवचःप्रवृत्तिम्।
ध्यायेद् गदाभयकरं सुकृताञ्जलिं तं प्राणं यथेष्टतनुमुन्नतकर्मशक्तिम्॥ ६६॥
udyadraviprakarasannibhamacyutāṅke svāsīnamasya nutinityavacaḥpravṛttim|
dhyāyed gadābhayakaraṃ sukṛtāñjaliṃ taṃ prāṇaṃ yatheṣṭatanumunnatakarmaśaktim||
66||
Exact English meaningOne should meditate on Prāṇa (Vāyu), resembling the rays of the rising sun, well-seated at the foot of Acyuta, ever engaged in speech that is His praise, his hands (bearing) boon and fearlessness, well-folded in añjali, of forms at will, of exalted power of action.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "udyadravi." (69)
Chapter 4 · verse 67
4.67
प्राण्याद्याः पञ्च मन्त्राश्च तस्य देवस्य वाचकाः।
उच्यते हरिरप्येतैः साक्षान्नारायणादिकः॥ ६७॥
prāṇyādyāḥ pañca mantrāśca tasya devasya vācakāḥ|
ucyate harirapyetaiḥ sākṣānnārāyaṇādikaḥ||
67||
Exact English meaningAnd the five mantras beginning with 'prāṇa' are the designators of that god (Vāyu); and by these Hari too, directly, is named — Nārāyaṇa and the rest.
Word and compound explanation
"Prāṇādyāḥ" — "prāṇāya namaḥ," "apānāya namaḥ," and so on. By these mantras, principally, Nārāyaṇa Himself directly is named. (70)
Chapter 4 · verse 68
4.68
ब्रह्माण्या अपि भारत्या गौरीर्यस्त इति श्रुतौ।
मन्त्रौ दीर्घतमोदृष्टौ सर्वाभीष्टप्रदायकौ॥ ६८॥
brahmāṇyā api bhāratyā gaurīryasta iti śrutau|
mantrau dīrghatamodṛṣṭau sarvābhīṣṭapradāyakau||
68||
Exact English meaningEven Brahmā and the rest (are surpassed) by Bhāratī (Sarasvatī), (as expressed) in the two śruti-(mantras) 'gaurīḥ' and 'yas te,' seen by Dīrghatamas, the givers of all that is desired.
Word and compound explanation
Of Brahmāṇī and of Bhāratī: "gaurīr mimāya," etc., and "yas te stanaḥ śaśayo yo mayobhūḥ," etc. — these two mantras, seen by Dīrghatamas. (71)
Chapter 4 · verse 69
4.69
उद्यद्दिवाकरसमूहनिभां स्वभर्तुरङ्कस्थितामभयसद्वरबाहुयुग्माम्।
मुद्रां च तत्त्वदृशये वरपुस्तकं च दोर्युग्मकेन दधतीं स्मरतात्मविद्याम्॥ ६९॥
udyaddivākarasamūhanibhāṃ svabharturaṅkasthitāmabhayasadvarabāhuyugmām|
mudrāṃ ca tattvadṛśaye varapustakaṃ ca doryugmakena dadhatīṃ smaratātmavidyām||
69||
Exact English meaningRemember the knowledge-of-the-Self (goddess, Sarasvatī), resembling a mass of rising suns, seated on the lap of her own Lord, one pair of arms (bearing) fearlessness and the boon, and with the other pair of arms bearing the mudrā (of teaching) — for the perceiving of truth — and the boon and the book.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "udyad-divākara." (72)
Chapter 4 · verse 70
4.70
पादैर्व्यस्तैः समस्तैश्च तदङ्गानि विदो विदुः।
स्वयमुद्देशनत्यन्तौ मन्त्रौ द्वावपरौ तयोः॥ ७०॥
pādairvyastaiḥ samastaiśca tadaṅgāni vido viduḥ|
svayamuddeśanatyantau mantrau dvāvaparau tayoḥ||
70||
Exact English meaningBy the pādas taken severally and jointly the knowers know their limbs; and there are two other mantras of those two (goddesses), (formed of) their own (name) with the object and ending in 'namaḥ' (nati).
Word and compound explanation
By the pādas taken severally and jointly the limbs are to be fashioned. He states other mantras of Brahmāṇī and Bhāratī: "svayam." "Brahmā" ending in the dative and in "namaḥ"; "Hiraṇyagarbha" ending in the dative and in "namaḥ"; and of Brahmāṇī and Bhāratī, ending in the dative and in "namaḥ" — thus four mantras: "brahmāṇyai namaḥ," "hiraṇyagarbhāyai namaḥ," "sarasvatyai namaḥ," "bhāratyai namaḥ" — such is the sequence of mantras. "That (masc.) and that (masc.) — the two"; "that (fem.) and that (fem.) — the two"; "of those two (tayoḥ)" — by the (grammatical) rule, the taking together of both the masculine and feminine (deities). (73)
Chapter 4 · verses 71–72
4.71
मन्त्रा एते हरेः प्रीतिदायकाः सर्वकामदाः।
इष्टं दैवं हरिः साक्षाल्लक्ष्मीर्भाग्यस्य देवता।
गुरुर्ब्रह्माऽथ वायुर्वा विद्या देव्यौ प्रकीर्तिते॥ ७१॥
mantrā ete hareḥ prītidāyakāḥ sarvakāmadāḥ|
iṣṭaṃ daivaṃ hariḥ sākṣāllakṣmīrbhāgyasya devatā|
gururbrahmā’tha vāyurvā vidyā devyau prakīrtite||
71||
4.72
तस्मादेते प्रिया विष्णोरन्तरङ्गतया सदा।
ज्ञेयाश्चैव प्रपूज्याश्च विष्णोः प्रीतिमभीप्सता॥ ७२॥
tasmādete priyā viṣṇorantaraṅgatayā sadā|
jñeyāścaiva prapūjyāśca viṣṇoḥ prītimabhīpsatā||
72||
Exact combined meaningThese mantras are givers of Hari's pleasure and of all desires; the desired deity is Hari Himself directly; the deity of the wealth-portion (bhāga) is Lakṣmī; the guru is Brahmā, or Vāyu; the goddesses of learning are proclaimed. Therefore these (deities) are ever dear to Viṣṇu, as being His intimates, and are to be known and worshipped by one desiring the pleasure of Viṣṇu.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"But what use have the Vaiṣṇavas for others?" Anticipating this doubt he speaks the two-and-a-half (verses beginning) "mantrā ete." (74–75)
Chapter 4 · verse 73
4.73
उद्देशनतियुङ्मन्त्रः स्वयमेव शिवस्य तु।
वर्णैरेतैस्तदङ्गानि वामदेवो मुनिः स्मृतः।
पङ्क्तिः शब्दार्थविज्ञाने नहि च्छन्दसि संशयः॥ ७३॥
uddeśanatiyuṅmantraḥ svayameva śivasya tu|
varṇairetaistadaṅgāni vāmadevo muniḥ smṛtaḥ|
paṅktiḥ śabdārthavijñāne nahi cchandasi saṃśayaḥ||
73||
Exact English meaningThe mantra joined with the object and 'namaḥ' (nati), of Śiva himself; its limbs are by these letters; Vāmadeva is recorded as the sage. In understanding the sense of the word 'paṅkti' there is no doubt as to the metre. (77-intro)
Word and compound explanation
He states the five-syllable Śiva-mantra: "śiva" ending in the dative, joined with the "nati," the word "namaḥ." And thus "śivāya namaḥ" is the mantra. Its five limbs by its letters. Vāmadeva (as) ṛṣi, Paṅkti (as) metre. (76)
Chapter 4 · verse 74
4.74
ध्येयः पञ्चमुखो रुद्रः स्फटिकामलकान्तिमान्।
विद्युच्छुभ्रासितरजःश्यामान्यस्य मुखानि तु।
जटावबद्धेन्दुकलः प्रियायुङ् नागभूषणः॥ ७४॥
dhyeyaḥ pañcamukho rudraḥ sphaṭikāmalakāntimān|
vidyucchubhrāsitarajaḥśyāmānyasya mukhāni tu|
jaṭāvabaddhendukalaḥ priyāyuṅ nāgabhūṣaṇaḥ||
74||
Exact English meaningRudra is to be meditated as five-faced, of a radiance pure as crystal; his faces (colored) like lightning, bright, dark-dust-hued, black; with the crescent moon bound in his matted hair, together with his beloved, adorned with serpents.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "pañcamukha." "Vidyuc-chubhrāsita-jaṭaḥ" — his mass of matted hair shining like lightning. (77–78)
Chapter 4 · verse 75
4.75
मन्त्रेष्वेतेषु सर्वेषु वाच्यस्तारेण केशवः।
अन्तर्यामितया ध्येयः सर्वदेवेषु सर्वदा॥ ७५॥
mantreṣveteṣu sarveṣu vācyastāreṇa keśavaḥ|
antaryāmitayā dhyeyaḥ sarvadeveṣu sarvadā||
75||
Exact English meaningIn all these mantras, Keśava is to be named by the tāra (praṇava); as the Inner Ruler in all the gods, He is ever to be meditated upon.
Word and compound explanation
In these mantras of Lakṣmī and the rest, Keśava alone is named by the tāra, the praṇava; as the Inner Ruler in all the gods He is ever to be meditated upon. (79)
Chapter 4 · verse 76
4.76
स एव वनितालिङ्गः पार्वत्या मनुरुच्यते।
गौरी वराभयकरा ध्येया पीताम्बरा च सा॥ ७६॥
sa eva vanitāliṅgaḥ pārvatyā manurucyate|
gaurī varābhayakarā dhyeyā pītāmbarā ca sā||
76||
Exact English meaningThat same (Śiva-mantra) in the feminine gender is called the mantra of Pārvatī; Gaurī is to be meditated as bearing boon and fearlessness in her hands, and clad in yellow.
Word and compound explanation
That same, in the feminine gender — the word "śivā" ending in the dative, ending in "namaḥ" — is the Pārvatī mantra; "śivāyai namaḥ" is its form. He states the meditation: "gaurī." (80)
Chapter 4 · verse 77
4.77
स्वयमुद्देशनत्यन्तः पूर्वपूर्वः षडक्षरः।
सनत्कुमारदृष्टोऽयं मनुः शेषस्य कामदः॥ ७७॥
svayamuddeśanatyantaḥ pūrvapūrvaḥ ṣaḍakṣaraḥ|
sanatkumāradṛṣṭo’yaṃ manuḥ śeṣasya kāmadaḥ||
77||
Exact English meaningHis own (name Śeṣa) with the object, ending in 'namaḥ' (nati), preceded by its first (letter) — a six-syllable mantra, seen by Sanatkumāra, this mantra of Śeṣa, giver of desires.
Word and compound explanation
He states the Śeṣa-mantra: "svayam." The "uddeśa" is the dative; the "nati" is the word "namaḥ," joined with it. His own (name), "Śeṣa"; "pūrvapūrva" — that whose first letter with the bindu (śeṁ) precedes it. And thus "śeṁ śeṣāya namaḥ" is the six-syllable mantra of Śeṣa, seen by Sanatkumāra. (81)
Chapter 4 · verse 78
4.78
दधानो हलसौनन्दौ श्वेतवर्णः कृताञ्जलिः।
सहस्रमूर्धाऽद्वितीयकर्णभूषः प्रियायुतः।
वनमाली नीलवासा ध्येयो विष्णोस्तु पृष्ठतः॥ ७८॥
dadhāno halasaunandau śvetavarṇaḥ kṛtāñjaliḥ|
sahasramūrdhā’dvitīyakarṇabhūṣaḥ priyāyutaḥ|
vanamālī nīlavāsā dhyeyo viṣṇostu pṛṣṭhataḥ||
78||
Exact English meaningBearing the plough and (the mace) Saunanda, white-hued, with folded hands, thousand-headed, with a single ear-ornament, together with his beloved, wearing a wood-land garland, clad in blue — (Śeṣa) is to be meditated behind Viṣṇu.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation: "dadhāna." "Halasaunandau" — the plough and the pestle(-mace). "Advitīyakarṇabhūṣa" — having a single earring. (82)
Chapter 4 · verse 79
4.79
व्यत्यस्तपक्षितारौ च स्वाहान्तो गारुडो मनुः।
काश्यपोऽस्य मुनिर्ध्येयः सुधापूर्णं हिरण्मयम्॥ ७९॥
vyatyastapakṣitārau ca svāhānto gāruḍo manuḥ|
kāśyapo’sya munirdhyeyaḥ sudhāpūrṇaṃ hiraṇmayam||
79||
Exact English meaningThe Paṅkti and the tāra reversed (i.e. 'ṭha ṭha' and the praṇava transposed), ending in 'svāhā' — the Garuḍa-mantra; its sage is Kāśyapa, to be meditated (as bearing) a golden (vessel) full of nectar.
Word and compound explanation
He states the five-syllable Garuḍa-mantra: "vyatyasta." The "paṅkṣi" word (ṭha ṭha, i.e. "kṣipa") reversed, with the praṇava at the beginning, ending in "svāhā" — the Garuḍa-mantra. "Oṁ kṣipa svāhā" is its form. Its sage is Kaśyapa. The meditation is "sudhāpūrṇa(m)." (83)
Chapter 4 · verses 80–81
4.80
दधानः कुम्भमभयं पीतशुक्लारुणो हरिः।
कृष्णश्चाजानुतो नाभेः कण्ठात् कादन्त एव च॥ ८०॥
dadhānaḥ kumbhamabhayaṃ pītaśuklāruṇo hariḥ|
kṛṣṇaścājānuto nābheḥ kaṇṭhāt kādanta eva ca||
80||
4.81
प्रियायुक् पुरतो विष्णोः पक्षतुण्डसमन्वितः।
जङ्गमस्थावरविषहृदयं सर्वकामदः॥ ८१॥
priyāyuk purato viṣṇoḥ pakṣatuṇḍasamanvitaḥ|
jaṅgamasthāvaraviṣahṛdayaṃ sarvakāmadaḥ||
81||
Exact combined meaning(Garuḍa) bearing a pot and (the gesture of) fearlessness, (colored) yellow, white, red — Hari; and black — from the knee, from the navel, from the throat, and even the tooth (i.e. successive regions differently colored); together with his beloved, in front of Viṣṇu, furnished with wings and beak, (his) heart (destroying) the poison of moving and unmoving (creatures), the giver of all desires.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"Ājānutaḥ": from the foot up to the knee, yellow-hued; from there up to the navel, white; from there up to the throat, red; from there up to the head, green-hued; and then on the head, black-hued — such is the meaning. (84–85)
Chapter 4 · verses 82–83
4.82
मन्त्राश्च लोकपालानां नमोन्ताः सर्वकामदाः।
नीलरक्तासितश्वेतरक्तशुक्लाश्च वर्णतः॥ ८२॥
mantrāśca lokapālānāṃ namontāḥ sarvakāmadāḥ|
nīlaraktāsitaśvetaraktaśuklāśca varṇataḥ||
82||
4.83
सप्रियाः साभयाः वज्रशक्तिदण्डासिपाशिनः।
गदाकुमुदशूलेष्टखड्गिनो भूषणोज्वलाः॥ ८३॥
sapriyāḥ sābhayāḥ vajraśaktidaṇḍāsipāśinaḥ|
gadākumudaśūleṣṭakhaḍgino bhūṣaṇojvalāḥ||
83||
Exact combined meaningAnd the mantras of the world-guardians, ending in 'namaḥ,' are givers of all desires; in color blue, red, black, white, red, white; together with their consorts, with fearlessness, bearing the thunderbolt, the śakti (spear), the staff, the sword and the noose, the mace, the lotus, the trident, the boon and the sword, resplendent with ornaments.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
The mantras of the world-guardians, Indra and the rest, ending in "namaḥ": "indrāya namaḥ." Indra and the rest are, in order, of colors beginning with blue.
