Among them, Vāyu, Soma, Īśāna, Brahmā and Śeṣa are white; and they are to be meditated as furnished with the thunderbolt and their other weapons. There: "laṁ indrāya namaḥ," "yaṁ vāyave namaḥ," "saṁ somāya namaḥ," "śaṁ īśānāya namaḥ" — these are the mantras of Indra and the rest. (86–87)
Chapter 4 · verse 84
4.84
अग्निश्च स्वयमुद्देशी सनमः स्कन्दवाचकः।
कौशिकोऽस्य मुनिर्वह्निवर्णः षण्मुख उज्वलः।
धनुःशक्तिधरो ध्येयः कामदो भयनाशनः॥ ८४॥
agniśca svayamuddeśī sanamaḥ skandavācakaḥ|
kauśiko’sya munirvahnivarṇaḥ ṣaṇmukha ujvalaḥ|
dhanuḥśaktidharo dhyeyaḥ kāmado bhayanāśanaḥ||
84||
Exact English meaningAnd 'agni' (the fire-bīja 'raṁ'), His own (name) with the object, with 'namaḥ' — designating Skanda; Kauśika is its sage; (Skanda) is to be meditated as fire-hued, six-faced, blazing, bearing the bow and the śakti (spear), the giver of desires, the destroyer of fear.
Word and compound explanation
He states the Skanda-mantra: "agniś ca." "Agni" is the fire-bīja, "raṁ." The "uddeśa" ends in the dative. His own (name) "Skanda." Ending in "namaḥ." And thus "raṁ skandāya namaḥ" is the six-syllable Skanda-mantra. He states the ṛṣi and the meditation: "kauśika." (88)
Chapter 4 · verse 85
4.85
ह्रस्वः कृपालुः सेतश्च स्वयमन्यः स्वयं तथा।
सतारोऽष्टाक्षरः सौरः क्रमव्युत्क्रमतः पदैः॥ ८५॥
hrasvaḥ kṛpāluḥ setaśca svayamanyaḥ svayaṃ tathā|
satāro’ṣṭākṣaraḥ sauraḥ kramavyutkramataḥ padaiḥ||
85||
Exact English meaningShort, gracious, with 'sa' (the sun-bīja), His own (name), another His own likewise, with the tāra (praṇava) — an eight-syllable solar (mantra); (its limbs) by its words in the direct and reverse order.
Chapter 4 · verse 86
4.86
अङ्गानि पद्मयुगलधरो ध्येयोऽरुणो रविः।
सर्वव्याधिहरः श्रीदो वृष्टिदोऽयं मनुः स्मृतः॥ ८६॥
aṅgāni padmayugaladharo dhyeyo’ruṇo raviḥ|
sarvavyādhiharaḥ śrīdo vṛṣṭido’yaṃ manuḥ smṛtaḥ||
86||
Exact English meaningthe limbs (are formed thus); the Sun is to be meditated as bearing a pair of lotuses, ruddy; this mantra is recorded as the remover of all diseases, the giver of prosperity, the giver of rain.
Word and compound explanation
He states the solar mantra: "hrasva." By the word "kṛpālu" the word "ghṛṇi" is meant. (The word "kṛpālu," the word "ghṛṇi") — if it be short, it becomes "ghṛṇi." "Seta" — with the visarga (sa → saḥ). His own (name) "Sūrya." "His own, another" — "Āditya." With the tāra, joined with the praṇava at the beginning. And thus "Oṁ ghṛṇiḥ sūrya āditya" is the eight-syllable Sūrya-mantra. By the direct and reverse order, the limbs are (formed) by its words. The meditation is "padmayugaḷa," etc. (89–90)
Chapter 4 · verses 87–88
4.87
मोनयदासाप्रप्रक्षि व्यत्यस्तो विघ्नराण्मनुः।
रक्ताम्बरो रक्ततनू रक्तमाल्यानुलेपनः॥ ८७॥
monayadāsāpraprakṣi vyatyasto vighnarāṇmanuḥ|
raktāmbaro raktatanū raktamālyānulepanaḥ||
87||
4.88
लम्बोदरो गजमुखः पाशदन्ताङ्कुशाभये।
बिभ्रद् ध्येयो विघ्नहरः कामदस्त्वरया ह्ययम्॥ ८८॥
lambodaro gajamukhaḥ pāśadantāṅkuśābhaye|
bibhrad dhyeyo vighnaharaḥ kāmadastvarayā hyayam||
88||
Exact combined meaning(The syllables) reversed — this is the mantra of the Lord of Obstacles (Gaṇeśa); he is to be meditated red-garmented, red-bodied, with red garland and unguent, pot-bellied, elephant-faced, bearing the noose, the tusk, the goad and (the gesture of) fearlessness — the remover of obstacles, and this (mantra) is a swift giver of desires.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the Gaṇeśa-mantra: "monayadāsāpraprakṣi." Reversed — with the letters inverted — and ending in "namaḥ." And thus "kṣipraprasādāya namaḥ" is the eight-syllable mantra of the King of Obstacles. The meditation is "raktāmbara." (91–92)
Chapter 4 · verse 89
4.89
तत्रतत्र स्थितो विष्णुस्तत्तच्छक्तीः प्रबोधयन्।
एक एव महाशक्तिः कुरुते सर्वमञ्जसा॥ ८९॥
tatratatra sthito viṣṇustattacchaktīḥ prabodhayan|
eka eva mahāśaktiḥ kurute sarvamañjasā||
89||
Exact English meaningViṣṇu, abiding in each (of these deities), awakening their several powers, He alone, of great power, does everything readily.
Word and compound explanation
He states that even in the case of other gods, it is Viṣṇu alone who bestows the fruit: "tatra tatra." (93)
Chapter 4 · verse 90
4.90
स्वयमुद्देशवान् पूर्ववर्णपूर्वो नमोयुतः।
धान्वन्तरो महामन्त्रः संसृतिव्याधिनाशनः।
अत्रोक्तसर्वमन्त्राणां नायकोपलसन्निभः।॥ ९०॥
svayamuddeśavān pūrvavarṇapūrvo namoyutaḥ|
dhānvantaro mahāmantraḥ saṃsṛtivyādhināśanaḥ|
atroktasarvamantrāṇāṃ nāyakopalasannibhaḥ|||
90||
Exact English meaningHis own name, placed in the dative, preceded by its first letter and joined with ‘namaḥ,’ is the great Dhanvantari mantra, destroyer of the disease of transmigration, resembling the foremost jewel among all the mantras taught here.
Chapter 4 · verse 91
4.91
चन्द्रौघकान्तिममितोरुकरैर्जगन्ति संजीवयन्तममितात्मसुखं परेशम्।
ज्ञानं सुधाकलशमेव च सन्दधानं शीतांशुमण्डलगतं स्मरतात्मसंस्थम्॥ ९१॥
candraughakāntimamitorukarairjaganti saṃjīvayantamamitātmasukhaṃ pareśam|
jñānaṃ sudhākalaśameva ca sandadhānaṃ śītāṃśumaṇḍalagataṃ smaratātmasaṃstham||
91||
Exact English meaningRemember the Supreme Lord (as Dhanvantari), of a radiance like a flood of moons, reviving the worlds with His great nectar-rays, of measureless bliss, bearing (the gesture of) knowledge and the pot of nectar, seated in the orb of the cool-rayed (moon), abiding within the Self.
Chapter 4 · verse 92
4.92
मूर्ध्नि स्थितादमुत एव सुधां स्रवन्तीं भ्रूमध्यगाच्च तत एव च तालुसंस्थात्।
हार्दाच्च नाभिसदनादधरस्थिताच्च ध्यात्वाऽभिपूरिततनुर्दुरितं निहन्यात्॥ ९२॥
mūrdhni sthitādamuta eva sudhāṃ sravantīṃ bhrūmadhyagācca tata eva ca tālusaṃsthāt|
hārdācca nābhisadanādadharasthitācca dhyātvā’bhipūritatanurduritaṃ nihanyāt||
92||
Exact English meaningHaving meditated on the nectar streaming from (the moon) situated on the head, and from the same seated between the brows, and from the same abiding in the palate, and in the heart, and in the navel-seat, and in the lower (region), one whose body is thereby filled should destroy sin.
Chapter 4 · verse 93
4.93
अज्ञानदुःखभयरोगमहाविषाणि योगोऽयमाशु विनिहन्ति सुखं च दद्यात्।
उन्मादविभ्रमहरः परतश्च सान्द्रमानन्दमेव पदमापयति स्म नित्यम्॥ ९३॥
ajñānaduḥkhabhayarogamahāviṣāṇi yogo’yamāśu vinihanti sukhaṃ ca dadyāt|
unmādavibhramaharaḥ parataśca sāndramānandameva padamāpayati sma nityam||
93||
Exact English meaningThis yoga swiftly destroys ignorance, sorrow, fear, disease and the great poisons, and gives happiness; the remover of madness and delusion, and beyond (all), it ever leads to the state of dense bliss itself.
Chapter 4 · verse 94
4.94
ध्यात्वैव हस्ततलगं स्वमृतं स्रवन्तं देवं स यस्य शिरसि स्वकरं निधाय।
आवर्तयेन्मनुमिमं स च वीतरोगः पापादपैति मनसा यदि भक्तिनम्रः॥ ९४॥
dhyātvaiva hastatalagaṃ svamṛtaṃ sravantaṃ devaṃ sa yasya śirasi svakaraṃ nidhāya|
āvartayenmanumimaṃ sa ca vītarogaḥ pāpādapaiti manasā yadi bhaktinamraḥ||
94||
Exact English meaningHaving meditated on the God resting on his palm, streaming His own nectar, whoever, placing his own hand on (a sick person's) head, repeats this mantra — that (person) too becomes free of disease and departs from sin, if with his mind he be humble with devotion.
Word and compound explanation
He states the meditation by three (verses): "candraugha." (95–98)
Chapter 4 · verse 95
4.95
शतं सहस्रमयुतं लक्षं वाऽऽरोगसङ्क्षयात्।
इममेव जपेन्मन्त्रं साधूनां दुःखशान्तये॥ ९५॥
śataṃ sahasramayutaṃ lakṣaṃ vā’’rogasaṅkṣayāt|
imameva japenmantraṃ sādhūnāṃ duḥkhaśāntaye||
95||
Exact English meaningA hundred, a thousand, ten thousand, or a hundred thousand (times), for the removal of disease, one should repeat this very mantra for the quelling of the sufferings of good people.
Word and compound explanation
With a view to the removal of disease. (99)
Chapter 4 · verse 96
4.96
ज्वरदाहादिशान्त्यर्थं तर्पयेन्मनुनाऽमुना।
ध्यात्वा हरिं जले सप्तरात्राज्जूतिर्विनश्यति॥ ९६॥
jvaradāhādiśāntyarthaṃ tarpayenmanunā’munā|
dhyātvā hariṃ jale saptarātrājjūtirvinaśyati||
96||
Exact English meaningFor the quelling of fever, burning and the rest, one should offer libations with this mantra; having meditated on Hari in the water, in seven nights the fever is destroyed.
Word and compound explanation
"Jūrti" means fever. (100)
Chapter 4 · verse 97
4.97
अयुतामृतसमिद्धोमाद् गोघृतक्षीरसंयुतात्।
सर्वरोगा विनश्यन्ति विमुखो न हरेर्यदि॥ ९७॥
ayutāmṛtasamiddhomād goghṛtakṣīrasaṃyutāt|
sarvarogā vinaśyanti vimukho na hareryadi||
97||
Exact English meaningBy a homa of ten thousand (oblations) with the amṛtā (guḍūcī), joined with ghee and milk, all diseases are destroyed — provided one be not averse to Hari.
Word and compound explanation
"Ayuta": the amṛtā is the guḍūcī. From a homa with its fuel-sticks, numbering ten thousand. (101)
Chapter 4 · verse 98
4.98
भूताभिचारशान्त्यर्थमपामार्गाहुतिक्रिया।
द्विगुणाऽमृतया पश्चात् केवलेन घृतेन वा॥ ९८॥
bhūtābhicāraśāntyarthamapāmārgāhutikriyā|
dviguṇā’mṛtayā paścāt kevalena ghṛtena vā||
98||
Exact English meaningFor the quelling of (afflictions by) beings and of sorcery, the offering of apāmārga oblations; twofold with the amṛtā (guḍūcī), and afterward with ghee alone.
Chapter 4 · verse 99
4.99
आयुर्विवृद्धये नित्यं जन्मनक्षत्र एव वा।
चतुश्चतुर्भिर्दूर्वाभिः क्षीराज्याक्ताभिरिष्यते॥ ९९॥
āyurvivṛddhaye nityaṃ janmanakṣatra eva vā|
catuścaturbhirdūrvābhiḥ kṣīrājyāktābhiriṣyate||
99||
Exact English meaningFor the increase of longevity, daily, or (especially) on one's birth-star (day), (a homa) with four-and-four dūrvā grasses smeared with milk and ghee is desired.
Chapter 4 · verse 100
4.100
सर्वक्रिया हरौ भक्ते हरिभक्तैः स्वनुष्ठिताः।
गुरुभक्तैः सदाचारैः फलन्त्यद्धा नचान्यथा॥ १००॥
sarvakriyā harau bhakte haribhaktaiḥ svanuṣṭhitāḥ|
gurubhaktaiḥ sadācāraiḥ phalantyaddhā nacānyathā||
100||
Exact English meaningAll rites, well performed toward Hari by devotees of Hari, by devotees of the guru, of good conduct — (bear) fruit truly, and not otherwise.
Word and compound explanation
"Apāmārgāhutikriyā" — the act in the form of a homa with the fuel-sticks of apāmārga (and the northern leaves); twice as much (again) with the amṛtā (guḍūcī) fuel-sticks. (102–104)
Chapter 4 · verses 101–103
4.101
ब्रह्मान्ता गुरवश्चैव सम्प्रदायप्रवर्तकाः।
तत्तद्गुणानुसारेण पूज्या ध्येयाश्च कृत्स्नशः॥ १०१॥
brahmāntā guravaścaiva sampradāyapravartakāḥ|
tattadguṇānusāreṇa pūjyā dhyeyāśca kṛtsnaśaḥ||
101||
4.102
सर्वेभ्योऽप्यधिका भक्तिर्विष्णौ स्यादतिनिश्चला।
लक्ष्मीब्रह्मादिषु ततः क्रमेण स्यात् सुभाविता।
एवं विद्याः फलिष्यन्ति नान्यथा तु कथञ्चन॥ १०२॥
sarvebhyo’pyadhikā bhaktirviṣṇau syādatiniścalā|
lakṣmībrahmādiṣu tataḥ krameṇa syāt subhāvitā|
evaṃ vidyāḥ phaliṣyanti nānyathā tu kathañcana||
102||
4.103
शुभान् ध्यायन्ति ये कामान् गुरुदेवप्रसादजान्।
इतरानात्मपापोत्थांस्तेषां विद्या फलिष्यति॥ १०३॥
śubhān dhyāyanti ye kāmān gurudevaprasādajān|
itarānātmapāpotthāṃsteṣāṃ vidyā phaliṣyati||
103||
Exact combined meaningThe gurus, up to Brahmā, the promoters of the tradition, are to be worshipped and meditated upon entirely, according to their several qualities. Devotion to Viṣṇu should be greater than to all, most firm; then to Lakṣmī, Brahmā and the rest, in order, well cultivated. Thus the mantras (vidyās) will bear fruit, and in no other way whatsoever. For those who meditate on auspicious desires, born of the grace of guru and god, and (avoid) the others born of their own sin, their mantra will bear fruit.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"Brahmāntāḥ": beginning from one's own guru and extending up to Brahmā. (105–107)
Chapter 4 · verses 104–105
4.104
मुक्तावपि च संसारे नात्मनो विष्णुतां क्वचित्।
ब्रह्मरुद्रादिभावं वा साम्यं वाऽऽधिक्यमेव वा॥ १०४॥
muktāvapi ca saṃsāre nātmano viṣṇutāṃ kvacit|
brahmarudrādibhāvaṃ vā sāmyaṃ vā’’dhikyameva vā||
104||
4.105
अतद्वशत्वमथवा यः स्मरेत् स तु सिद्धिभाक्।
नान्यथा सिद्धिमाप्नोति कल्पकोटिशतैरपि॥ १०५॥
atadvaśatvamathavā yaḥ smaret sa tu siddhibhāk|
nānyathā siddhimāpnoti kalpakoṭiśatairapi||
105||
Exact combined meaningHe who, both in liberation and in transmigration, never (imagines) for himself Viṣṇu-hood, or the state of Brahmā, Rudra and the rest, or equality (with them), or superiority, or independence (from Him) — he indeed attains success; not otherwise does one attain success, even in a hundred crore of aeons.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
The construction is: both in liberation and in transmigration, one should not imagine for oneself Viṣṇu-hood and the rest. (108–109)
Chapter 4 · verses 106–108
4.106
स्मृत्वा गुरुं पूर्वगुरुमादिमूलगुरूंस्तथा।
देवतां वासुदेवं च विद्याभ्यासी तु सिद्धिभाक्॥ १०६॥
smṛtvā guruṃ pūrvagurumādimūlagurūṃstathā|
devatāṃ vāsudevaṃ ca vidyābhyāsī tu siddhibhāk||
106||
4.107
अवज्ञाता गुरूणां च देवतानां न सिद्ध्यति।
प्रयाति च तमो घोरं मन्त्रावज्ञाकृदेव च॥ १०७॥
avajñātā gurūṇāṃ ca devatānāṃ na siddhyati|
prayāti ca tamo ghoraṃ mantrāvajñākṛdeva ca||
107||
4.108
यस्तारतम्यवेत्ता स्याद् गुरूणां देवतास्वपि।
भक्तिमान् गुरुदेवेषु तद्भक्तेषु च सिद्ध्यति॥ १०८॥
yastāratamyavettā syād gurūṇāṃ devatāsvapi|
bhaktimān gurudeveṣu tadbhakteṣu ca siddhyati||
108||
Exact combined meaningHaving remembered the guru, the preceding guru, and the primal root-gurus, and the deity, Vāsudeva — the practicer of the mantra attains success. But a despiser of the gurus and of the deities does not succeed, and goes to terrible darkness — and so does one who slights the mantra. He who knows the gradation (tāratamya) among the gurus and among the deities, being devoted to the gurus, the gods, and their devotees, succeeds.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Chapter 4 · verses 109–110
4.109
गुरुदेवप्रतीपानां विमुखस्तत्प्रिये रतः।
अकामक्रोधलोभश्च नचिरात् सिद्धिमाप्नुयात्॥ १०९॥
gurudevapratīpānāṃ vimukhastatpriye rataḥ|
akāmakrodhalobhaśca nacirāt siddhimāpnuyāt||
109||
4.110
सकामेभ्योऽमितगुणा याऽकामैस्तु कृता क्रिया।
तस्मादकाम एवालं भजेद् विष्णुं सनातनम्॥ ११०॥
sakāmebhyo’mitaguṇā yā’kāmaistu kṛtā kriyā|
tasmādakāma evālaṃ bhajed viṣṇuṃ sanātanam||
110||
Exact combined meaningAverse to those hostile to guru and god, delighting in what is dear to them, free of desire, anger and greed — he would attain success before long. A rite performed by the desireless has qualities infinitely greater than (one performed) by those with desires; therefore, desireless indeed, one should abundantly worship the eternal Viṣṇu.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"Smṛtvā": the guru — the teacher of the mantra — the preceding gurus, the highest gurus and the rest; the primal guru is Pūrṇaprajña (Madhva); the root-guru is Vedavyāsa; and having remembered the deity named Vāsudeva, the practicer of the mantra, the reciter, attains success. When the japa of a given mantra is performed, then at the end the salutation to that deity is to be made — so say the knowers of the tradition. (110–114)
Chapter 4 · verse 111
4.111
अयं मे हस्त इति च मन्त्रः पूर्वोक्तवत् स्मृतः।
अत्रोक्तास्तु क्रियाः सर्वमन्त्रेष्वप्युपलक्षणम्॥ १११॥
ayaṃ me hasta iti ca mantraḥ pūrvoktavat smṛtaḥ|
atroktāstu kriyāḥ sarvamantreṣvapyupalakṣaṇam||
111||
Exact English meaningAnd the mantra 'ayaṁ me hastaḥ' is recorded as being like the aforesaid; and the rites stated here are indicative for the preceding mantras too.
Word and compound explanation
"Ayaṁ me hastaḥ" is a Vedic mantra; it is recorded as being just like the aforesaid — like the Dhanvantari-mantra. The homa and other rites stated here for the Dhanvantari-mantra are to be understood for the preceding mantras too. (115)

सर्वमन्त्रसाधारणविषयाः

Chapter 4 · verses 112–115
4.112
विजयस्तम्भनादीनि नारसिंहैर्विशेषतः।
जामदग्न्यैश्च कार्याणि राघवैर्यादवैरपि।
वाराहैः स्तम्भनं क्षिप्रं श्रीकराः कथिताः पुरा॥ ११२॥
vijayastambhanādīni nārasiṃhairviśeṣataḥ|
jāmadagnyaiśca kāryāṇi rāghavairyādavairapi|
vārāhaiḥ stambhanaṃ kṣipraṃ śrīkarāḥ kathitāḥ purā||
112||
4.113
बहुबाहुं महाकायमुद्युक्तं चोद्यतायुधम्।
स्मरन् विजयमाप्नोति स्तम्भनादींश्च कारयेत्॥ ११३॥
bahubāhuṃ mahākāyamudyuktaṃ codyatāyudham|
smaran vijayamāpnoti stambhanādīṃśca kārayet||
113||
4.114
वसुपूर्णकरं रत्नपर्वतस्थं विभावयन्।
वसुसिद्धिमवाप्नोति सेव्यमानमजादिभिः।
श्रिया विभूतिभिर्युक्तं भावयन् भूतिमाप्नुयात्॥ ११४॥
vasupūrṇakaraṃ ratnaparvatasthaṃ vibhāvayan|
vasusiddhimavāpnoti sevyamānamajādibhiḥ|
śriyā vibhūtibhiryuktaṃ bhāvayan bhūtimāpnuyāt||
114||
4.115
यद्यन्मनोहरं तस्य तत्तदाप्नोत्यसंशयम्।
तथातथा हरिं ध्यायन् क्रियास्तास्ताश्च साधयन्॥ ११५॥
yadyanmanoharaṃ tasya tattadāpnotyasaṃśayam|
tathātathā hariṃ dhyāyan kriyāstāstāśca sādhayan||
115||
Exact combined meaningVictory, immobilizing (of enemies) and the rest are to be effected especially by the Nārasiṁha (mantras), and by the Jāmadagnya, the Rāghava, and the Yādava (mantras); by the Vārāha (mantras), immobilizing is swift; the Śrīkara (mantras) were declared before. Remembering (Hari as) many-armed, huge-bodied, energetic and with raised weapons, one attains victory, and can effect immobilizing and the rest. Contemplating (Him as) with hands full of wealth, seated on a jewel-mountain, one attains success in wealth; contemplating Him served by the Unborn (Brahmā) and the rest, endowed with Śrī and the (other) glories, one would attain glory. Whatever is in his mind, that he attains without doubt, meditating on Hari in each such manner and accomplishing the several rites.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the particular mantras for particular applications: "vijayastambhanādīni." (116–119)
Chapter 4 · verses 116–117
4.116
श्रिये पद्मानि जुहुयात् सम्प्रीत्या उत्पलानि च।
शरान् जयाय जुहुयादभिमन्त्र्य प्रयोजयेत्॥ ११६॥
śriye padmāni juhuyāt samprītyā utpalāni ca|
śarān jayāya juhuyādabhimantrya prayojayet||
116||
4.117
विद्यायै मन्त्रितं वारि वचां वा तुलसीमपि।
ब्राह्मीं घृष्टसुवर्णं वा शतावृत्त्या तु नित्यशः॥ ११७॥
vidyāyai mantritaṃ vāri vacāṃ vā tulasīmapi|
brāhmīṃ ghṛṣṭasuvarṇaṃ vā śatāvṛttyā tu nityaśaḥ||
117||
Exact combined meaningFor prosperity (Śrī) one should offer lotuses, and, with love, blue lotuses; for victory one should offer arrows, and, having consecrated them with the mantra, employ them. For learning, water consecrated with the mantra, or vacā (a herb), or tulasī, or brāhmī, or ghee, or gold — daily, with a hundred repetitions.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the application of the homa: "śriye" — blue lotuses. "For victory" — for the sake of victory, arrows — fuel-sticks of the reed-grass from which arrows are made; having consecrated the arrows with the mantra, one should employ them against the enemy. (120–121)
Chapter 4 · verse 118
4.118
अकुर्वंश्च क्रिया भक्त्या निरपेक्षो भजन् हरिं।
सर्वमेतदवाप्नोति विशेषेण न संशयः॥ ११८॥
akurvaṃśca kriyā bhaktyā nirapekṣo bhajan hariṃ|
sarvametadavāpnoti viśeṣeṇa na saṃśayaḥ||
118||
Exact English meaningAnd one who, not performing the (special) rites, worships Hari with devotion, without expectation, attains all this in special measure — no doubt.
Word and compound explanation
Not performing the rites, the homa and the rest, worshipping Hari without expectation, he attains all. (122)
Chapter 4 · verse 119
4.119
सृष्टिस्थितिलयन्यासाः प्रातिलोम्यानुलोम्यतः।
स्थित्यन्ता एव वर्णानां श्रेष्ठा एवं चतुर्दश।
संहृत्यन्ता मोक्षिणां तु एकविंशतिरेव वा॥ ११९॥
sṛṣṭisthitilayanyāsāḥ prātilomyānulomyataḥ|
sthityantā eva varṇānāṃ śreṣṭhā evaṃ caturdaśa|
saṃhṛtyantā mokṣiṇāṃ tu ekaviṃśatireva vā||
119||
Exact English meaningThe creation, maintenance and dissolution nyāsas, in the reverse and the direct order: (those) of the letters ending with maintenance are the best, fourteen (repetitions); but for seekers of liberation, those ending with dissolution, twenty-one (repetitions).
Word and compound explanation
He states the varieties in the tattva-nyāsa and the rest: "sṛṣṭi." For the letters — for brāhmaṇas and the rest, those other than ascetics — the nyāsas ending with maintenance are the best; and they are to be done fourteen times. The nyāsas ending with dissolution belong to the seekers of liberation, the ascetics; and these are to be done twenty-one times — such is the meaning. (123)
Chapter 4 · verses 120–123
4.120
पादहृत्केषु नाभ्यास्ययुतं च सललाटकम्।
सनासिकादृग्जान्वोश्च सश्रोत्रं सोरुकं तथा॥ १२०॥
pādahṛtkeṣu nābhyāsyayutaṃ ca salalāṭakam|
sanāsikādṛgjānvośca saśrotraṃ sorukaṃ tathā||
120||
4.121
सोरस्कं च सकण्ठं च सभुजद्वयमेव च।
सगण्डयुग्मं सालीकं सतुण्डक्रोडकं तथा॥ १२१॥
soraskaṃ ca sakaṇṭhaṃ ca sabhujadvayameva ca|
sagaṇḍayugmaṃ sālīkaṃ satuṇḍakroḍakaṃ tathā||
121||
4.122
समूलं च सगुह्यं च सपार्श्वद्वयमेव च।
सदोर्द्वयं व्यापकं च गलपार्श्वद्वयेन च॥ १२२॥
samūlaṃ ca saguhyaṃ ca sapārśvadvayameva ca|
sadordvayaṃ vyāpakaṃ ca galapārśvadvayena ca||
122||
4.123
ककुदा स्तनद्वयेनापि पृष्ठमूलेन सर्वशः।
न्यासो यथाक्रमं प्रोक्तो मन्त्रेष्वेतेषु सर्वशः ।
प्रतिमायामात्मनि च न्यासोऽयं सन्निधानकृत्॥ १२३॥
kakudā stanadvayenāpi pṛṣṭhamūlena sarvaśaḥ|
nyāso yathākramaṃ prokto mantreṣveteṣu sarvaśaḥ |
pratimāyāmātmani ca nyāso’yaṃ sannidhānakṛt||
123||
Exact combined meaningOn the feet, heart and head, joined with the navel and the forehead; with the nose, the eyes, the knees, the ears, and the thighs; with the chest, the throat, the two arms, the two cheeks, the brow, the belly, and the breast; with the root (mūlādhāra), the genitals, the two flanks, the two arms, the pervading (nyāsa), and the two sides of the throat; and with the shoulder-hump and the two nipples, and the base of the back, entirely — the nyāsa in due order is declared, completely, for these mantras; and this nyāsa, (performed) on the image and on oneself, effects (the Lord's) presence.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the stations of the nyāsa for the mantras stated here: "pāda." The meaning is: in these mantras, in due order — following the order of the letters of the mantra — is the order of the stations. He states the fruit of the nyāsa: "pratimāyām." (124–127)
Chapter 4 · verses 124–125
4.124
बीजान्तःसाध्यमध्यं च विभक्तदलमन्त्रयुक्।
वर्णानुप्रतिलोमेन वृत्तं भूपुरसंयुतम्।
बीजसन्दृब्धकोणं च यन्त्रं रक्षादिसाधकम्॥ १२४॥
bījāntaḥsādhyamadhyaṃ ca vibhaktadalamantrayuk|
varṇānupratilomena vṛttaṃ bhūpurasaṃyutam|
bījasandṛbdhakoṇaṃ ca yantraṃ rakṣādisādhakam||
124||
4.125
समं तत् सर्वमन्त्रेषु जप्तं धार्यं तु वा भुवि।
विलिख्य मण्डले वा तत् पूजयेद्धरिमञ्जसा॥ १२५॥
samaṃ tat sarvamantreṣu japtaṃ dhāryaṃ tu vā bhuvi|
vilikhya maṇḍale vā tat pūjayeddharimañjasā||
125||
Exact combined meaningWith the bīja within, the sādhya (object) in the middle, joined with the mantra on the divided petals, with the letters (arranged) in reverse order, round, furnished with the earth-square (bhūpura), and with the corners bound with bījas — this yantra is the accomplisher of protection and the rest. That is common to all the mantras; (the yantra,) recited over, is to be worn, or drawn on the ground; or, having drawn it on a maṇḍala, one should readily worship Hari therein.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the manner of drawing the yantra: "bījāntam." This is the sense: having drawn a lotus with as many petals as the mantra has letters, having written the bīja in the middle of the lotus, one should write the sādhya (object) in the space around the bīja; on the petals one should write the letters of the mantra; on the outside, having drawn a four-cornered bhūpura, and having written the bījas of the world-guardians in the corners of the bhūpura, one should worship Hari there. (128–129)

विद्यागुरुः

Chapter 4 · verses 126–130
4.126
प्रतिमोक्तलक्षणैर्यावद्युक्त आचार्य उत्तमः।
सर्ववित् तपसा युक्त आज्ञाशक्तियुतो हरेः॥ १२६॥
pratimoktalakṣaṇairyāvadyukta ācārya uttamaḥ|
sarvavit tapasā yukta ājñāśaktiyuto hareḥ||
126||
4.127
यावद्भक्तो यथालब्धैर्गुणैर्युक्तो हि वैष्णवः।
गुरुः सत्सम्प्रदायज्ञस्तारतम्येन सिद्धिदः॥ १२७॥
yāvadbhakto yathālabdhairguṇairyukto hi vaiṣṇavaḥ|
guruḥ satsampradāyajñastāratamyena siddhidaḥ||
127||
4.128
उत्तमादुत्तमा सिद्धिर्धर्ममोक्षादिषु स्फुटा।
तस्मादुत्तम आचार्ये लब्धे नातोऽवरं व्रजेत्॥ १२८॥
uttamāduttamā siddhirdharmamokṣādiṣu sphuṭā|
tasmāduttama ācārye labdhe nāto’varaṃ vrajet||
128||
4.129
एकस्य शिष्यतां प्राप्य तदाज्ञां न विना समम्।
अवरं वा व्रजेदुच्चगुणश्चेन्नैव दुष्यति॥ १२९॥
ekasya śiṣyatāṃ prāpya tadājñāṃ na vinā samam|
avaraṃ vā vrajeduccaguṇaścennaiva duṣyati||
129||
4.130
विशेषतो गुणश्च स्याद् देवेष्वप्येवमेव हि।
तस्मादुत्तममाचार्यं देवेशं चाश्रयेद्धरिं॥ १३०॥
viśeṣato guṇaśca syād deveṣvapyevameva hi|
tasmāduttamamācāryaṃ deveśaṃ cāśrayeddhariṃ||
130||
Exact combined meaningAn excellent teacher (ācārya) is one furnished with the marks stated for the image, all-knowing, endowed with austerity, joined with attachment to the commands of Hari; devoted (to Him) to the utmost, a Vaiṣṇava endowed with such qualities as are available, a guru who knows the true tradition, a bestower of success according to (his) gradation. From the best (teacher) the best success in dharma, liberation and the rest is manifest; therefore, an excellent teacher being obtained, one should not go to a lower one than this. Having become the disciple of one, one should not, without his leave, go to an equal or a lower one — (though) if (the other) be of higher qualities, one does not thereby err. And there should be, in particular, quality (in the teacher); just so indeed among the deities (one should discriminate). Therefore one should resort to an excellent teacher and to Hari, the Lord of the gods.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the characteristics of the guru: "pratimoktalakṣaṇaiḥ." (130–134)

योगोपासनादि

Chapter 4 · verse 131
4.131
अपरोक्षदृशेरेव यस्मान्मोक्षो नचान्यथा।
अपरोक्षदृशिश्चापि श्रवणान्मननादनु।
सम्यङ्निश्चिततत्त्वस्य निदिध्यासनया भवेत्॥ १३१॥
aparokṣadṛśereva yasmānmokṣo nacānyathā|
aparokṣadṛśiścāpi śravaṇānmananādanu|
samyaṅniścitatattvasya nididhyāsanayā bhavet||
131||
Exact English meaningSince liberation (comes) only from direct vision (aparokṣa-dṛś), and not otherwise; and direct vision arises, after hearing (śravaṇa) and reflection (manana), for one whose (truth) is rightly ascertained, through profound meditation (nididhyāsana).
Word and compound explanation
Now he states the secret of the supreme means: "aparokṣa." (135)
Chapter 4 · verse 132
4.132
द्विविधा सा च सम्प्रोक्ता विष्णुशास्त्रावमर्शनम्।
एकं ध्यानं हरेरन्यच्चत्वार्यङ्गानि तत्र च।
यमाश्च नियमाश्चासा प्राणायाम इतीरिताः॥ १३२॥
dvividhā sā ca samproktā viṣṇuśāstrāvamarśanam|
ekaṃ dhyānaṃ hareranyaccatvāryaṅgāni tatra ca|
yamāśca niyamāścāsā prāṇāyāma itīritāḥ||
132||
Exact English meaningAnd that (nididhyāsana) is declared to be twofold: one, the pondering of the Viṣṇu-śāstra; the other, the meditation on Hari; and therein there are four limbs — yama, niyama, āsana, and it is stated (also) prāṇāyāma.
Word and compound explanation
That nididhyāsana is twofold: the pondering of the Viṣṇu-śāstra, and meditation. Therein, in the meditation, there are four limbs — yama and the rest. (136)
Chapter 4 · verses 133–136
4.133
अहिंसा सत्यमस्तेयं ब्रह्मचर्यापरिग्रहौ।
यमाः शौचं तपस्तुष्टिः स्वाध्यायो हरिपूजनम्।
नियमा वीरपद्मे च स्वस्तिकाद्यानि चासनम्॥ १३३॥
ahiṃsā satyamasteyaṃ brahmacaryāparigrahau|
yamāḥ śaucaṃ tapastuṣṭiḥ svādhyāyo haripūjanam|
niyamā vīrapadme ca svastikādyāni cāsanam||
133||
4.134
रेचकः पूरकश्चैव कुम्भकश्च त्रिधा स्मृतः।
प्राणायामः स्मृतिं विष्णोर्विना नियम उच्यते॥ १३४॥
recakaḥ pūrakaścaiva kumbhakaśca tridhā smṛtaḥ|
prāṇāyāmaḥ smṛtiṃ viṣṇorvinā niyama ucyate||
134||
4.135
अधीतिः स्मृतियुक्ता तु निदिध्यासनमेव हि।
प्राणायामे च विष्णोर्यत् स्मरणं ध्यानमेव तत्॥ १३५॥
adhītiḥ smṛtiyuktā tu nididhyāsanameva hi|
prāṇāyāme ca viṣṇoryat smaraṇaṃ dhyānameva tat||
135||
4.136
आसा शौचं प्राणयामो निष्परिग्रहपूर्वकाः।
तपआद्याः क्रमात् सर्व उत्तरोत्तरतोऽधिकाः॥ १३६॥
āsā śaucaṃ prāṇayāmo niṣparigrahapūrvakāḥ|
tapaādyāḥ kramāt sarva uttarottarato’dhikāḥ||
136||
Exact combined meaningNon-injury, truthfulness, non-stealing, celibacy and non-possession (aparigraha) — (these are) the yamas; purity, austerity, contentment, self-study (svādhyāya), and the worship of Hari (are) the niyamas; the hero-posture, the lotus-posture, the svastika and the rest are the āsana. Recaka, pūraka and kumbhaka — (prāṇāyāma) is recorded as threefold; prāṇāyāma without the remembrance of Viṣṇu is (merely) called niyama. But study (adhīti) joined with remembrance (of Viṣṇu) is verily nididhyāsana; and in prāṇāyāma too, whatever remembrance there is of Viṣṇu — that is meditation. Āsana, purity, prāṇāyāma, preceded by non-possession, austerity and the rest — all, in order, are greater one after the other.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the characteristics of yama and the rest: "ahiṁsā." He states the āsanas: "vīra." "Svastika and the rest": by the word "and the rest" the peacock-posture, the garuḍa-posture and others are included. (137–140)
Chapter 4 · verses 137–138
4.137
सर्वस्माच्छ्रवणाद्यास्तु मुख्या मुक्तिप्रसाधने।
अज्ञस्य श्रवणं श्रेष्ठमयुक्तेर्मननं तथा॥ १३७॥
sarvasmācchravaṇādyāstu mukhyā muktiprasādhane|
ajñasya śravaṇaṃ śreṣṭhamayuktermananaṃ tathā||
137||
4.138
ध्यानं निश्चिततत्त्वस्य तस्माच्छास्त्रावमर्शनम्।
वरं दशगुणं तस्माद् व्याख्यैकस्य शतोत्तरा।
अपरोक्षदृशाऽप्येषा कर्तव्या विष्णुतुष्टिदा॥ १३८॥
dhyānaṃ niścitatattvasya tasmācchāstrāvamarśanam|
varaṃ daśaguṇaṃ tasmād vyākhyaikasya śatottarā|
aparokṣadṛśā’pyeṣā kartavyā viṣṇutuṣṭidā||
138||
Exact combined meaningBut of all, hearing (śravaṇa) and the rest are the chief in the accomplishment of liberation; for the ignorant, hearing is best; for one who has not (reasoned), reflection likewise; meditation for one whose truth is (already) ascertained. Therefore, the pondering of the śāstra is ten times better than that; and the exposition (vyākhyā) of a single one is a hundred times (better) than that. Even by one of direct vision (aparokṣa-dṛś) this is to be done, being a giver of Viṣṇu's pleasure.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states the gradation in the means: in the accomplishment of liberation — in the matter of the means to liberation — hearing, reflection and profound meditation are the chief means. He states the difference of eligibility with regard to hearing and the rest: "ajñasya." For one incapable of exposition, lacking certainty. "Than that" — than meditation, the pondering of the śāstra — the investigation of the Viṣṇu-śāstra — is ten times greater. And greater than that by a hundredfold is the exposition — the explaining — of even a single quality of the Lord to a single worthy disciple. Even by the knower it is to be done, solely for the pleasure of Viṣṇu. (141–142)
Chapter 4 · verses 139–140
4.139
“य इमं परमं गुह्यं मद्भक्तेष्वभिधास्यति।
भक्तिं मयि परां कृत्वा मामेवैष्यत्यसंशयः॥ १३९॥
“ya imaṃ paramaṃ guhyaṃ madbhakteṣvabhidhāsyati|
bhaktiṃ mayi parāṃ kṛtvā māmevaiṣyatyasaṃśayaḥ||
139||
4.140
नच तस्मान्मनुष्येषु कश्चिन्मे प्रियकृत्तमः।
भविता न च तस्मादन्यः प्रियतरो भुवि॥ १४०॥
naca tasmānmanuṣyeṣu kaścinme priyakṛttamaḥ|
bhavitā na ca tasmādanyaḥ priyataro bhuvi||
140||
Exact combined meaning(As the Lord says:) He who will declare this supreme secret among My devotees, having made supreme devotion to Me, will come to Me alone, without doubt. And there is none among men who does more dear a service to Me than he; nor shall there be another dearer to Me than he on earth.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
He states, by a saying of the Gītā, the special fruit of declaring the secret of one's own means to the devotees of Hari: "ya imam." (143–144)
Chapter 4 · verses 141–142
4.141
” शौचासने त्वङ्गमात्रं न पृथग् धर्मकारणम्।
प्राणायामादयस्तुष्टिपर्यन्ता द्विगुणाधिकाः॥ १४१॥
” śaucāsane tvaṅgamātraṃ na pṛthag dharmakāraṇam|
prāṇāyāmādayastuṣṭiparyantā dviguṇādhikāḥ||
141||
4.142
स्वाध्यायोऽतः कोटिगुणस्ततोऽनन्तगुणा हरेः।
पूजा तत्त्रिगुणं ध्यानमिति धर्मक्रमः स्मृतः॥ १४२॥
svādhyāyo’taḥ koṭiguṇastato’nantaguṇā hareḥ|
pūjā tattriguṇaṃ dhyānamiti dharmakramaḥ smṛtaḥ||
142||
Exact combined meaningPurity and posture are merely a limb, not a separate cause of merit; prāṇāyāma and the rest, up to contentment (tuṣṭi), are twice as great (each successively). Self-study is a crore times (greater) than this; than that, the worship of Hari is infinitely greater; than this, meditation is threefold — such is the recorded order of merit.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"Śauca": purity and posture are limbs of meditation, (serving) a perceptible purpose. "Prāṇāyāmādayaḥ": prāṇāyāma, non-injury, truth, non-stealing, celibacy, non-possession, austerity and contentment — in order, each twice as great. Self-study is a crore times greater than that — than contentment. Than that — than self-study — the worship of Hari is infinitely greater. Threefold of that is meditation — such is the order of merit. (145–146)
Chapter 4 · verses 143–145
4.143
ऊर्वोरधः पदोरूर्ध्वं विभागेनेति च त्रिधा।
आसनत्रयमुद्दिष्टं मन्त्रस्मरणपूर्वकम्॥ १४३॥
ūrvoradhaḥ padorūrdhvaṃ vibhāgeneti ca tridhā|
āsanatrayamuddiṣṭaṃ mantrasmaraṇapūrvakam||
143||
4.144
द्विगुणोत्तरं रेचकादि षोडशादिक्रमेण तु।
मात्राणां ह्रस्ववर्णस्य कालो मात्रेति शब्द्यते॥ १४४॥
dviguṇottaraṃ recakādi ṣoḍaśādikrameṇa tu|
mātrāṇāṃ hrasvavarṇasya kālo mātreti śabdyate||
144||
4.145
क्रमेणैवाभ्यसेन्मात्रावृद्धिमेकाग्रधीः पुनः।
रेचयित्वा दक्षिणतः पूरयित्वा तु वामतः।
कुम्भकं च सुषुम्नायां विष्णुं वायुं च संस्मरेत्॥ १४५॥
krameṇaivābhyasenmātrāvṛddhimekāgradhīḥ punaḥ|
recayitvā dakṣiṇataḥ pūrayitvā tu vāmataḥ|
kumbhakaṃ ca suṣumnāyāṃ viṣṇuṃ vāyuṃ ca saṃsmaret||
145||
Exact combined meaningBelow the thighs, above the feet, and by (their) division — thus a triad of postures is declared, preceded by the remembrance of the mantra. Recaka and the rest, twice-greater in succession, in the order beginning with sixteen; the time-measure (mātrā) of a short letter is what is called a 'mātrā.' The one-pointed-minded should practice, in order, an increase of the mātrā again; having exhaled through the right, having inhaled through the left, and (performing) kumbhaka in the suṣumnā, one should remember Viṣṇu and Vāyu.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
Now he states the manner of the postures: "ūrvoḥ." Placing the feet below the thighs — this is one posture. Placing the feet above the thighs — the second. By division, placing one foot below the thigh and the other above the other thigh — the third; such is the triad of postures. He states the procedure of prāṇāyāma: "smaraṇapūrvakam." This is the sense: a mātrā is the time of pronouncing a short (letter). One should make the recaka of sixteen mātrās' time; the pūraka of thirty-two mātrās' time; the kumbhaka of twice that, sixty-four mātrās' time. At the time of prāṇāyāma one should remember Vāyu, abiding in the suṣumnā channel, and Viṣṇu. (147–149)
Chapter 4 · verses 146–147
4.146
मूलाधारं समारभ्य त्वामूर्धानमृजुस्थिता ।
मध्ये सुषुम्ना विज्ञेया वज्रिकाऽऽर्या प्रकाशिनी।
वैद्युता ब्रह्मनाडीति सैव पञ्चप्रभेदिनी॥ १४६॥
mūlādhāraṃ samārabhya tvāmūrdhānamṛjusthitā |
madhye suṣumnā vijñeyā vajrikā’’ryā prakāśinī|
vaidyutā brahmanāḍīti saiva pañcaprabhedinī||
146||
4.147
पृष्ठवामाग्रदक्षान्तर्भेदास्ते च क्रमेण तु।
हरिन्नीलः सितः पिङ्गो लोहितश्चात्र केशवः।
प्रद्युम्नादिस्वरूपेण ध्येयः सिद्धिमभीप्सता॥ १४७॥
pṛṣṭhavāmāgradakṣāntarbhedāste ca krameṇa tu|
harinnīlaḥ sitaḥ piṅgo lohitaścātra keśavaḥ|
pradyumnādisvarūpeṇa dhyeyaḥ siddhimabhīpsatā||
147||
Exact combined meaningBeginning from the mūlādhāra up to the crown, standing straight, in the middle is to be known the suṣumnā, the brahma-channel — (with its parts) Vajrikā, Āryā, Prakāśinī and Vaidyutā — that same being fivefold in division. And they, in order, are the differentiations at the back, the left, the front and the right; green, white, tawny, and red — herein Keśava, (Vāsudeva, etc.), in the forms of Pradyumna and the rest, is to be meditated by one desiring success.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
Wishing to state the meditation, he speaks of the suṣumnā channel and the forms of Viṣṇu abiding in it: "mūlādhāram." From the mūlādhāra up to the head, the straight — uncurved — channel called suṣumnā is to be known, in the middle of the heart. That same is the brahma-channel. At its back part is the Vajrikā; at the left, the Āryā; at the front, the Prakāśinī; at the right, the Vaidyutā. Abiding in these five, Hari, of colors beginning with green, is to be meditated. In the middle, in the suṣumnā, Nārāyaṇa, red-hued, is to be meditated. Behind it, in the Vajrikā, Pradyumna, green-hued, is to be meditated. In the Āryā situated to its left, Aniruddha, blue-hued, is to be meditated. In the Prakāśinī situated in front of it, Vāsudeva, white-hued, is to be meditated. In the Vaidyutā situated to its right, Saṅkarṣaṇa, tawny-hued, is to be meditated. (150–151)
Chapter 4 · verse 148
4.148
मूले च नाभौ हृदयेन्द्रयोनिभ्रूमध्यमूर्धद्विषडन्तकेषु।
चतुःषडष्टद्विचतुर्द्विषट्कदलेषु पद्मेषु सितारुणेषु।
पञ्चात्मकोऽसौ भगवान् सदैव ध्येयो हृदन्तान्यरुणानि तानि॥ १४८॥
mūle ca nābhau hṛdayendrayonibhrūmadhyamūrdhadviṣaḍantakeṣu|
catuḥṣaḍaṣṭadvicaturdviṣaṭkadaleṣu padmeṣu sitāruṇeṣu|
pañcātmako’sau bhagavān sadaiva dhyeyo hṛdantānyaruṇāni tāni||
148||
Exact English meaningOn the root (mūlādhāra), the navel, the heart, the base of the senses (genitals), the space between the brows, and the crown — the 'twelve-plus-two-plus-six'-ending (i.e. the various lotuses) — on the lotuses of four, six, eight, two, four, and two-times-six petals, white and ruddy — that fivefold Lord is ever to be meditated; those (lotuses) up to the heart are ruddy.
Word and compound explanation
He thus states the stations of meditation of the fivefold Lord: "mūle ca." "In the root" — in the mūlādhāra. "Indrayoni" — the palate region, according to the śruti "sendrayoni," etc. "Dviṣaḍanta" — the region twelve aṅgulas above the head. On these lotuses of four petals and so on, white and ruddy in color, the fivefold Lord is ever to be meditated. The lotuses at the root, navel and heart are ruddy; those at the palate, between the brows, and the crown are white. Here the sense is this: at the mūlādhāra a four-petalled lotus; at the navel a six-petalled; at the heart an eight-petalled — these are ruddy. At the palate region an eight-petalled (sic; the verse gives two); between the brows a two-petalled; at the crown a six-petalled. One should meditate the fivefold Viṣṇu abiding in those lotuses. (152)
Chapter 4 · verse 149
4.149
त्रिकोणवह्नौ च षडस्रवायौ द्विस्तावदस्ररविवृत्तशशित्रिवह्नौ।
वृत्ते विधावपि विचिन्त्यमिदं दशार्धरूपं सितं तदखिलं हृदयात् परस्थम्॥ १४९॥
trikoṇavahnau ca ṣaḍasravāyau dvistāvadasraravivṛttaśaśitrivahnau|
vṛtte vidhāvapi vicintyamidaṃ daśārdharūpaṃ sitaṃ tadakhilaṃ hṛdayāt parastham||
149||
Exact English meaningIn the triangular fire-(maṇḍala), and in the six-cornered air-(maṇḍala), and in the fire seated on the moon set within a circle of twice-that (twelve) corners, and in the round moon-(maṇḍala) too — this fivefold form, white, is to be contemplated, all of it stationed beyond the heart.
Word and compound explanation
He states the maṇḍalas that serve as the supports of the several lotuses: "trikoṇa." At the mūlādhāra stands the triangular fire-maṇḍala; at the navel region, the six-cornered air-maṇḍala; at the heart, the twice-that, twelve-cornered, sun-maṇḍala; between the brows, the triangular fire-maṇḍala. In these maṇḍalas and lotuses the fivefold Lord is to be meditated. (153)
Chapter 4 · verse 150
4.150
इडा च पिङ्गला चैव वज्रिका धारिणी तथा।
सुषुम्नायाश्चतुर्दिक्षु वज्रिकाद्यासु तत्र च।
चतुर्मूर्तिः स भगवान् हृदये संव्यवस्थितः॥ १५०॥
iḍā ca piṅgalā caiva vajrikā dhāriṇī tathā|
suṣumnāyāścaturdikṣu vajrikādyāsu tatra ca|
caturmūrtiḥ sa bhagavān hṛdaye saṃvyavasthitaḥ||
150||
Exact English meaningIḍā and Piṅgalā, and Vajrikā and Dhāriṇī likewise, in the four directions of the suṣumnā; and there, in the Vajrikā and the rest, that Lord, four-formed, is established in the heart.
Word and compound explanation
He states other channels: "iḍā ca." In the four (channels), Iḍā and the rest, and in the aforesaid Vajrikās, the four-formed Lord, in the forms of Vāsudeva and the rest, abiding in the heart, is to be meditated. (154)
Chapter 4 · verses 151–152
4.151
द्वासप्ततिसहस्राणि नाड्योऽन्यास्तु प्रधानतः।
बृहतीसहस्रस्पर्शानामृचो वाच्योऽत्र केशवः॥ १५१॥
dvāsaptatisahasrāṇi nāḍyo’nyāstu pradhānataḥ|
bṛhatīsahasrasparśānāmṛco vācyo’tra keśavaḥ||
151||
4.152
रूपाणां लक्षणं तेषां रहस्यत्वान्न कथ्यते।
विष्णुना ब्रह्मणे प्रोक्तं ग्रन्थसङ्कोचतस्तथा॥ १५२॥
rūpāṇāṃ lakṣaṇaṃ teṣāṃ rahasyatvānna kathyate|
viṣṇunā brahmaṇe proktaṃ granthasaṅkocatastathā||
152||
Exact combined meaningBut other channels, seventy-two thousand principally; herein Keśava is named by the ṛcs of the stops (sparśas) of the thousand bṛhatī (metres). The description of their forms is not stated, because of its secrecy — (though) it was declared by Viṣṇu to Brahmā — and likewise on account of the abridgment of the book.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
The seventy-two (thousand) channels are principal; the others, Vajrikā and the rest, are subsidiary — such is the sense. He states what is to be meditated in these: "bṛhatī." The bṛhatī is a nine-syllable pāda, the bṛhatī metre; and by the "sparśas" — the stop-letters of the ṛcs, those of the five classes from ka onward — and by those ṛcs, Keśava, of manifold forms, is named and to be meditated. (155–156)
Chapter 4 · verses 153–154
4.153
खण्डस्मृतिर्धारणा स्यादखण्डा ध्यानमुच्यते।
अप्रयत्नात् समाधिश्च दर्शनं चिरया तया।
अथवा सततं शास्त्रविमर्शेन भविष्यति॥ १५३॥
khaṇḍasmṛtirdhāraṇā syādakhaṇḍā dhyānamucyate|
aprayatnāt samādhiśca darśanaṃ cirayā tayā|
athavā satataṃ śāstravimarśena bhaviṣyati||
153||
4.154
परमस्नेहसंयुक्तयथार्थज्ञानतो भवेत्।
सा भक्तिरिति विज्ञेया साधनं भोगमोक्षयोः॥ १५४॥
paramasnehasaṃyuktayathārthajñānato bhavet|
sā bhaktiriti vijñeyā sādhanaṃ bhogamokṣayoḥ||
154||
Exact combined meaningBroken (piecemeal) remembrance is dhāraṇā; unbroken (continuous remembrance) is called dhyāna; and, effortlessly, samādhi; and, by that prolonged (samādhi), the (direct) vision. Or else it will come about by constant pondering of the śāstra. That which arises from true knowledge joined with supreme love — that is to be known as bhakti, the means to both enjoyment and liberation.

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"Khaṇḍasmṛti": remembrance of one limb at a time is broken remembrance; it is called dhāraṇā. Unbroken — remembrance of all the limbs together — is samādhi, called dhyāna. "Aprayatnā" — the standing (in remembrance) of all the limbs without effort. By remembrance in the form of samādhi, practiced for a long time, comes the vision of Hari — the direct knowledge (aparokṣa-jñāna), such is the sense. He states another alternative: "athavā." Or by constant pondering of the śāstra — by unceasing investigation of the meaning of the śāstra — the vision, the direct knowledge, of Viṣṇu will come about. He states the nature of bhakti: "parama." (157–158)
Chapter 4 · verse 155
4.155
मुख्यमेषैव नान्यत् स्यात् सर्वं व्यर्थं तया विना।
सैकाऽप्यभीष्टफलदा मोक्षो दर्शनयुक्तया॥ १५५॥
mukhyameṣaiva nānyat syāt sarvaṃ vyarthaṃ tayā vinā|
saikā’pyabhīṣṭaphaladā mokṣo darśanayuktayā||
155||
Exact English meaningThis (bhakti) alone is the chief; nothing else is; everything is vain without it. It alone gives the desired fruit; liberation (comes) by (bhakti) joined with (direct) vision.
Word and compound explanation
It alone is independent of any other means. As in the Gītā: "But by undivided devotion, O Arjuna, I of such kind can be known and truly seen and entered into, O scorcher of foes." "Darśana" is (direct) knowledge. (159)
Chapter 4 · verse 156
4.156
दर्शनं च तया हीनं व्यर्थमन्यत् किमुच्यते।
योग्यं तु दर्शनं नैव तया हीनं भविष्यति॥ १५६॥
darśanaṃ ca tayā hīnaṃ vyarthamanyat kimucyate|
yogyaṃ tu darśanaṃ naiva tayā hīnaṃ bhaviṣyati||
156||
Exact English meaningAnd (even) vision, devoid of it (bhakti), is vain — what to say of other things? But fit (liberation-granting) vision will never come about devoid of it.
Word and compound explanation
Devoid of that — of bhakti — (it is) vain; what to say of other rites and the rest? Even fit vision — the direct knowledge that is the means to liberation — will not come about devoid of bhakti; it would not be — such is the meaning. (160)
Chapter 4 · verse 157
4.157
आनन्दचित्सदितिपूर्वगुणैः समस्तैर्मर्त्यादिभिर्विधिपरैर्भगवान् क्रमेण।
ध्यातोऽधिकैरधिगुणोऽधिकमेव सौख्यं मुक्तौ ददाति सततं परमस्वरूपः॥ १५७॥
ānandacitsaditipūrvaguṇaiḥ samastairmartyādibhirvidhiparairbhagavān krameṇa|
dhyāto’dhikairadhiguṇo’dhikameva saukhyaṃ muktau dadāti satataṃ paramasvarūpaḥ||
157||
Exact English meaningThe Lord, meditated upon in order — with all the qualities beginning with bliss, consciousness and being (sat) — by mortals and the others up to Vidhi (Brahmā), (each) greater (than the last), being of ever-greater qualities, gives in liberation ever greater happiness, being of supreme essential form.
Word and compound explanation
He states the gradation in meditation: "ānanda." Meditated upon with (ever-)greater, superior qualities. (161)

उपसंहारः

Chapter 4 · verses 158–159
4.158
निर्दोषाखिलसम्पूर्णगुणेन हरिणा स्वयम्।
ब्रह्मणे कथितात् तन्त्रसारादुद्धृत्य सादरम्।
आनन्दतीर्थमुनिना कृतो ग्रन्थोऽयमञ्जसा॥ १५८॥
nirdoṣākhilasampūrṇaguṇena hariṇā svayam|
brahmaṇe kathitāt tantrasārāduddhṛtya sādaram|
ānandatīrthamuninā kṛto grantho’yamañjasā||
158||
4.159
ग्रन्थोऽयं पाठमात्रेण सकलाभीष्टसिद्धिदः।
किमु ज्ञानादनुष्ठानादुभयस्मात् पुनः किमु॥ १५९॥
grantho’yaṃ pāṭhamātreṇa sakalābhīṣṭasiddhidaḥ|
kimu jñānādanuṣṭhānādubhayasmāt punaḥ kimu||
159||
Exact combined meaningFrom the Tantrasāra, declared by Hari Himself — flawless and complete in all attributes — to Brahmā, having reverently extracted (it), this book was readily composed by the sage Ānandatīrtha. This book, by mere recitation, grants the accomplishment of all that is desired; how much more from knowledge (of it), from practice (of it), and how much more again from both?

These verses form one translation unit because the commentary edition joins their Sanskrit syntax across different verse boundaries.

Word and compound explanation
"Grantha": by mere recitation, even of one lacking investigation of the meaning. "From both" — from knowledge and practice. (163)
Chapter 4 · verse 160
4.160
यस्य त्रीण्युदितानि वेदवचने रूपाणि दिव्यान्यलं बट् तद्दर्शतमित्थमेव निहितं देवस्य भर्गो महत्।
वायो रामवचोनयं प्रथमकं पृक्षो द्वितीयं वपु- र्मध्वो यत् तु तृतीयमेतदमुना ग्रन्थः कृतः केशवे॥ १६०॥
yasya trīṇyuditāni vedavacane rūpāṇi divyānyalaṃ baṭ taddarśatamitthameva nihitaṃ devasya bhargo mahat|
vāyo rāmavaconayaṃ prathamakaṃ pṛkṣo dvitīyaṃ vapu- rmadhvo yat tu tṛtīyametadamunā granthaḥ kṛtaḥ keśave||
160||
Exact English meaningHe whose three divine forms are amply proclaimed in the Vedic word — 'baṭ, tad darśatam' — thus indeed is fixed the great splendor (bhargas) of the God: of Vāyu, the first is the form (that spoke) Rāma's words (Hanumat); the second, the questioned body (Bhīma); and the third, which is Madhva's — by this (third) was this book made concerning Keśava.
Word and compound explanation
Indicating the authoritativeness of even his own book, as being rooted (in the Veda), to the devotees, he states his own essential nature as established by the śruti "bali(t)," etc.: "yasya." "Concerning Keśava" — the meaning is: expounding Him. (164)
Chapter 4 · verse 161
4.161
अशेषदोषोज्झितपूर्णसद्गुणं सदा विशेषापगतोरुरूपम् ।
नमामि नारायणमप्रतीपं सदा प्रियेभ्यः प्रियमादरेण॥ १६१॥
aśeṣadoṣojjhitapūrṇasadguṇaṃ sadā viśeṣāpagatorurūpam |
namāmi nārāyaṇamapratīpaṃ sadā priyebhyaḥ priyamādareṇa||
161||
Exact English meaningI bow with reverence to Nārāyaṇa — devoid of every fault, full of true good qualities, ever specially known in His manifold forms, without any opponent, ever dearer than the dear to His beloved ones.
Word and compound explanation
Teaching his disciples that a book should not be concluded merely by the statement of one's own nature, he performs an auspicious (closing) in the form of a salutation to his own unconditioned, cherished Lord: "aśeṣa." The construction is "with reverence I bow." "Viśeṣāpagatoru-rūpam": "uru-rūpa" means of many forms; and thus, by the viśeṣa (the particular), which produces the effect of difference within non-difference, He possesses many differentiations — such is the meaning. Or the compound may be resolved: He whose forms are, by the viśeṣa (as) gradation, free of (mutual) voidness. And thus the purport is that all His forms are equal — this is the wholly supreme auspiciousness. (165)
